The client's own far tiers by share of the screen are built and off (`holdShare` stops a small body's animation only while it stands still, `goneShare` takes a smaller one off the screen; both nought), a body in a fight is never drawn at a far tier, and a body this browser keeps for a server thinks at least every 0.4 s. `__debug.crowd()` lists every body's tier, share, walk and parts, `crowd({ compare })` holds a switch to the same picture, and the debug menu's `crowd` carries the feel numbers as sliders. Measured only in headless Chrome of our own on Direct3D 11 at 1600x900, before and after in turn in one session: on Bestine's square (the design's T12 and T13, already met as the wave began: its 23.4 ms and 4 to 6 were measured before wave 2) the frame's median with the people went from 6.5 and 6.6 ms to 5.9 and 5.8, and the people's own share of it from 1.4 and 1.5 ms to 1.0 and 0.8; on Mos Eisley's street from 9.3 to 8.1 and 8.3 ms, the people's share from 1.7 and 1.9 to 0.9 and 1.1; in the profile the walk went from 1.34 to 0.77 ms a frame and the motion blur's bookkeeping from 0.16 to 0.08. **Wave 6 took down the town's shadow pass.** The sun's near map is drawn every frame and its middle and far maps only once their pictures would be shown 13 and 41 ms old (at 144 Hz every 2nd and every 6th frame), each held exactly where it was drawn while it still covers what the camera sees, and drawn at once when it no longer does, when the sun moves, or when anything listed as fast (a vehicle, timed in the physics' own steps, a starport's shuttle in flight, another player's ride or figure) could cast into it; each loaded region tier's still placed casters reach the shadow maps as one draw; every plain caster's depth program, instanced or not, is built behind the first screen with the batches' own; and, the owner's answer to Q8, each shadow map's depth now reaches its own band (`farDepth` 'whole', the far one about 908 to 966 m by the window's shape), so the far shadows reach the ground across the sun that the old depth left bare, and the far map is held as often turning as standing. The leaf cards' crown skip takes each cascade's own depth range. `__debug.shadowPass()` says each cascade's age, limit, draws a second and why, the batches and the movers, its four feel numbers are sliders in the debug menu, and the perf switches `shadowCadence`, `shadowBatches`, `shadowBoth` and `farDepth` put each part back. Measured only in headless Chrome of our own on Direct3D 11 at 1600x900, people up, held noon (the sun 46 degrees up), heading 270, the ages set to give the 144 Hz schedule at the tab's own rate, no shuttle listed, the old way (every cascade every frame, no batches, the old depth) and what ships in turn in one session: the shadow pass's own time (its `shadows` and `shadowHooks` lines) on Mos Eisley's street went from 2.97 to 1.52 ms (0.51 of the old way, where the design asked 0.47) and its draws from 1,328 to 185, on Bestine's square from 2.68 to 1.46 ms (0.55, asked 0.62); turning at 30 and 90 degrees a second scaled to 144 Hz, Mos Eisley 2.25 to 1.43 ms (0.63) and 2.52 to 1.50 ms (0.60), Bestine 2.17 to 1.49 (0.69) and 2.20 to 1.51 (0.69), against the design's allowance of T16's share plus 0.08 (0.59 and 0.63), so turning is not met at either town, the far map drawn on one frame in six or seven throughout; on the graphics card at Mos Eisley 2.81 to 1.60 ms (0.57, asked 0.60), turning 2.38 to 1.60; the frame's median from 9.2 to 7.7 ms at Mos Eisley and 9.0 to 7.3 at Bestine; the far map's longer depth cost nothing measurable on the main thread standing and 0.05 ms on the graphics card, and turning at 90 degrees saved about 0.25 ms against the old depth with the same exact guard, whose far map was drawn about 100 times a second; Bestine's premium over the open desert in the shadow pass from 0.51 to 0.28 ms (the scene's walk beside it, 0.9 against 0.55 ms, is not the wave's); a two-minute run through Mos Eisley with its doors casting and on to the nearest camp built no depth program in play (it built none of any kind, though it covered only 270 m between the walls); every picture pair held to the fresh draw, the view still or stepped 4 degrees, with the old depth and the new, came out with no pixel over 8 levels at any view tried, the batches too, and from a hill 200 m south of Bestine looking across the sun the new depth shades 0.7% of the picture at noon and 1.6% at a 26-degree sun that the old depth left bare; a riderless speeder at 40 m/s 150 m across the view drew the far map as a mover every frame it was over 20 m/s and was held again under it, and one ridden at up to 36 m/s never drew the middle or far map as a mover. **Wave 4 gave many people one program key.** Every skinned material (people, creatures, the player, other players, a pod's hull) and every weapon off the rack is given at load whatever it lacks of one set of maps that changes no pixel -- a flat normal map, a white roughness and metalness map, the world's environment at nought -- so their colour programs come in 24 shapes of our own, and every loading screen compiles all of them behind it before anybody is stood; a person stood outdoors in play whose every material is on those programs has its rooms pass found on the queue at the urgent rank and is ready for a doorway's room a frame or two after it shows, where it waited half a second. `__debug.personKeys()` lists the shapes, what was warmed, everything outside them by name and why, and what was built in play; `personKeys({ selfTest: true })` draws each test look with the maps off and on; `localStorage['swg.personKeys'] = '0'` leaves it out at load. Outside the shapes, and built off the frame when met as before: the blended eye films of the Trandoshans and the Mon Calamari, the visors and goggle lenses, glow maps, the unlit droids and the Jawa's eye, and the untextured placeholders, nearly all of them the skin a garment shows (352 of the 6,810 skinned and rack primitives the packs name, 5%; 64 shapes as converted, 21 once given the maps). Measured only in headless Chrome of our own at 1600x900, against the commit before the wave in turn in one session: a fresh profile's first arrival at Mos Eisley took 28.0 and 25.1 s on Direct3D 11 where it took 37.3 and 41.1 s before (and 32.0 and 35.9 s at the sixth wave's own base), and 16.1 s on Vulkan against 16.8, the warm itself 5.4 and 2.6 s of it on Direct3D 11 and 0.6 s on Vulkan, overlapped with the loading of the buildings, and every person stood up as the screen lifted (54 and 53 of 54 and 53, where 22 and 6 of 53 were before, the rest still loading); a two-minute walk across the town after that arrival built no colour program for anybody (and none at all, against 12 people's programs and a worst frame of 1.5 s before; worst 121 ms); and each test look -- six species, the Trandoshan's and the Mon Calamari's eye films among them, an armoured Imperial, a robed Jedi, a Jawa and a bantha -- drawn with the maps off and on differed by nothing in the world and by at most one level in the doll's and the select screen's own contexts (bound two, mean bound 0.2). **Wave 5 took away the requests that could only miss, the sky's allocations and the boot's one mystery.** Nothing under `assets-private/<id>/` is asked for a world that declares no pack (`PlanetDef.pack` false: the four real-elevation worlds, which still read their own arrival, about nought); a file the converter writes only for some kinds of world is asked only where its own rule for writing it would have written it (`packHolds` in `src/world/packFiles.ts`: no places, sound places, walk grid, measured sky or map picture for a space zone or the gallery, no map for the copies' world, no `spawns.json` but on the server's ten worlds and its copies, no gates but between a planet's zones, and from the pack's own manifest no floors or doors for a pack with no portal building); a pack's manifest is the one probe the rest wait on; a world's `sounds.json` is one request for its three readers; a file that answered 404 is not asked again that session (a missing space pack among them, which the galaxy tab asked for at every showing, and whose note now says so), while a page handed back or a failed request is asked again; a far world's map picture and pads (the galaxy tab, a travel terminal, a ticket) are asked with no probe of its manifest, which runs to megabytes; a loading picture is asked only where the `loading` command's own index lists one, and the stand-in is drawn on two canvases where the pictures stand, never encoded with `toDataURL`; and a built game asks for a world's tracks, its permanent placements and a real-elevation raster only where its own list of what it ships holds them (`virtual:shipped-public`, git's tracked files at build time), while the dev server hands no list and asks for every one, so the editors' first saves stand at once. The player's own story's index is still asked at every boot, since the launcher serves a folder the player names at run time there, and a launcher with no folder named answers it missing: the one ask of a boot that can miss. Our own sky is captured into one kept 128 px cube and filtered into one kept target in place, through a generator of its own, so a capture makes no target and hands no material a new environment (`reflections({ dome: 'scene' })` is the way before, a target a capture with `fromScene`'s 0.04 rad blur, which the kept capture has not got). A software rasteriser is said once on the message line, and on Vulkan the console says beside it that the start-up driver message "GPU stall due to ReadPixels" is the browser reading its own canvas back for software compositing, never the game. The 'game' reflection source no longer throws on a world that borrows its sky (it read the cube's files out of the world's own pack, which such a world has not got, cutting short the frame's update it was asked on). `__debug.files()` lists every ask, its answer and every ask answered without one. Measured in headless Chrome of our own on Direct3D 11, on a built game served by `vite preview` (which answers a missing file with the page and a 200, so page answers to a file's ask were counted as failures too) and on the dev server: a fresh boot to the select screen asked for 133 files and none failed, where 18 did (measured while the story's index was kept out of a build's boot, which the review found took the launcher's served story folder away; asked again at every boot, it is the one miss where no folder is there to serve); an arrival on Monument none, where 12 did; Kashyyyk's main zone, Mustafar and Tatooine's orbit none, where 5, 6 and 12 did; on the dev server only the placements and tracks files the disk answers missed, as they must; ten sky captures made no texture, framebuffer or renderbuffer (five the way before made five of each), the 'game' source between them two textures and a framebuffer for its own cube, the kept target stayed one, 512 high, and nothing was built in play; and the editor's save of a new world's placements file stood its row without a reload. The ground an arrival builds on the stand-in terrain before the converted one replaces it was measured, 175 ms of a 55 s cold Naboo screen, and left. **What the whole pass leaves.** Not built: a fighter's root never skips the walk, since it has no whole cull and is posed from outside the mobiles' folder; the materials outside the person shapes are still built in play, off the frame; a merged far look per body, the client's lower-detail meshes, one light set for every pass and a keeper of the shaders of trimmed looks are not this pass's; the launcher does not change the browser's renderer, since it cannot as an option (the owner's answer); 144 frames a second in every town is not promised; the streaming-at-speed pass is not built; and the Kashyyyk caves' zone still asks once a session for a map picture and gates it has not got, as does any install for a file a command it has not run would have written. Left for the Light and shadow pass, with what this pass did there: the near shadow map ends at a reach of its own (16 m, `shadowSplits.ts`), the middle and far maps are drawn by age and held where they were drawn, a region's still casters reach them as one draw, and the far map's depth reaches its whole band (the owner's "now"), so the far shadows reach the ground across the sun; what is left is the outer part of the far cascade's fade band, 320 to 360 m, which its box only partly reaches, the casters drawn from both sides or swaying (254 and 51 of Mos Eisley's), which still cast on their own, a fourth cascade, the cached map of what stands still and the light shafts across a planet, which the held cascades and the batches are what a far map drawn a few times a second would build on. Every number of the pass was measured in headless Chrome of our own, never on the owner's machine: his visible Chrome is the authority, through the design's console lines (§2.5). Nothing of it has been seen by eye: whether the screen's words read right, whether the world drawn behind the screen at ten a second shows anything amiss on the reveal, whether a person first seen through a doorway pops into a room a moment late, whether a small part leaving a far body can be seen go, whether the client's far tiers are worth turning on, whether a moving thing's shadow 54 to 320 m off can be seen trailing it while the view turns, whether the far shadows now reaching the ground across the sun look right, whether a face, an armour plate or a head of hair up close in the world, the doll or the select screen looks exactly as it did before the fourth wave, whether the sky's reflection, a few texels sharper without the old blur, reads right on water and chrome, whether a real-elevation world's loading screen looks as it did, and how the arrival, a walk and a crowded square feel at the owner's frame rate are his to judge.

**SOE complete** (on `claude/soe-complete`, waves 1 to 4 of 4 and a fifth, a sixth and a seventh the owner asked for; the real conversion of the owner's packs for waves 1 to 3 ran on 2026-10-19, 12 minutes 28 seconds, and wave 4 asks for Rori's again, which `status` names). Built: every command reads SOE's last publishes out of an NGE install through SOE's final names list (`docs/ASSETS.md`), the one file of the game's the repository carries, by the owner's decision of 2026-10-18; every pack says which mount it came from and `status` asks once for whatever came from the other; and the converter half of the menus: 63 vehicles in the gallery (the airspeeders and two more walker templates sorted as kinds), 182 houses, 28 deeds of ours over the client's house deeds Core3 has no row for (their lots and upkeep not known, which the Housing tab says), fourteen more ridden mounts found by their appearance (the beast master's, the trading-card ones, the whisper bird, the reptilian flier and the snorbal among them, every one on a saddle of its own), SOE's final creator table (424 rows, 489 hairstyles, with rows of the Sullustans' and the Wookiees' own), the trading-card decorations as props (their deeds, datapads and travel terminals left out), 295 NPC ship types in 47 families up to tier 10 which fly, fit and take damage as tier 5 above it (`npcShips({ ladder })`: the skill reaches ships in the air, the fit and the strength the next ship stood), the AT-XT seating two in the bird saddle's pose, the `undead` walk and run converted and worn by nobody, and no lair stood on the sites the late buildout tables added (circles of ours on Dathomir, Endor and Lok). A pilot conversion of a copy of the owner's packs took 12 min 25 s, left nothing a stamped command owns unwritten, and the suite is green on it and on the packs before it. Wave 2 finished the menus at run time: the four last dances named on the emote list (`breakdance 3` to `6`, the performance table's own keys), beards reading "Beard N" and the other gender's hairstyles left out where this gender's already show their meshes (so a Wookiee of either gender sees the table's twelve and a Sullustan man his thirty-nine), the clothes that read "Other" for want of a word in their ids under their arrangement's first slot (Other is two to four pieces now, where it held over a hundred), the NPC ship ladder's tiers 6 to 10 as sliders in the debug menu (`npcShips` among the Tuning helpers), and `__debug.late()`, saying for every pack already fetched whether it carries SOE's final files and for every menu whether its late thing shows, which `lateMenus.test.ts` asks of the packs on disk (33 checks of packs converted with them on the owner's). A headless tab of our own on the owner's packs found every one of the twelve packs it fetched carrying them and every menu showing its late thing, opened the seven menus they fill with no program built in play, and moved the ladder's sliders through the debug menu; a tier 10 X-wing stood with tier 5's shields, armour, hull and gun damage. Wave 3 gave the owner's install SOE's own people for Kashyyyk's seven zones and Mustafar: `spawns --nge=<his NGE copy>` reads their placed spawners, the people who stand in one place, the corpses, the dungeons' crews room by room in every copy and Mustafar's wild regions into `assets-private/nge/`, and every arrival on one of those worlds stands them after everybody else, behind its loading screen, with Mustafar's herds laid by the game's own grid; wave 6 made them everybody's (below). It gives no city crowds (SOE's crowd in Kachirho and Mensix came from a table his copy does not hold), no quest givers' conversations (they stand, silent beyond the game's greeting), no travel rows and no comparison with Core3 (which holds nothing on these worlds). A pilot conversion into a copy of what `spawns` reads wrote it with nothing unresolved and every zone's placed objects on the pack's own at a median of nought, and left the Core3 half's packs byte for byte what the command writes without the flag; a headless tab of our own stood its people on Kachirho and Mustafar, Mustafar's herds over its own regions, a Myyydril copy's crew in its rooms, left it out against a relay of this build and stood it there once switched to `always`. Mustafar's arrival point, the flattest spot near its layout centre, stood among SOE's own aggressive people, and arriving killed the player there and again at every coming round, so the overlay's people and herds who pick a fight on sight are kept 64 m (ours, `__debug.people({ arrivalClear })`) from where the player comes in and back, a teleport's spot included; its patrollers walk their rounds. Wave 4 gave Rori Restuss in both of its states: the living launch city, which no copy of the final client's snapshot places, read out of the late patch's copy with its raw ids and appended after everything else (696 of its 820 objects stand, with its own starport and shuttleport, their nine travel things, 17 fittings, and Core3's 37 people indoors who find their rooms only now), or its 35 ruins on the city's own places, the late camp's two dead pads its only ports, Core3's town within 700 m skipped and a First Brigade of ours standing guard (23 people at posts by a rule over the ruins, every number a slider under `restuss` in the debug menu); the camp and the faction cantinas stand either way, and nothing that stands in only one state moves anybody's index. Which it is, is a setting, the ruins unless changed (the owner's choice; Esc → **Gameplay** → The world, `__debug.restuss('living')`), taken at the next arrival on Rori and never in the middle of one; on a server it is the admin's word for everybody (`worldStateSet`, the hail's `states`; the relay must be restarted for it), which a session's first arrival reads by a look at the saved server's hail of its own. A permanent placement row may carry a state, so rubble the editor puts down stands in the ruins alone. A pilot conversion of a copy of the owner's packs took 3 min 16 s (Rori's snapshot, its walk grid, its placed sounds -- 231 emitters, 77 of them the living city's -- travel, fittings, spawns, effects, doors, the creator's Rori scene and the conversations), kept every row the pack held before in its place but the fittings' last three (the server's own, keyed by nothing that outlives a run), and left `status` asking nothing and the local overlay unasked; every suite of `test:converter` and every other suite that reads the packs passed on it but `ngeSpawns`' one check that git ignores its folder (the copy was no checkout). A headless tab of our own stood both states in numbers and screenshots with no program built in play across either arrival (counted over the whole history, `shaders({ full: true })`, since the plain list stops at twenty), played 231 placed sounds in the living city and 154 in the ruins, bought a ticket at the living shuttleport's terminal and had the living starport's collector take it and begin the ride, and on its own dev server put rubble down with the editor, turned it to ruins only with the State button (it then stood only after the arrival in ruins) and took it out again; the brigade's 23 people stand clear of every ruin; and on a relay of this build a non-admin was refused, the admin's word reached the other, the next arrival stood it, a restart kept it, and a tab whose own setting was ruins stood the server's living city and another that started on Rori stood the server's ruins on its very first arrival. Nothing of wave 4 has been seen by eye: the ruins draw very dark in those screenshots, which may be their scorched pictures or a fault; whether the brigade's posts, people and levels read as a guard, and whether the ruins want rubble placed by hand, are the owner's. Wave 5 lets every one of the garage's 70 ship hulls, the room ships among them, be stood as an NPC ship flying for the Empire, the Alliance, Black Sun, the pirates or nobody, at a tier from 1 to 10 through the ladder, alone or as a patrol of three in that side's formation (the NPC tab's "Any ship as an NPC" list, found by its find box, and `npcShip('<garage id>', { faction, tier, count })`), fitted as an NPC type it draws at the nearest tier where one exists (37 of the 70) and on its stock fit otherwise, named as the game names that type or in the garage's words; no conversion and no relay restart. A headless tab of our own in Tatooine's orbit stood a pirate YT-1300 patrol that went for the player's X-wing, an Imperial X-wing at tier 8 with tier 5's chassis, shields, armour and gun damage, a neutral corvette from the tab's own button that was held while the panel stood open and flew once it shut, three Imperial corvettes 446 to 913 m apart and a Star Destroyer 1,564 m out, every stand's programs built behind its hold but one Star Destroyer shadow program after it showed (the performance pass's known plain-caster gap). Its review found the Star Destroyer the one hull the NPC ships' fighter-sized distances could not hold: a patrol of three stood two of its members past the 4 km every NPC ship was drawn to, its wingmen's own slots past the 4 km a pilot chases, and its pilots never fired nor were fired on, since a pilot measured its 1 km guns from the middles and kept off the whole 1.3 km sphere; a big hull's group is now drawn and leashed as far as its size asks and every pilot's sight and reach count the part of a hull past a fighter's sphere, all of it nought for the game's own NPC hulls. A headless tab of our own then drew an Imperial patrol of three Star Destroyers whole (3.3, 6.3 and 4.6 km off) with its leader firing on the player's X-wing within ten seconds, and a lone one traded fire with a typed Rebel X-wing patrol (48 shots of theirs in half a minute, one hit; six of its own, none), with no program built after either stand. Fighters still reach a Star Destroyer only off its bow and stern, since the break-off keeps them off its whole sphere, and its guns take their turns round the whole hull (`takeShot`), so many of its shots leave from its far side and fall short. How any of them flies, fights or looks is the owner's to see. Wave 6 gave Kashyyyk and Mustafar their people for everyone, by the owner's decision of 2026-10-19: the rows wave 3 read off his copy are committed as `tools/swg/ngeref/` (533 KB: names, numbers and places, no model, string, script or quest; `nge-reference` writes it, and keeps it only when the overlay built from it is the one built from the copy, with the archives and without), every `spawns` reads it with no flag, so every launcher conversion stands those worlds' 17,677 people, 34 wild areas and 496 creatures, `--nge=<folder>` still reads a live copy and `--nge=none` leaves them out, the release carries the folder, `status` asks for `spawns` again of packs converted without them, and the browser stands them everywhere with no opt-in (on a server as the world's seeded bodies, each named by its own key so a browser whose catalogue joins fewer bodies still names every other alike; `__debug.people({ nge })` stays to compare); and Mustafar now arrives on the Mensix Mining Facility's landing deck, its own floor about 125 m over the valley, where a death comes round too, under a loading screen when it came far from the deck (`__debug.arrival()`). A pilot of `spawns` on a copy of the owner's packs took 17 s, stood the very counts wave 3's local overlay did zone by zone, left every Core3 file byte for byte as it was, and left `status` asking only for `conversations` (whose own rule asks again after any `spawns`); a headless tab of our own stood a character new to Mustafar on the deck's own collider (0 m over it, 125 m over the ground), brought a death round there, and stood Kashyyyk's and Mustafar's people with no opt-in, against a relay of this build as well (as seeded bodies), with no program built in play. The owner's own packs need `spawns` and `conversations` run once more (the lead's real run; `status` asks for both in turn), which replaces wave 3's local overlay in place. Wave 7 stood what SOE's last publishes added and nothing names, made the skeletal props stand, and gave the NPC-only ships hulls: 46 entries of ours from a committed table (`tools/swg/manifests/ours-entries.json`, 20 of the census's 66 files left out with their reasons, eight of them for wearing exactly what a template's own file wears) -- 15 garments in the Clothes tab (the Kel Dor masks, Han's shirt, trousers and vest, the dark side amulet, the Singing Mountain holograms, the Kubaz cloak and goggles, a second Nightsister hat, the Ithorians' Love Day halo), the Singing Mountain hair hologram in a human woman's hair grid, 14 bodies in a group of their own in the NPC tab (Selonians, a Kubaz, Togorians, an Ankura Gungan, Dugs, an Ugnaught in a robe) and 14 props in the Props tab's "Ours" (the Dathomir fences, gate and sign, mushroom clumps, Vader's pod in two pieces, two paintings, a Havoc gun), every one marked "ours" with a name of ours; 438 props on 366 skeletal models (221 MB) the props command had skipped, each standing still in its own idle's first frame (or its bind pose, `--skeletal-pose=bind`, which later runs keep) and drawn, cast and picked up as any prop; and 23 NPC-only hulls (111 MB, never in the garage), which take the NPC types from 295 to 410 in 67 families and give the NPC tab an End of War group of eight capital ships and stations by their own names, each for its own side at a tier of their own (`npcShips({ endOfWar: { tier } })`). `__debug.oursEntries()` says what each menu holds of it. A pilot conversion of a copy of the owner's packs took 12 min 51 s (the four wardrobes 1 min 47 s, 1 min 47 s, 37 s and 35 s, `customization` 2 s, `props` 2 min 39 s, `objeffects` 4 s, `ships` 1 min 48 s, `sounds` 5 s, `mobiles` 3 min 27 s, the deliberate full rebuild), left `status` asking nothing of this wave, and every suite passed on it but `ngeSpawns`' one check that git ignores its folder (the copy was no checkout). A headless tab of our own on the pilot wore Han's vest and a Kel Dor mask with nothing built, put the Singing Mountain hair on with nothing compiled on the swap, stood five bodies of ours, put a fence of ours, a rancor statue, a gackle bat and an armour helmet down, picked each up, turned it and put it down again (they build what any prop put down builds: a game prop of the game's own, a muja fruit, built ten programs and waited on a link one frame the same way), found every menu marking the entries of ours, and in Endor's orbit stood a Lancer Frigate that went for the player's X-wing, a patrol of three rebel transports 300 m to 1.3 km out and a Nebulon frigate from the tab's own buttons, with none built after any stand; three stood one after another from one spot had first wrecked each other, which is now refused in words, as is one asked for while another is still being built there. The owner's own packs need `wardrobe` (four runs), `customization`, `props`, `objeffects`, `ships`, `sounds` and `mobiles` run once more (the lead's real run; `status` asks for each). Nothing of it has been seen by eye at the owner's frame rate: how the new bird mounts carry their rider (their saddle stands pitched about 49 degrees at the idle on the pilot where the pose's own clip root leans about 17, which may be the game's authoring or a fault of how the two compose; the saddle test prints the pitch and pins none), how an airspeeder flies, how a tier 10 fighter fights (the sliders under `npcShips` in the debug menu are where he moves it), and whether the hair grid's beards, the Wookiees' styles above their fur pattern and the humans' trim colour read right on the appearance page are the owner's to judge.

**Volumetrics** (on `claude/volumetrics`, waves 1, 2, 2b, 3, 4, 5, 6, 7a and 7b, the whole pass; the design is `A:\swg3js\volumetrics\DESIGN.md`, the waves' notes `W1-notes.md`, `W2-notes.md`, `W2b-notes.md`, `W3-notes.md`, `W4-notes.md`, `W5-notes.md`, `W6-notes.md`, `W7a-notes.md` and `W7b-notes.md` beside it). Built: a master on the Graphics page's new **Volumetrics** section, **off by default**, and off it is today's game to the program (proven in node against PRE_PASS's own source and in a driven tab by shader-source hashes, passes, products and memory against a PRE_PASS build, on Direct3D 11 and Vulkan both); turned on in play it says "Volumetrics on: preparing shaders" while it builds what it needs off the frame (about half a second, the old picture drawn meanwhile, no frame waiting on a link) and changes over once every program has linked, and nothing it does builds a program in play. With it on, a window of moving air 192 m across follows the camera (a stable-fluids solver on the card at 60 steps a second, one layer over the ground in this wave), stirred by every running engine (a hovering ship's and a flying car's downwash straight down included), every blast (a thermal detonator queues 22 shoves), the Force's push, pull and repulse, and the weather's wind and gusts; it carries dust lifted where the ground gives it -- in the ground's own colour, much off sand, little off rock and paving, none off mud or water, as the ground's families are sorted by a table of ours -- settling in three seconds, shed by dusty ground in a dust storm and blown in on its wind; machines throw grains that fall back and lie a moment (from wave 3 a tenth as many off rock and paving as off sand with the kick-up on, `grains.hard`, and none over water, alike at any frame rate) and a thermal detonator 300; the game's own storm sheets are thinned while the volume draws the storm (`volStormThin`); and the dust is drawn as a volume over the band of air near the ground, lit by the very sun and sky the scene is, darkened by the dust toward the sun and by the sun's own cascades (dust in a wall's shade measures half as bright), and never drawn indoors, under water, in space, with nothing airborne, inside a building's rooms seen through a door (a compound's halls included), or with the moving air or the dust switched off. Every number is in `AIR_TUNE` (`air({ tune })`, its feel numbers sliders in the debug menu) and every setting a console line (`volumetrics({...})`). Seen only in my own headless tab, in numbers and a few screenshots, never by the owner: how thick the dust under a ship or behind a pod should be, how fast it settles, and above all the storm -- at the design's numbers a dust storm is an opaque wall about six metres off -- are his to judge, as is whether the volume reads as dust at all; the GPU time was not measured (headless Chrome has no timer query), and a pod was not driven past about 125 m/s. The review round's mends (the switches, the halls, the obstacles under the direct draw, the shed, the moving frame's allocation) were proven in node and checked once in a headless tab of our own, in numbers only. **The second wave gave the ground what passes over it** (its drift of the ground's own picture taken out again by wave 2b, below): a speeder skimming sand cuts a rut (2 to 3 cm along its track at 18 m/s), a ship hovering at 8.7 m scours a pit (21 cm in ten seconds, with 2.3 of dust over it), a blast leaves a crater, and every footstep on ground that gives is pressed into it as a print in place of the flat decal (seven steps on sand, seven prints and no decals; on rock today's rule, which lays none); pressed ground darkens and bends the light, and thick dust shades the ground under it. Three art dials (the sand, since wave 2b the blowing sand's; how deep the prints read; how dark the dust's shadow) and switches and details for the sand and the prints are on the page; at every art dial nought the ground draws the plain ground (0 pixels on Vulkan, one half-float step on 84 pixels on Direct3D 11). Seen only in my own headless tab, in numbers and screenshots, never by the owner: how the prints, the ruts and the pits look is his to judge, and above all that at the demo's swirl the still air sustains eddies of its own that lift the sand over a share of the window in Tatooine's 0.9 m/s breeze (about a fifth by the second wave's drift; wave 2b's blowing sand shows over 2.6% of the window with no jet near, and over none with the swirl at nought) (`air({ tune: { solver: { swirl: 0 } } })` is the still air; `air({ tune: { ground: { liftAdd } } })` the sand's threshold); and a speeder left idling digs its rut under itself as deep as the fade lets it, as the demo's arithmetic does. Its review round mended what the session's ground read after an in-play warm was thrown away (the prints went unseen and undecaled for the session), a step lost on the frame a door was walked through, a crater pressed under a ship destroyed overhead (a blast now digs only within half its radius of the ground, `prints.craterReach`), the sand's and the prints' storage held while their switches were off, and the air's ground going unknown after a flight round a place (the terrain let go of the family grids of chunks still standing, which the feet read too); the zero-dial diff's canvas now reads at the level of its own drift on Vulkan (seven runs: within both of its noises in four, and 46 to 101 pixels against 20 to 89 in three, scattered over the picture as the noise is, the scene nought every time), the compare having measured its noise one draw apart and the switch two. At the defaults a person's print (a boot's size, 3.5 cm on sand, at most 18% darker) is hard to make out on Tatooine's rippled sand from the third-person camera -- the depth, the darkening and the print's size are the owner's dials -- and since wave 2b a print reads where it was pressed (the second wave read it where its drift had carried the sand). **The third wave's first half (2b) gave the dust its shape and the sand its blowing**: every patch of dust carries how high it stands, how spread it is, how fast it rises and how old it is (a float32 pair beside its density, by six laws exact at any frame rate), so a speeder's dust is thrown up behind it to about 2.1 m 1.8 s on and falls back, a racing pod's and bike's held at the same throw (3.3 m) rather than a wall along the line, a hovering ship's downwash and a blast climb and spread, and a storm's dust stands as a half-normal about the storm's own height; the dust's colour is its ground's lighter tones under the ground's own colour map, drawn by the ground's own functions, the storm's eased toward the ground it blows over; the dust fades out between 0.5 and 2.5 m of the eye; the light toward the sun is an optical depth at four heights (0, 2, 5 and 10 m), so dust lifted over a dark patch is lit as itself, with two new dials on the page, Dust brightness (60%) and Strength of the dust's own shade (100%); the second wave's drifting sand is gone, and in its place blowing sand -- a picture of grains of our own, made a slice a frame while the master warms, carried in two phases that restart in turn so it never smears -- streaks over sand, snow, ash and dirt where the air blows past the threshold and stops a moment after, the ground's own picture never moving (`air({ view: 'sand' })` shows its strength); and a Force push sends a wave of air and a band of dust out along its cone over about a second, a pull rolls one in from its reach and a repulse opens a ring with its middle clear (`air({ force })` makes one at your feet). Seen only in my own headless tab, in numbers and screenshots, never by the owner: at the defaults a speeder's trail barely shows in the chase view (the camera sits behind it, the dust is as bright as its sand by design and the near fade takes what passes the eye; at Dust brightness 100% it is hardly more), a front reads mostly as the dust's shade on the ground (the art dial), and a full dust storm is a wall the figure five metres off is lost in -- each the owner's to judge, as is how the blowing sand looks, which shows round any jet and, at the demo's swirl, over patches of the still air's own eddies. On Vulkan the zero-dial diff's scene now differs by one half-float step on 2 to 9 pixels of 1.27 million (none in the second wave), under the canvas's own noise, while the ground self test still holds the variant at dial nought to the plain ground to the bit there; turning the master on in a fresh session built its 18 programs behind the notice with no frame gap over 20 ms on either backend. Its smoke, in numbers on Tatooine in a headless tab of our own (Direct3D 11): a speeder at 18 m/s stands its dust 0.39 m up at the machine and 0.43 m three metres back, rising to 2.09 m 60 m (3.3 s) behind it and down to 1.5 m 105 m back; a pod raced at 167 m/s (upgrades full, boost held) never stood its dust past 2.6 m; the carried colour read back as laid is the mirror's over the rasters as the pass filters them within 4e-4; with the camera inside a column of dust the near fade takes half its in-scatter (0.0096 against 0.0199) and nine metres back none (0.00487 against 0.00485); a full dust storm's density at eye height ten metres out is 0.729 of the first wave's for the same read-back density, its colour eased from the first wave's constant to the ground's in about four and a half seconds, and the light's optical depth there is 7.6, 7.1, 6.2 and 4.5 at 0, 2, 5 and 10 m; with the swirl at nought the blowing sand is gone 2.5 s after a jet stops; a push lays its first dust about a metre ahead, a pull at 16 m and a repulse leaves its middle clear, alike at 65 and a held 32 frames a second; the master turned on in play built its 18 programs behind the notice and none in play, waited on no link over 0.7 ms and made the sand's picture in eight slices of at most 1.8 ms; the ground's air variant holds 14 samplers of 16; and no new view, dial or switch built a program. A thick column (D 3.7) on sand at noon reads at a third of the sand's brightness on the canvas and at 0.93 of it in the morning: the brightness dial scales the in-scatter exactly (0.067, 0.133 and 0.223 at 0.3, 0.6 and 1) and the self-shadow at nought takes it from 0.14 to 0.25, and where either should sit is the owner's. **The third wave gave the air its layers, its smoke and its fog.** The air is three layers over the ground (to 3.5, 9 and 16 m, the page's Layers of air, three at High), each relaxing toward its own share of the wind and stopped only by what stands taller than its floor, the layers trading momentum; dust rides them by how much of each patch stands in each (a blast's dust at 4.8 m drifted at 6.0 m/s in a 9.4 m/s wind where a low jet's drifted at 2.75), and crosses a roof lower than its layer (over a 3.7 m wall, 0.2 to 0.5 at 5.4 m over the roof and a trail 20 m beyond it). Smoke is a channel a layer, carried by its layer's air, climbing and thinning by the demo's table, put in by a blast (a grenade's held 2.6 s), a wreck, a damaged engine's smoke and the gas and bug clouds, whose harm is still the sphere and whose old puffs are drawn wherever the volume does not draw the smoke. A fog of each world's own is drawn per world per day on the shared clock (the owner's table: Rori and Naboo about three mornings in four, Corellia and Talus one in three, the jungles and forests often, Dantooine sometimes, none on Tatooine, Lok, Mustafar, in space, the copies or a real-elevation world), comes in on the wind as a bank with gaps and from the ground in patches, drains into hollows (over 44 m of Naboo's relief 0.35 in the lowest quarter against 0.24 on the highest after two minutes), its middle drifting downwind and its pattern losing its likeness (0.95, 0.88 and 0.76 after 10, 30 and 60 s), parts behind a speeder (0.29 on its path against 0.90 fourteen metres off it), lies on a lake's water, and burns off over the three hours after first light (3,100 to 1.3 by mid-morning). The dust breaks into wisps (a material a mode: off, bands, noise, two octaves, High's the one octave; a dust storm's wall turns to curls with a figure five metres off seen through it), blown sand is carried on over any ground (on Lok, 6.6 m of carry at 6 to 9 m over ground that does not blow), and a Force push, pull or repulse knocks what it reaches as its wave arrives, which changes Force play for everybody (the Gameplay page's "The Force's knock travels", on, with its speed; off is the instant knock to the bit). With the master off nothing of it is made (`airOff.test.ts` against the pass's start still holds). Seen only in my own headless tabs, in numbers and a few screenshots (`A:\swg3js\volumetrics\w3-shots\`): how the fog looks (a pinkish-brown haze in Naboo's hollows by morning light, the hills standing out of it), how tall a gas cloud's column should stand (its smoke climbs into the top layer by the demo's table, so a held cloud is a pillar), whether the knock's wave speed reads, and above all whether the climb is right (the design's own check wants the top layer under a tenth of the middle one's, which the demo's table it also asks for cannot give: 0.55 at 2.6 s and about 1 at the peak, the `smoke.climb` dials) are the owner's. On a cold Direct3D 11 profile the master turned on in play built its 23 programs behind the notice and none in play, but one passed the queue's 5 s bound and the main thread waited about 0.8 s for it, and after the change-over the browser held the frames 1.2 to 2.6 s while its GPU process caught up; with the shader cache warm, nothing over 21 ms; Vulkan two 33 ms frames. Its review round counted the fog's day at the drawn midnight against the shared clock (it had stepped mid-morning at a low frame rate and after a hidden tab), laid a gas cloud's smoke before a blast's and judged its puffs by its whole sphere and by a full batch, put the knock's loop where node runs the game's own (`forceWaveKnock.ts`), and gave the fog by world its thickness ranges as dials; a world's fog does not follow its weather, by choice (the weather is an area's, the draw the world's). Measured in a headless tab of our own after it: the knock landed as the front reached a creature 12.46 m out at 465 ms on the simulation's clock against the front's 463 with the master off, and at 12.22 m at 451 against 448 with it on, the instant knock in the frame of the press either way; a poison cloud stung a held creature alike with the master off and on (52.4 and 52.3 health over six seconds, the same at every tick), its puffs drawn with the master off and its smoke with it on; Naboo drew 0.44, none, 0.66, 0.72 and 0.72 on five mornings running and Tatooine none on three; and on Lok sand blown by a jet ran 3 to 5 m onto ground that does not blow and, the jet taken away, faded by the dial's exp(−1.5 t) (0.147 to 0.033 from one second to two). **The fourth wave gave the air what it carries besides dust.** A machine, a blast and a Force push on grass and forest litter throw leaves (the game's own leaf, Corellia's green one where it has it) that tumble, fall, lie a few seconds and are lifted again by a wind past their share of the sand's threshold; on sand, ash and dirt puffs of the game's own sand cloud, and where a jet blows hard on the ground, and in a dust storm, fine streaks of ours stretched along their own motion (the owner's Q7); over water spray and never a grain, a puff, a leaf or a streak (Q6), and behind a machine skimming water the game's own vehicle water trail's look (Q14: `pt_vehicle_water_trail.prt`, its splash in no converted pack, so its sizes, throw, life and fade are kept and the fountain's splash lent); a running foot kicks a couple of leaves off grass, or spray where it wades (Q17). They live on the card (a pool of 4,352 at High: 2,048 leaves, 256 puffs, 1,024 spray, 1,024 streaks), carried by the air of their own height, lifted by a slope or a wall and lofted by a swirl, the leaves lit by the leaf cards' own client sum and shaded by the trees (the grass's shadow tap), the rest as the dust is. The rain of ours falls in a box 32 m round the camera (16,384 drops at High), carried by the same air, stopped by the weather's own roofs and the ridden hull, lit by the sky's light and against the sun, the game's own rain sheets stood down whole while it falls (`volRainThin`), and snow the same off until turned on (`volSnow`). The pictures are borrowed from an effect that already holds them, or a copy loaded from the world's pack (or the props pack's). Every number of feel is a slider under "Leaves, puffs and spray" and "Rain and snow"; the page's Volumetrics section has a group for the kinds, their count, the leaves' shadow, the rain, the snow and the drops; `air({ particles })`, `air({ rain })` and `air({ view: 'class' })` are the console's. The master turned on in play now builds the wisps mode in force alone (W3's cold warm on Direct3D 11: a mode moved later is built off the frame, the last ready one drawn meanwhile). Seen only in my own headless tabs, in numbers and screenshots (`A:\swg3js\volumetrics\w4-shots\`), never by the owner: on a cold Direct3D 11 profile the master on in play built its 27 programs behind the notice and none in play in 0.9 s, the main thread never busy past 50 ms, then one frame held 0.9 s while the browser's GPU process caught up (W3: an 0.8 s main-thread wait and holds of 1.2 to 2.6 s); Vulkan 1.1 s with one 50 ms frame; every setting stepped built nothing in play; a jet on a lake threw spray alone, a landspeeder skimming it at 12 m/s 512 drops of the wake (not seen from the chase camera behind it), a speeder on sand puffs and streaks; the rain forced stood 16,384 drops with none under a roof, leaning with the wind, and falls on the wind's inflow with the moving air off. Found there and mended: every drop of spray drew black (a packed white unpacked as `int(p + 0.5)` in a float32 came back 2^24), and no dust storm lifted a streak (at a `streakLift` of one the sand's threshold, rising with the wind, stood over the storm's own air: 0.4 now, the storm's strength scaling the tries; 57 streaks in six seconds of a full storm). Its review round mended five things nobody had seen: the rain left the box whenever the camera came down faster than it fell (it wraps now, and a move past the box fills it afresh), a puff's lower edge faded by its middle's height, the leaves took none of the air's light toward the sun, a wisps mode chosen while the master was off was never built after its warm, and a field the warm threw away stayed in the world's material sets; its programs were compiled and run again in a headless Direct3D 11 tab of my own (every one linked, a staged record of 40 puffs and 35 leaves born as staged, 4,096 drops kept in the box through a 60 m/s descent). The owner's to judge: how thick each kind should be and how big, the rain (faint against sunlit sand, clear against a dark sky), the snow (at its 1 cm a flake is not seen at 1600 by 900; at 5 cm it is), whether the wake reads at all, and how many streaks a storm lifts. **The fifth wave gave the water and the lava a current, and drew Mustafar's lava falls** (the owner's Q8). With the master on, the lakes and the sea near you move: a fourth tile of the air's atlas over the cells whose ground is water or lava, the land its walls, the water pulled toward a share of the wind (0.12) and the lava toward stillness or its fall's own flow, every engine, blast and Force push stirring it; a jet or a blast over water rings the ripples and a drop of spray that falls back rings them where it lands; the water's own fine pattern rides the current (a variant of each body's material and its mask twins, key `…-air`) and its ripples are carried by it (a Catmull-Rom carry before a frame's first ripple step); and the lava's crust is carried by its flow and torn where it is stirred (a crust twin of each lava look, two phases three seconds apart). Each has its switch (Water current, Lava's crust), a detail (64 to 192) and an art dial (Current over the water, Crust carried and torn), every number of feel a slider under "Water and lava"; with a switch off or an art dial at nought the material is today's. The survey (`W5-notes.md` §1) found that the only river items the game was missing are Mustafar's thirty-three lava falls, the terrain's ribbons, which nothing read; they are drawn now behind the Graphics page's "Lava falls" switch, off by default (on with the master off: two meshes on Mustafar, about 1,200 triangles, no program), in the lava's own look, feeding their flow to the liquid with the master on. Thirteen of them name a lava shader no converted pack holds (the `water` command collects only the water tables' shaders), so they wear the world's own lava until the converter takes the ribbons' shaders and Mustafar's water is converted again (not run); they do not burn, and the ground family under them is not painted. And on today's ground most of them cannot be seen: measured in the review round, only 24 of their 2,052 quad corners stand at or over our ground, on 5 of the 33 falls (the client's ribbon evidently lowered its ground along the strip, which our generator, holding the affector inert, does not), so the switch shows little until a terrain wave carves them. Dathomir's two rivers and Lok's one draw no water in the client either. Seen only in my own headless tabs, in numbers and screenshots (`A:\swg3js\volumetrics\w5-shots\`): at an arrival with the master on every water body wears its current variant and every lava mesh its crust twin, nothing of the wave built in play; turned on in play the master built its 37 or 38 programs behind the notice and none in play (Mustafar 0.55 s on Direct3D 11 and 1.0 s on Vulkan, Naboo 1.2 s on Vulkan, and on a cold Direct3D 11 profile 6.6 s with the main thread never busy past 257 ms while the browser held frames up to 1.6 s), after a fault found there and mended (only one stand-in a program key was adopted, so two lake programs were built in play on Vulkan and the main thread stalled 8.1 s on cold Direct3D 11); its review round mended, in node and by reading and never yet in a tab, a variant made in play being worn before its warm had built it (the master turned on in play over a field the session kept, ribbons stood in play), every drop of spray ringing the ripples every frame over a sea below -0.5 m (Naboo's two), the last world's ribbons and liquid state outliving a travel, and the flow raster uploaded at every window move on every world; a blast on a fall pushed the lava to 7.4 m/s and it was back to its fall's own 0.9 within a second; a jet 8 m over Naboo's lake pressed the ripples every frame. The owner's to judge: how the current and the crust look (`air({ view: 'current' })`, `air({ view: 'crust' })`), and above all how much still water swirls: at the demo's swirl share of 1 Theed's water keeps eddies of its own, 1.5 to 3 m/s with peaks of 12 to 14 near the shores, where at 0 it is a smooth 0.2 to 0.6 m/s toward the wind's share (`air({ tune: { liquid: { waterSwirl: 0 } } })`). **The sixth wave gave the air what else stirs it, the grass, and its cost measured.** Another player's speeder, pod or ship stirs the air on your screen from where you see it, with a push of ours by its kind (nothing of theirs is sent and the relay is as it was), throwing its dust, leaves and spray through the fourth wave's records, and a ship of theirs standing clear of the ground blows down under it; somebody on foot, or shown nowhere (another world, a jump), stirs nothing. A stood shuttle blows straight down from each engine while a flame its clip lights is in its window, so every screen's pad is blasted on the same shared instant. The near grass leans in the air at its foot by the demo's wheat (`clamp(|v| / 13, 0, 0.85)`, its top sinking by 0.45 of that), laid on its own sway: a twin of the grass's one material, worn once built, and while the grass casts only once a loading screen has drawn its shadow program (turned on in play then, the grass stays plain until the next arrival and the page says so). The page has Grass in the air, Grass bent by the air and Other players, the debug menu "Other players and the shuttles" and "The grass"; `air({ grass: { at } })` reads the lean where you look, and `air({ budget: true })` times every preset and every quality key on the spot. The blowing sand's preset rows moved to twice the air's grid on Low, Medium and High and three times on Ultra (decision 54: High 52 MB where it was 64 at 1600×900, no measurable time between them), a settings file from before taking its preset's new row. Measured in my own headless tabs, in numbers and screenshots (`A:\swg3js\volumetrics\w6-shots\`): at Mos Eisley the transport's lift-off and landing raised the dust's peak on its pad to 10.3 and 11.0 while its flames were lit, with about 40 puffs and 200 streaks a second off the pad, and nothing while they were out; on a relay of this build one tab's swoop driven past another stirred the second tab's air (its engines taken about every frame, 8 to 20 m/s of air and 0.13 to 0.16 of dust at its tail, the grass there leaning its most, 24 leaves started every half second), and none once the rider stood on foot or travelled away; the grass at a swoop's tail leaned 0.51 m over on a 0.6 m tuft and none at all at Grass bent by the air nought; the master turned on in play over grass built its 38 programs behind the notice and none in play, the twin worn after with the grass not casting and kept plain with it casting until the next arrival, which drew its shadow program and wore it with nothing built in play. The budget (`W6-notes.md`; the frame report's GPU timer answered in headless Chrome on both backends this time): at High the air's own step is about 0.25 ms of the card and 0.2 to 0.3 of the main thread, the volume 0.17 (1600×900) and 0.37 ms (2560×1440) of the card on Vulkan and about 0.3 with a wide spread on Direct3D 11; Ultra's volume is 1.1 to 2.7 ms; the volume's registry budget is now 0.4 ms of the card and 0.05 of the main thread. Its review round read a friend's ride off its picture's own box (their ship or flyer blows down from the middle of its underside, and only above the floor its kind flies from, as yours), left the shuttle a passenger rides one downwash (its rig's flames), kept every variant unworn for an effects target nothing had compiled it for (the ground and the liquids had the same hole), made Other players say it needs Engines on, and measured the liquid rows over water and lava (a third of the window Theed's lake, two fifths Mustafar's lava, both backends at 1600×900): the liquid's detail and the current and the crust each off and on moved the air's card time by about 0.04 ms at most; the README carries the presets' table. Q3, the gas across the relay: a thrown gas or bug grenade does not cross at all (it is a physics body, not a bolt), only the stings of its cloud on another player do, as `hit` words, so nobody else's screen draws its smoke; what a relay word would carry is in `W6-notes.md`, and nothing was changed. The owner's to judge: how hard a friend's ride and a shuttle's downwash push (a shuttle's dust on its pad comes to about ten times a speeder's), how far the grass bends and how quickly, and whether the screenshots' dusk light shows enough. **The seventh wave's first half gave the world one wind, which the weather updates and every reader takes.** **Its hold works with the master off** (the owner's "a wind slider or changer I can use right now"): `__debug.wind({ speed, heading, gust })`, or the debug menu's "Hold the wind" sliders under `wind`, holds the speed, the heading or the gusts for the water, the trees and plants, the rain's lean and the sky's two cloud sheets at once (the sheets drift at the held speed times their share aloft, 2.5 and 3.5, a held 0 standing them still), and `wind(null)` gives it back to the weather, every reader then the weather's own to the bit. With the master on, the page's **The wind** switches make it one field over the world: a mean from the weather's rows held to each kind of weather's floor, gust patches travelling downwind on a pattern every browser works out alike from the shared clock with nothing sent (two tabs: 1,000 of 1,000 points equal; on a relay, the same devils), and dust devils by day on dry ground, with an art dial (Gusts over the wind) and the trees taking each gust where they stand from the next arrival (`volWindTrees`, 16 to 18 programs of a forest's arrival, nothing in play, nothing missed). **That form is the owner's question Q23**: on cold profiles in headless Chrome of ours its depth programs, linked one by one behind the screen, added about 11 to 13 s to Naboo's arrival on Direct3D 11 (32.1 and 32.3 s against 21.0 and 18.9; the screen's sweep 16 programs against 6) and 0.6 s on Vulkan, and on Yavin 4 a noisy +3 s and nothing; it stays on until he answers, and with it off the trees sway at the weather's mean. The air relaxes toward it, a gust sheds dust where the ground gives it, a devil spins its air and stands a column of dust (1.55 at its core, its middle 4 m up), leaves and puffs, the kick-up's pickups and the rain's lean follow the gusts, the near grass leans by it outside the air's window and shows a paler side as it bends, the sky's sheets drift at the world's wind aloft, and `air({ view: 'gusts' })` draws the patches. The gust function in the game's own program agrees with its processor copy within 2.8e-4 on Direct3D 11 (the design's line 1e-3), its text now written so the node tests run it; letting go of a held heading gives every player's pattern back at once, the devils' list keeps the nearest sixteen, the read-outs follow a forced level and the hold, the grass's lean outside the window takes slab 0's own wind, and the sheets take the world's wind aloft while a cloud deck of the next wave draws (`WIND_STATE.decks`); the master on in play built its 34 programs behind the notice, none in play; the wind's frame makes 0.029 bytes in twelve node processes at once (it made 1.2 MB every thousand frames before five boxing traps were found). Seen only in my own headless tabs, in numbers and screenshots (`A:\swg3js\volumetrics\w7a-shots\`), never by the owner: how gusty a fair day should be, how often devils come and how hard they spin, how far the grass's far cards lean, the sheen's strength and the sheets' wind aloft are his, every one a slider. **The seventh wave's second half gave the sky one cloud system**, which stands in for the flat sheets and the September's volumetric clouds while the master is on (the September's row greys in words; with the master off both are exactly as they were, the source-hash compare against PRE_PASS's build identical over 432 programs on Direct3D 11 and 438 on Vulkan). Each deck is the sky's own mix as the sheets group it (one function both read), every picture a weather map at the sheets' repeat and scroll, drawn as heaps where it is patchy and a translucent layer where it is even by its own numbers (the light `clouds --no-noise` pass, 0.58 s over 19 worlds, run here), the decks drifting at the world's wind aloft at every distance and never faster with the hours (ten minutes of frames: 4.004 m/s against a wind of 4.00), stretched into streets and smeared by the one wind's gusts at a cloud's scale, lit by the dome's own sky and its clear colour on a row with no gradient, night included. In rain and the sunless overcast an overcast layer fills a deck that hangs no sheet, and a low deck 200 m over the eased ground (`clouds({ low: true })` holds it on to see on a dry day) is holed by every ship flying through it, every friend's ride and a landing shuttle, each hole drifting with its deck and filling over about 45 s; from under the deck a hole reads 0.3 to 0.9 clearer than the deck beside it (0.76 to 0.99 again after the review round), under a rain overcast it shows the overcast above, and seen across the deck from inside it the cloud before and beyond the hole hides it whatever the steps (the design's chase-camera line cannot pass by that geometry). A dust storm stands the decks down. Its review round made every ray across a hole's live box take its fine steps (two in three stepped over one narrower than a step), kept the sheets up wherever the clouds refuse (a building's view, under water), put the noise on the card for a chain the Effects switch builds, reset the feed at every new world however it is reached, took the calibration's answers in the order they were asked, and ran every rule of the hole tick, the march's reads and the weather pass's picture read from the programs' own text, the tick on the card equal to its script within 3.6e-7 on both backends. The page's **The clouds** and **The clouds' look** (two art dials) and the debug menu's "Clouds" and "The low deck and its holes" under `air` hold every number of its look. The Start measured the march as one program: the smear stayed a uniform branch, while carrying the low deck's branch while off cost 23 to 82% of the march, so the low deck is a march of its own drawn only while it draws, both built in the warm; on the card the whole pass costs 0.07 to 0.26 ms at High (1600×900, both backends), the noise uploads inside the warm in under 3 ms, the master on in play builds its programs behind the notice and none in play, and every clouds switch, preset and dial built nothing. Seen only in my own headless tabs, in numbers and screenshots (`A:\swg3js\volumetrics\w7b-shots\`), never by the owner: how big, high, deep, dense, bright and soft the decks are, how fast they stream and smear, where the low deck stands and how thick, how big a hole is and how fast it fills, and both art dials are his, every one a slider; a held low deck on a dry noon reads sandy brown in the shots. **The pass is complete.** Not built: other players' steps, any word on the wire for their grenades and clouds, far devils beyond the air's window (Q20), a deck shared between browsers (Q21), a low deck on a fair day (Q22), and Q23 (the trees' gust form's cost on a cold Direct3D 11 screen) waits for the owner with `volWindTrees` on.

**The world editor** (Map-maker I, on `claude/mapmaker1`, waves 1, 2 and 3, the whole pass: the owner moved its fourth wave, looks and attachments, into Map-maker II before cities on the map, and its fifth, taking the world's people and lairs away by key, his own people and the seats, to the end of Map-maker II with the no-spawn zone, both to be built as this design's §7 and §8 have them; the design is `A:\swg3js\mapmaker1\DESIGN.md`, the waves' notes `W1-notes.md`, `W2-notes.md` and `W3-notes.md` beside it). Built, on the dev server alone: F2 turns it on and off. The body is hidden and kept where it stood, and the camera flies free (the move keys, the editor's own up and down, Shift fast, Alt slow, the wheel for the speed) through walls, buildings and the ground's own rooms, the room it is in decided again every frame. Tab gives a cursor (Point) for the editor's window, docked at the left: a **Library** of everything the game can put down (12,023 entries from the light `library` pass: the props catalogue, the gallery's buildings, every world's own placed models and its flora, each with a picture the converter draws, found by a find box, in folding groups, with Recent, Favourites and Used here) and a **Here** tab (the permanent rows round the camera, with Go to, Pick up and Delete). A thing taken in hand stands where the aim meets the world, as it will stand, its box outlined and amber wherever a player would be refused; a click writes it as a row into the world's map-edits file (`public/placements/world/<world>.json`, or the story's with L) and it stands at once, nothing of it built on a frame; O picks a row up, Delete takes it out of its file (the file goes with its last row), and N stands the body where the aim meets the ground for when you leave. A player's refusals are notes and refuse nothing (so are a file past 20,000 rows and a circle past the old 64 m); only the world of copies and a building in space ("not this pass") are refused, and a dev server still running an older endpoint is named and asked to restart. Leaving puts the body back where it stood, in the room it was in, under a loading screen when that is far from the camera and at its own height in space or in water, and closing the tab with the editor on asks first. Rows may name a template and come out of another world (a Naboo tree on Monument, colliding by its trunk and never its canopy), and the real-elevation worlds take them. Every number of feel is a slider under `editor` in the debug menu. **The second wave edits what stands**: a click picks the row under the aim (Shift adds or takes away), a drag in Point boxes rows in, and the Here tab picks by its boxes; what is picked is outlined and carries a gizmo at its pivot, drawn on the interface canvas (three arrows and a ground square, the yaw ring with R held, pitch and roll with Shift+R, the up arrow with F, the scale boxes with C), each handle dragged keeping the point grabbed under the cursor (a press on the rings, the up arrow or the scale boxes while their key is held gives the hold up and drags the handle; a prop's scale boxes lie on its own axes); R, Shift+R (props alone, and only while no move key is held), F and C held turn, tip and roll, raise and scale everything picked about the pivot as one piece, the wheel stepping the angle, the grid or the scale, Ctrl held pausing it, a scale held inside the reader's 0.001 to 1,000; a row moved, pasted or duplicated is written in the room it lands in; the arrows, Page Up and Page Down nudge a grid step, End drops each row onto what is under it (Shift leans it), Home flies to it, the right button orbits it, V takes its faces flush to a neighbour's; snapping (on, 0.25 m and 15°, `[` `]` and `\`) is laid in the file's own frame so the numbers written come out round; Ctrl+D, Ctrl+C, Ctrl+X, Ctrl+V and Delete duplicate, copy, cut, paste and take out, Ctrl+Z and Ctrl+Shift+Z undo and redo up to 200 actions a world (the History tab goes to any point), the middle button takes the thing under the aim in hand again, 1 to 9 are a hotbar, G hides every mark and H shows the keys; the Selected tab edits the first picked row's place, turn and scale and every picked row's own fields, the Tools tab holds the snapping, the axes, the sliders and git's word on the world's files, which the bar says too. A row may carry `scale`, which every browser reads and only the editor writes. Seen only in a headless tab of our own on Direct3D 11, in numbers: on Theed's plaza three crates put down, moved, undone and done again with the file byte for byte both ways, one scaled to 2 whose collider's top the aim then read at twice the height, fifty duplicated in one write of 5 ms with no file read whole, a hundred taken out, put back by an undo and taken out again, the file gone and the tree as it was, no program built for any of it, and a frame of 6.9 ms with a hundred rows picked as with none (the tab's own frame cap). Its review round's mends (the history's jumps over one row, the room a row lands in, a world model pasted elsewhere, a scaled row picked up, the held handles grabbed, Ctrl's pause, the click to Fly, the editor's copies a world left behind, the scale held to the backstops, the dev server's words about the page's own batches, Shift+R for props alone, and the editing's own frame with a hold under way, which made about 60 bytes a frame and now makes nothing in twelve processes, as the steady frame with three rows picked does) were proven in node against the real editing class and never driven in a browser. Not built: everything Map-maker II now holds -- picking the world's own objects, a row's look, effects and sounds, painting and sculpting the ground, and the removal and people files (`removed`, `people`, which the batch refuses in words) -- and Alt with a gizmo drag duplicating the selection (deferred, `W2-notes.md`). Seen only in a headless tab of our own on Direct3D 11, in numbers: a flight 300 m into Mos Eisley's cantina found its room on crossing its wall and never on the street beside it; a world model and a prop never stood before taken, put down and taken out again with nothing built in play and no frame waiting on a link; three houses, a chair and a Naboo tree put on Monument, the tree's trunk stopping a ray and its canopy passing one, all nine rows standing again after a trip away and back; the body put back within 3 mm whether near or far. The gallery houses the editor put down on Monument holding a frame 300 to 440 ms on a link was traced in the second wave to their rooms being drawn before their compile was done, and is mended. How it feels to fly and to edit, how the window and the gizmo read and whether the library's pictures help are the owner's to judge. **The third wave folds and paints.** Every arrival folds the world's permanent rows into the streamer's own region tiers -- instanced with the snapshot's own objects, at their levels, batched for the shadow pass and solid by distance -- holding the regions they fall in until they are filed; a row stood in play stands on meshes of its own until the next arrival or **Fold now** on the Tools tab (a second's loading screen; past 200 such rows the bar says how many and what they cost), and an automatic fold, off, does it in a quiet moment (`editor({ tune: { autoFold: true } })`). Every edit of the second wave works on a folded row. The scatter brush: Shift+click entries in the library, B arms it (with none chosen, the thing in hand), a ring shows its disc on the ground, LMB held paints toward a density counting what of its own already stands there, RMB held erases its own rows in the file a click writes into, each stroke one undo entry; its numbers are sliders under `editor`'s "The brush" and its switches are on the Tools tab. The world's own placed trees can be made to collide by the client's shapes to compare (`flora({ collision: { placed: 'extent' } })`, off). Seen only in a headless tab of our own on Direct3D 11, in numbers: the brush painted 512 Naboo trees in four strokes; at that view the frame was 10.2 ms with them drawn one by one, and 3.5 ms folded at the next arrival against 3.3 ms with none; Fold now took 489 rows in about a second with no program built in play; the arrival with the 512 rows was within the people's own noise of the one without; a Naboo tree folded on Monument cast with the Trees switch and not with the smallest caster; B, LMB and RMB armed, painted, erased and undid; a folded row was moved, undone and done again, and folded again by the automatic fold and by Fold now; everything was deleted afterwards and the tree left as it was. Its review round's mends -- Fold now racing a tier still loading its models (the rows put down after it were never solid), a region held after its tier had begun, every snapshot copy of a model cast at a scaled row's size, a button let go over the editor's window leaving the brush painting, a building painted floating 16 m up on its foundation's box, Fold now from the console with the editor off -- were proven in node on the real streamer and the real editing, never driven in a browser. How the brush feels (its density, spacing, rate and spread of sizes) and whether the automatic fold should be on are the owner's to judge.

**Shaping and painting the ground and its plants, its lakes and its rivers, the looks and attachments of what stands, places on the map, the sky, the grade and the weather per place, the owner's own models and houses with rooms, what the world places taken away, his own people and the no-spawn zone, and the looks his people wear** (Map-maker II, on `claude/mapmaker2`, waves 0 to 13, the whole pass; the design is `A:\swg3js\mapmaker2\DESIGN.md`, the waves' notes `W1-notes.md` to `W13-notes.md` beside it). Built, on the dev server alone: the editor's tools are one list on the Tools tab (`editor().tools`), one armed at a time, its key a toggle -- B the scatter brush as it was, K the sculpt brush, U the paint brush -- and while one is armed the picked rows stay picked but take no hold, so C and the wheel size the brush. The sculpt brush raises (LMB) and lowers (RMB) the ground of any converted world and of the real-elevation worlds a metre in half a second at its middle, flattens it to a height (the height pressed on, a height sampled with Shift+click, a height typed, or each stamp's own; both ways, up only or down only) and smooths it, on a disc 1 to 200 m across with a hard middle and the client's own feathers, live and with no program built: the ground, its heightfield, every planting and its colliders, the grass, the far tiles and their cards, the lakes' depth, the air's ground and the sun's held shadow maps follow it, a held stroke rebuilding within two milliseconds a frame nearest the brush first. It keeps off the game's own objects (a placed tree by its trunk), our buildings and the players' things, and carries our other rows up and down with the ground; a stroke is one undo, its tiles and its carried rows one write. What it writes is a world's heights file, `public/placements/heights/<world>.json` (a 64 m tile of deltas a row), which ships with the build as the placements files do, so a ground shaped here reaches players once its file is committed and merged; with no file, or a file of noughts, every world's ground is the client's to the bit. Three rooms of two buildings are drawn as rooms at last (the deep chasm bunker's 17 and 18, a station's 22), where a cell name beginning with a digit had read as another number. `__debug.sculpt()` says the store, the stroke and the rebuild, `sculpt({ stroke })` runs one from the console and `sculpt({ at })` reads the raw height, the edit and the ground; every number of feel is a slider under `sculpt` in the debug menu. Seen only in a headless tab of our own on Direct3D 11, in numbers: on Tatooine east of Mos Eisley a raise of 40 m radius held two seconds wrote five tiles and stood the ground 4 m higher, rewrote and replanted four chunks and two far tiles in 4.4 ms, built no program and waited on no link, the body put there stood on it and a ray down met the new heightfield; a flatten and a smooth the same; a stroke against a building's wall left the ground under it raw with 56 feet kept off; a crate of ours rose 2 m with a raise under it and came back with the undo; a reload stood a kept stroke at the arrival with nothing rebuilt; on Monument a raise and its undo the same, and the largest brush rewrote and replanted 56 chunks in 23.3 ms; every stroke undone left the file gone. Not driven in a browser: a stroke at a planted rock's foot, beside a placed palm, at the cantina's doorstep, and a stroke held with the mouse (node runs each of them). Its fix round's mends -- a heights file landing while the converted ground was being made (kept and handed to it), a real-elevation world whose raster fails reading its heights file, the keep-off laid once a stamp over its disc and its feet gathered once a stroke (a 200 m brush among a town's feet cost tens of milliseconds a stamp), a stroke begun before the last one's answer had landed keeping what it stamped (and no undo while a stroke is held), the far tiles a stroke reaches past their own squares, the brush's clock keeping its stamp at 144 frames a second, Restuss's other state kept off, a failed re-read leaving the ground as it stood, and a stroke saying what it reached (the game's objects, ours and the players' houses and props by name, with the keep-off off or on) and the file's size as written -- were proven in node, never driven in a browser; the largest brush has still not been held in a town. **The paint brush (the second wave)** paints any converted world, and a real-elevation world once its paint file's world row names a base family (Monument, Helensar: a family of a converted world's pack, `groundPaint({ world: { base: { pack: 'lok', name } } })`, which puts the world on the tile program at its next arrival behind the loading screen, borrowing that pack's pictures), in two layers: **families** -- the world's own (or the lent pack's), chosen from the Tools tab's palette of the pack's own ground pictures (`<img>`, no WebGL), each with the alternate it forces or the pole's own, or taken from under the aim with Shift+click or the middle button -- and **colour**, washed over the client's colour map by its own replace-with-amount; LMB paints, RMB erases, C and the wheel size it, its stamps on its own clock. Cells are 4 m (the owner's), a file of 8 m cells reads and draws as one; the paint is laid in the generator after every snap of the families onto the 8 m pattern, so a world's plants follow it unless the world row's `follow` is off, and the ground under the brush is rewritten in place as it is held; a stroke is one undo, its tiles one write into `public/placements/paint/<world>.json`, which ships with the build. The world row also carries each world's own switches over the client's four anti-tiling layers (the alternates, the tint and its gain cap, the border masks, the cloud shadows) and each family's alternate weights, and on a real-elevation world a colour wash of ours (a base, height and slope bands, a slow variation), off until named, its every number a slider under `groundPaint`. With no paint file every world is e6c2578's ground to the bit, and a real-elevation world with no world row is drawn exactly as before. `__debug.groundPaint()` says the palette, the cells, the worker's paint work, the landings, the world row and whether the ground is tiled; `groundPaint({ stroke })` runs one, `groundPaint({ at })` reads what is painted, built and the world's own at a place.
