Seen only in a headless tab of our own on Direct3D 11, in numbers and four screenshots in `A:\swg3js\mapmaker2\scratch\w2\`: on Yavin 4 forest floor painted into the spawn's clearing wrote two rows, asked six grids of the worker and landed all six, rewrote 24 chunks and two far tiles and replanted only those; scorched earth painted into the jungle took 21 plants away with `follow` on, gave them back with it off and took them again with it on; a colour stroke beside it laid its cells; one 4 m cell took exactly the four poles round its corner and a 4 m line exactly two rows of poles; 24 actions undone left the file gone, done again byte for byte, and undone again; Naboo's alternates switched off rebuilt the ground with nothing built and came back with the undo; Monument refused the brush in words before a base was named, stood on Lok's red sand after a reload with nothing built in play, took a second family and a wash; Tatooine painted, undone and done again byte for byte; nothing built in play anywhere, and every file taken out after. A file of 4 m cells measured 1.5 to 1.6 times an 8 m file for a painted family path or patch, 2.6 to 3.4 times for colour, and 2.9 (families) to 3.8 (colour) for speckled painting. Its review round's mends -- an undo of a stroke that added the palette's entry making the ground under its tile again, a repaint before the worker was ready no longer freezing the page over a cached far grid, Dathomir's family with brackets painted (and a refused write no longer leaving its entry to refuse every stroke after it), a real-elevation world's palette and base held to the pack really borrowed until the next arrival, a colour stroke's preview no longer laying colour already painted twice, the world row writing only the switches it sets (so a `__debug.ground` switch survives a travel), and the wash's waiting write dropped with its world -- were proven in node, never driven in a browser. **Families of other worlds (the third wave)**: the paint brush's family list on the Tools tab turns to any installed world (a row of its packs, this world's first, each pack's pictures read from its own list), and choosing one of another world's families lends it this world's ground behind a short curtain of the editor's (`App.editorCurtain`, Fold now's own screen factored out): its pack's family table and pictures read, the family copied into this world's families under an id of its own on both threads, its pictures put in the ground's one array beside the world's own, a texture swapped and no program built; it then paints at once, and every arrival after lends it behind the loading screen. The world row's `packs` lends whole packs at the next arrival (how a real-elevation world takes more than its base's families), up to the 256 layers the array holds, a family past them said and left out. A lent family is always its pack and its name. What it plants is this world's own layers' plants where no family filter asks for one of this world's families (its own world's plants are in its own terrain and do not come); the feet sound of its own surface and the air takes its own class. With no entry of another world and no `packs` nothing is read and the ground is the second wave's to the bit (held against ba83837 over every converted world). The editor's window now docks under the minimap's block while the minimap shows, and the editor stays on through any curtain of its own, where it had turned itself off and dropped its history at every Fold now. `__debug.groundPaint({ packs: true })` says what is lent, by pack, with its layers and the arrays' megabytes; `groundPaint({ choose: { pack, name } })` lends and chooses from the console. Measured (arithmetic of the textures' own sizes, 256 px layers, confirmed in a driven tab at two points): Tatooine's own ground 25.9 MB on the card; lending one pack whole (Naboo) 73.9 MB, three (Naboo, Yavin 4, Corellia) 135.9 MB, six 171.3 MB (the 256 layers full, 66 families left out); Monument on Lok's base 38.6 MB alone, 86.6, 148.6 and 171.3 MB (67 left out); a single family lent in play adds its two or three layers, 0.67 MB each. The page's own heap holds the arrays again without their mips (three keeps a data texture's bytes after it uploads them, half a megabyte a layer: Tatooine 19 MB alone, 55, 101.5 and 128 MB with one, three and six packs; arithmetic), and a lend in play holds two sets there until the old arrays are let go. A family lent whose pictures never reach the arrays lays nothing and is named, and a ruled real-elevation world whose world row's base names another pack than its rules borrow lends that family at the arrival and draws it as its base (the second wave laid nothing there); a pack no ground world gives is never asked for. Seen only in a headless tab of our own on Direct3D 11, in numbers and two screenshots in `A:\swg3js\mapmaker2\scratch\w3\`: on Tatooine a Naboo grass and a Yavin 4 leaf litter each lent in play in 0.7 to 0.8 s of curtain with no program built in play and no frame waiting on a link, painted, standing at the next arrival with nothing built, a Corellia family lent in a second session, undone and done again byte for byte; on Monument, Lok's base with Naboo and Yavin 4 lent whole at the arrival (166 layers, 111.2 MB), painted from both with no curtain, undone and done again byte for byte, and drawn at the next arrival; every file taken out after. **Painting the plants (the fourth wave)**: the paint brush's third layer, Plants, paints a recipe into 4 m cells of the paint file (`flo:` rows beside a `recipes` row): for each of trees, plants, grass and the far cards, keep, clear, remove a family, add a family at a density (the client's own add) or thin to a share, a feathered edge thinning out. The recipes are laid in the generator after its last layer, on both threads, over the world's own plants, whatever `follow` says (which says only whether the world's own plants follow the painted families), and a stroke's chunks are replanted as their ground lands; a road's flat part or a river's bed, a byte the generator now marks, is never painted unless a recipe says `roads`, and the scatter brush keeps off them too (`keepOffRoads`, on). Mustafar's baked trees take the recipe over the baked value. On a real-elevation world the world's own planter, grass and far cards plant all four classes from the paint alone out of the lent pack, on the ground as sculpted, its models loaded behind the editor's curtain in play and behind the loading screen at an arrival whose paint holds a flora cell; a chunk the paint does not reach keeps its procedural props. A recipe names only the world's own families (the lent pack's on a real-elevation world): another world's trees are the scatter brush's. With no flora row every world is the third wave's to the bit (held against 0a86da7: the four flora maps, the plantings, the grass and the far cards). The Tools tab builds a recipe class by class; `__debug.floraPaint()` says the recipes, the cells, what stands and what the paint's source answered. Measured in a headless tab of our own on Direct3D 11: about a thousand painted palms on Tatooine drew 60 to 70 world draws over none, the same trees by the scatter brush, folded, 176 to 316, every frame median within half a millisecond of none. Seen only in that tab, in numbers: on Yavin 4 a clear and a thin, on Tatooine shrubs and grass added beside Mos Eisley, on Monument a forest with grass on Lok's base, each undone to the file as it was and done again byte for byte, with nothing built in play; it found and mended a clear refused by the file's reader, the grass a real-elevation world made in play holding no chunk, and a flora stamp laying the families' preview for nothing. An undo of a flora tile replants its whole 512 m tile (the second wave's rule for any tile). Its review round's mends -- a real-elevation world's far cards made a few a frame and only where the paint reaches (a recipe moved or an undo had made the whole far ring's in one task, a tenth of a second and more), a recipe chosen while a stroke's answer was on its way kept (it had been dropped by the landing), a stamp's feather holding where two recipes meet, a real-elevation world's procedural rocks kept in a painted chunk wherever no recipe of trees or plants reaches (a dab of grass had taken every rock of its 64 m chunk), a paint file landing late on a real-elevation world standing its plants, and the Tools tab's family list drawn in the palette's colours -- were proven in node, never driven in a browser. How a recipe's density, a thin's share, the thickening and the feather feel are the owner's (sliders under `floraPaint`). **Lakes and falls (the fifth wave)**: the water tool (Z) puts a lake of ours on any world -- by clicks (an outline closed by a click by its first point, its level the lowest ground under its points, the first point's or a height typed), by a trace (the ground under a level flooded from a click at the ground's own 2 m, the shore walked and simplified to at most 256 points, said in words where it spills or floods nothing), or a fall between two levels (one row of the placements file: the game's own fall model scaled to the drop and 12 m wide, standing at its foot); a lake picked by its ring moves with the gizmo's ground square, F raises it, Delete takes it out, and its look (colour, opacity, ripple, drift, shader, name) is on the Selected tab. Each is one write and one undo into `public/placements/water/<world>.json`, which ships as the other edit files do, and stands at once in every reader of water as a client lake does: drawn and reflecting, swum in, walked and waded, a converted world's plantings taken out wherever it stands more than 0.3 m deep (`LAKE_TUNE.floraUnder`), the camera's look and the depth window under it, a building with rooms under it dry inside, and that building's doorways seen from inside under water through the underwater pass's exits variant (`underwater({ exits })`). A lava lake is drawn with the lava on a converted world and left out on a real-elevation one. **The walk grid follows the files when it is baked again** (the owner's answer: no run-time patch): `navgrid <world>` reads the world's heights file, its water file and its solid permanent rows (a row of a tree or a rock by the shapes the game stands it on, never its canopy's triangles), and `status` asks for it whenever one of them moves, or once the install comes to hold a model a row names; the old tutorial's world has had it, so its hall and Tansarii Point Station now stand in its grid. A lava lake moved, made water or taken out, and an undo or a redo of any of those, goes behind the editor's short curtain, since each makes the lava again. `__debug.lakes` reads and drives all of it, its numbers sliders under `lakes` and the editor's water tool. Seen only in a headless tab of our own on Direct3D 11, in numbers and screenshots (`A:\swg3js\mapmaker2\scratch\w5\`): on Helensar Spirit Lake traced from a seed at its shore in 1.1 s (205 points, 9.9 km²), written behind the editor's curtain as the session's first lake with nothing built in play and no frame held, raised 3 m in one write, a small player house sunk under it (listed as a roof; the body stood in its room dry 8.5 m under the surface, the exits variant drawing the water through its door), swum over, and laid again at the arrival after a reload with nothing built in play; on Tatooine a plateau raised 12 m with a lake on it, a lake at its foot and a fall between them; on Naboo a lake over a wood took six of nine trunks within 40 m and gave them back with its removal; and the plateau and the lower lake baked into Tatooine's grid sent a fighter round the plateau's side (none of 42 one-second samples on its flank or in the lake) where on the real grid it pressed along the flank for 23 samples and waded the lake's corner. Everything was undone and the real grid put back byte for byte. Its review round's mends -- an unmoved lava lake no longer stops burning when another lake lands, a lava lake's removal and every undo and redo of one go behind the curtain, a converted world whose pack is missing stands no lake of ours about the wrong centre, the frame that looks out of a building's doorway under water no longer parses its colour every frame, an outline under way in the water tool no longer makes anything a frame, and the grid's permanent tree rows stand by their trunks -- were proven in node and never driven in a browser. **Rivers (the sixth wave)**: the terrain's own wet rivers are drawn first, from their files (rivertest's straight and slanted test rivers and watertabletest's one, dry trenches before; no retail world has one that runs), as rows of the very shape a river of ours has, read by the water file's own reader and drawn by the same code; and the water tool's river mode (Z, A river) writes a river of ours into the water file by clicked points (each on its ground and `riverRise` over it, held level so it never runs uphill, `riverWidth` 8 m and `riverVelocity` 1 m/s), moved, raised, widened, slowed and taken out as a lake is, each change one write and one undo. A river is the client's ribbon quads down its spine, sloped along its points, and stands in every reader of water after the lakes: swum and waded, the rain stopping on it, the plants under it taken as under a lake, the camera's look naming its shader; each straight reach is a body of the lake's material with a wind of its own, so its detail runs downstream at its own speed and no program is built; with the volumetrics master on the moving air's liquid is pulled down it. A reach steeper than the slope limit (`RIVER_TUNE.slopeMax`, 12 degrees, the owner's slider; a river's own `fallFrom` wins) stays water and stands the game's own fall sprays and mists along its banks and at its foot, out of the props pack, scaled by its drop (Steep on the Tools tab, the default), or is cut out with a fall row of the placements file written there in the same action, or is cut out and said. The walk grid takes rivers as it takes lakes once `navgrid` is run again, the terrain's own too, which has been run on the two test worlds. `__debug.rivers` reads and drives it all, its numbers sliders under `rivers`. Seen only in a headless tab of our own on Direct3D 11, in numbers (`A:\swg3js\mapmaker2\scratch\w6\`): on Naboo a river of six points 300 m down a valley falling 2.7% between two lakes of ours, written with nothing built in play and no frame held, swum 74 m downstream (its surface falling 3.5 m, the body 1.1 m under it the whole way); with the volumetrics master on the liquid down its middle ran 0.74 m/s downstream against the river's 1.5 with the still water's swirl at nought (at the shipped swirl its eddies swamp the mean); a reach of 20.6 degrees stood 10 fall effects under the default, none past a 25-degree limit, was cut out with a fall row written beside it under `'fall'` and cut out and said under `'refuse'`; rivertest's two rivers and watertabletest's one stood behind their loading screens with their effects (40, 40 and 8) and were swum; and the river baked into a copy of Naboo's grid sent a fighter round the river's lower end where on the real grid it waded straight across. Everything was undone and the real grid put back by hash. The smoke found the editor's `rivers({ tune })` throwing on every shape change (mended, and pinned). Its review round's mends -- the fall effects stood again through one keeper, so two syncs that overlap (a slider dragged while an effect's file loads, a water file landing late) leave one set standing and not two; every steep stretch's foot stood before any bank's spray, where the cap had left half of rivertest's reaches with none; the effects' own sliders standing the effects again and nothing else, and the slope slider under the default keeping every river whose water did not move rather than hiding, replanting and remaking it at each tick -- were proven in node and never driven in a browser. Kashyyyk's particle falls, which the design named, are not stood along a steep reach: each is a sheet falling free for a sheer drop, whose particles would pass under the ground beside a sloped reach. **Looks and attachments (the seventh wave)**: with a row picked, the Selected tab lists its model's materials each with a little picture, and Tint (the dye picker's garment and creator colours), Picture from…, Material from… (the next surface clicked in the world, or a library entry chosen, whose materials are then offered) and Clear write that material's look on every picked row as one action (`mats` on the row; a borrowed material behind the editor's short curtain; a donor that is skinned, a leaf card onto a geometry with no card plane, or reads a second coordinate set the piece has not got refused in words); the library's new **Effects** (520, one a file name) and **Sounds** (398 placed loops) groups go in hand with no model, an effect shown at the aim, and a click hangs one on the row the aim meets in that row's own frame (`attach`) or stands it as a **mark** (`kind: 'mark'`, a row with no model), with a diamond or a ring (the sound's full-volume radius) for each, a mark's picking the mark, any other's picking that attachment for R, F and C held with the mouse, the arrows and Page Up/Down, and Delete; rows fold grouped by their model and their look, a copy of a material a source and look shared and counted, kept for the world once no row wears it (so a tier coming back builds no program) and forgotten by the world before it is disposed at the world's going, never a texture; and an attachment moved never takes its row down (`attachHash` beside the stand's hash), so a folded tree stays folded. Its review round's mends -- an effect sized by C held sized where it plays (it was placed again every frame, so the size never showed), a mark never solid, guarding, scaled, a story object or given a thing's fields, an older endpoint's refusal of a mark explained, the look picker's click in Fly no longer picking the donor row on its release, and nineteen holes in the tests filled -- were proven in node and never driven in a browser. Every row without either stands, hashes, folds, lands and batches as at a70487c (held against that commit's streamer, layer and reader out of git), and the library's light pass took the Effects and Sounds in (13,408 entries, 3.84 MB). Seen only in a headless tab of our own on Direct3D 11, in numbers: on Tatooine three crates put down, one tinted, one given a sign's picture out of the library, one a crate's material by a real click on another, a smoke and a sound hung on a crate and moved, a mark carrying a flame and a sound alone, a palm folded by Fold now with a smoke hung on it and moved (`editor().folded` 4 throughout), every look and attachment undone and done again with the file byte for byte, nothing built in play and no frame held, and the tree left as it was. Not built of it: no gizmo arrows on an attachment (its holds, nudges, fields and console move it), an effect's carried effects not sized with it, a look reaching a building's shell and never its rooms, the picker reading only what the world pass draws (a room's surface is taken from the library), and no sound heard in hand. **Places on the map (the eighth wave)**: the places tool (P) draws cities, places, landmarks, areas and zones of ours as circles (a click; C with the wheel sizes the next), rectangles (a drag) or outlines (clicks) into the world's locations file, `public/placements/locations/<world>.json`, which ships as the other edit files do; a place's ring picks it, its square moves it, C with the wheel sizes it, and the Selected tab writes its name, kind, whether it is drawn on the maps (a zone is not), whether its name is said walking in (a city's is), whether players may build in it, its tooltip and its loading lines, and copies it as a story area; every change one undo. A place drawn on the maps is named on the map window, the galaxy's card and the minimap, a city's outline is drawn on the minimap and its name said as the player walks in, and the Here tab lists them. **A place players may not build in** refuses a player's deed ghost, console house (the relay path's too) and prop in its own words, while the editor's own placing only notes it; the world row's switch (off by default) keeps players off the game's own cities, Core3's no-build areas and the client's no-build radii as well (on Tatooine 7 cities, 133 areas and 310 radii). A destination's loading screen draws one of the lines of the place of ours it stands in, else the world row's, else the planet's own description. The map window and the minimap draw the game's own services (Core3's map categories joined to the layouts by the `pois` command: 1,305 over the ten launch worlds) as marks of ours under the map's Services and Terminals switches (terminals off). A real-elevation world gets a map and a minimap made from its own raster in the browser, a few rows a frame and never committed, made again a couple of seconds after its ground, paint or water changes, and a click on a place of ours on such a world lands there at once. `__debug.locations` reads and drives all of it, its numbers sliders under `locations`; it needed `core3-reference` and `pois` run once, both done here. Seen only in a headless tab of our own on Direct3D 11, in numbers, with the full suite running beside it: on Tatooine a city of ours drawn as an outline near Bestine named on the galaxy's card, drawn on the minimap (one outline, twelve services) and said by the city line as the player walked in, its loading line its own; a no-build circle on open level ground 1.4 km from the arrival refusing a small house's ghost ("nobody may build in Quiet Flats") and a lawn chair ("nobody may put things down in Quiet Flats") that both stood there before it; the editor noting "inside Dune Town, where players may not build"; under the switch Bestine, a Core3 area and a rock spire's 20 m radius refused and open again with it off; Mos Eisley's minimap carrying 19 services and the map window 82 (248 terminals with Terminals on), none with Services off; on Monument the made map, 1024 px in 32 slices over 267 frames (the worst 2.2 ms), a landmark of ours on its card whose click landed the player on its exact spot, the picture made again 4.0 s after a lake of ours was poured and 4.5 s after a family was painted, and a teleport there from Naboo showing one loading screen with Monument's world row's line; the editor window's places tool armed from its Tools list, its place listed on the Here tab, picked and renamed from the Selected tab. Everything undone, `public/` as it was, nothing built in play by the wave's own drawing (all of it 2D; the two programs a Dune Town script built in play were traced in the review round to a wildlife lair's nest, `poi_lair_nest_aa7`, stood as the player was teleported 400 m off, in its world and rooms passes). Its review round's mends -- a city of ours players may build in no longer shut by the world row's switch, the minimap's outlines and services filtered again when a world state is taken (on Rori in the ruins it had drawn the living city's), a city on no map no longer outlined, the index no longer built at every ask on a world with no frame, the made map making no closure and parsing no colour a pixel, the loading line drawn for every arrival (the session's first world and a ticket's landing among them) by the screen's own moment, and the places tool's numbers and the services' switches as sliders -- were proven in node and by 36 mutations, and three driven in a headless tab of our own: on Rori in the ruins the minimap drew the late camp's four services within 800 m and none of the living city's 36, the session's first world showed its world row's line after a reload, and the Dune Town script built nothing of its own in play. Not built of it: the client's zone-map icons (they are only in the interface atlases the rules keep out: the marks are ours), a space zone's places (kept, drawn nowhere), a place's own picture on the loading screen, the sky, weather and grade a place may name (the ninth wave), and the client's own loading rows for the converted worlds. How the marks read, the made map's light and shade, and whether the world's rim should close under the switch are the owner's to judge (sliders under `locations`). **The buildings' own furniture (wave ILF, between the eighth and ninth)**: every building the worlds stand now holds the furniture the client stood in its rooms out of its interior layout files -- Mos Eisley's cantina its seats, tables, glasses and the band's instruments, the band playing in its room; Jabba's palace its pillows, rugs, small tables and bottles; the starports their chairs and crates; the guild halls, the houses, the dungeon copies and the gallery's walk-in buildings theirs -- 346,849 things over 924 buildings in 59 packs, from the light `interiors` pass, which `status` asks for with `props`, `ships`, `objeffects` and `sounds` after it (all run here). They are solid by their size with every seat and table passable, never stand twice where the world's own data stands the same thing, and a person the data stood inside one is stood at the nearest clear spot of the same room; the YT-1300 has its two passenger chairs in its cockpit room. A building the world editor puts down stands its template's furniture too, unless the Tools tab's "Furnish the buildings I place" is off or the Selected tab's Furnished is unticked (`furnish: false` on the row); a click on one of its pieces makes that copy's furniture rows of its own in one action, so editing it never touches another copy, and they go with the building however it is moved, turned, dropped with End, copied, cut, duplicated or deleted (O refuses such a building in words, since the hand holds one row; C leaves them as they stand, a building with rooms taking no size; and its Furnished cannot be ticked again while it holds rows of its own, which would stand every piece twice). Its review round's mends -- those carries, the make-own's effects (marks) carried with the rest, a patroller whose post was moved clear still walking its round, and the gallery's table held to SOE's final names list -- were proven in node and never driven in a browser. A player's deed house stands empty, as it always has. `__debug.ilf()` says what stands and `aimAt()` names a piece's key. Seen only in a headless tab of our own on Direct3D 11, in numbers: 231 pieces in and about the Mos Eisley cantina with eleven people seated on its chairs not drifting, 404 in Jabba's palace, 40 at the starport, three posts moved clear, none twice, the editor's copies, make-own, moves and deletes undone and done again byte for byte, and nothing built in play across two arrivals. Not built of it: the 28 files no template names (Bestine's cantinas and museum, Theed's hangar among them), the tutorial Falcon's layout, the 2,398 pieces whose room their building has not got, and editing the world's own buildings' furniture (their pieces carry keys for the removals by key to come); how any of it looks is the owner's to judge. **The sky, the grade and the weather per place (the ninth wave)**: a place of ours may name one of the environment families of the sky its world draws (the Selected tab's Its family, a choice of that sky's own families, or `locations({ set: { id, env } })`), whose light, fog, cloud sheets, weather effect, wind kind and ambience beds crossfade in over the weather's own four seconds as you walk in and out again as you leave; a weather kind (rain, dust storms or snow) forced over whatever family is drawn, at the world's own level, crossfading as the family does; and a colour-grade look (gloom, heat, warmth, cool, saturation, contrast, strength) laid over the planet's own field by field and eased in over `tune.gradeTau` (2 s), uniforms only. The smallest place naming each answers it; the world row names the same three under every place and gives the world its own climate (a weight a weather level), fog (all seven numbers of a fog look, so Monument fogs at all), wind (the unified wind's six multipliers) and cloud decks (cover, height, depth and density multipliers), on the Tools tab's box of JSON or through `locations({ worldSky })`. With no locations file, or rows naming none of these, the sky, the weather and its schedule, the grade, the fog, the wind and the decks are dd994e9's to the bit (held against its modules out of git). The README's "Clouds and weather on a world" says what a file can and cannot choose: the level falling stays the world's schedule's, nothing of a place crosses the wire, a place picks among its sky's own families, and the time of day, the sun and the lamps are the Light and shadow pass's (a row's `light` is kept for it). `locations({ sky: true })` says what the places say where you stand, what is drawn, how far the grade has eased a place's look in and the world row's tables in force; `wind()`'s `worldMultipliers` now name a world row's own. Seen only in a headless tab of our own on Direct3D 11, in numbers: on Tatooine a place of ours 700 m east of Bestine naming Mos Eisley's family and a warm, gloomy grade crossfaded Bestine's rows to Mos Eisley's at a quarter a second as the player walked in, Mos Eisley's ambience beds in and Bestine's out, the grade's weight 0.43 to 0.98 over six seconds and back toward nought as the player left; on Monument with the volumetrics master on a world row's climate, fog, wind and clouds stood in force at once (the high deck 1,200 to 2,160 m, its density 0.0715, the gusts 1.6), and the Selected tab's and the Tools tab's own fields wrote their rows; everything undone, the files gone, nothing built in play and no frame held. How a place's family looks against the world's own, how quickly a place's grade should come in, and whether a world's own fog, wind and clouds read right are the owner's to judge (`tune.gradeTau` under `locations` in the debug menu). **Your own models (the tenth wave)**: `npm run swg -- import <file.glb, a .gltf or a folder of them>` makes a model of the owner's own -- a Blender glTF at its exporter's defaults, or any tool's that keeps the authoring sheet's names (`tools/swg/README.md`) -- into a pack the game already reads, in `imports-private/` beside the checkout (git-ignored, served by the dev server alone) or, with `--ship`, in `public/imports/`, which every build carries once it is committed: every node's place, turn and size baked in and nothing mirrored, every material named for its import and single-sided unless its record says, a transmissive one a blended pane that casts nothing, lights, cameras, skins, shape keys and animations left out and said, Draco, meshopt, KTX2 and GPU instancing refused in words, its lower levels (`_LOD1` to `_LOD3`) at the record's distances, `-colonly` stand-ins that collide and are drawn nowhere, `-nocol` pieces drawn and never collided, a tree's or a rock's `collision-cyl`, `-box` and `-ball` shapes, its picture drawn by the converter, and a record beside each source (`<stem>.import.json`) keeping its id when the file is renamed, its kind, its collision rule, its look, the game's pictures it borrows by name and whether it may ship. In the editor the library's **Imported** source lists them with their pictures, and **Reload imports** stands every row of them again behind a short curtain after a run; a row names `pack: 'imported'` and stands out of the shipped folder where that holds the model, the private one otherwise, so shipping changes no row, folds into its tier at an arrival as any permanent row does, and a build from before skips it and names it. The walk grid's bake stands a solid imported row on its own triangles and is asked for again whenever the model is made again. `__debug.imports()` says the two folders, their models and which row stands out of which. Seen only in a headless tab of our own on Direct3D 11, in numbers and two screenshots (`A:\swg3js\mapmaker2\w10-shots\`): on Tatooine the TEST hut and plate (written by the test's own writer, never a model of the game's) in the Imported source with their pictures, put down with nothing built in play and no frame held, the hut solid at its stand-ins' 0.1 m floor and 3.2 m walls while its drawn 3.4 m roof let the ray through, folded by Fold now, Reload imports after both were made again fetching their new files with nothing built, and the plate's bump lit the glTF way (a dome) where `__debug.normals(1, -1)` drew it a dent; everything undone and the private folder emptied. How a model of his own looks beside the game's, whether three's own highlight (`look: 'pbr'`) or the client's from roughness (`'client'`) suits it, how big a stand-in should be and how much of a pane is drawn are his to judge (the command's own flags). Its review round's mends -- the suites that compare the placements files with the readers before the wave holding his imported rows rather than failing on them, a tint on one material of an import kept on its borrowed picture, a model made again under its id swaying and ruled a card by what it is now after Reload imports, a tree's shapes moving the walk grid's key, a model whose borrowed looks the game would drop whole refused in words, `look: 'client'` no longer dimmed by an occlusion packed in the metal-rough picture's red, an import's surface refused as a look's donor in words, the folders' sizes and the shipped note in `__debug.imports()`, and the dev server's imports middlewares opening only a file inside their folder -- were proven in node and never driven in a browser. Not built of it: a model's own effects and sounds, a look borrowed from one of his own models, and a model a release carries before it is shipped. **His own houses with rooms (the eleventh wave)**: a Blender house whose objects name its rooms (`cell:<n>:<name>` for each room and the shell, `portal:<a>:<b>` a flat polygon in each doorway and a `!` after it for a window, `floor:<n>` a room's walkable floor, `door:<style>` an empty naming one of the game's door styles, and point lights exported with Punctual Lights; the authoring sheet in `tools/swg/README.md`) is made by the same `import` command into a building with rooms the game already knows how to stand: walked into and out of room by room, seen through its doorways from outside and out of them from inside, its rooms lit by the room pool's eight lights from its own lamps (each at its own range from 14 to 40 m, the game's rule) and its own fill, a body indoors walking its floors, its doors standing in their doorways by the style named, sliding open for whoever comes to them and closing behind (a style's numbers are read from the player's own converted gallery, so with none its doorways stand open), dry inside under a lake of ours, and never scaled. What cannot be read is refused in words, and so is a room walked into with nothing of its own that collides; under the record's `collision: 'auto'` a `-colonly` stand-in is its own room's alone (the shell's for one under the shell), every other room colliding by its own triangles; a door object's own mesh is its marker and is left out. The pass's numbers are its flags (`--cell-pad=`, `--portal-flat=`, `--door-reach=`, `--light-reach=`, `--light-scale=`), and `__debug.imports({ rooms: '<model>' })` reads a house as the game does. Seen only in a headless tab of our own on Direct3D 11, in numbers: on Naboo the TEST house (written by the test's own writer, never a model of the game's) put down on open ground, walked into from the street a frame at a time (`cell()` naming the hall, then the parlour, then the hall again, then nothing), its door sliding open as the body came to it and shut again behind, the room pool lighting the hall's lamp and the parlour's through their doorway, and a lake of ours poured over it with the body standing dry in the hall and swimming the moment it walked out of the door; nothing built in play and no frame held; everything undone and the private folder emptied. Its review round's mends -- the stand-in rule room by room, a walked room with nothing solid refused, a doorway's first triangle never of no area, a door object's mesh left out -- were proven in node through the real streamer and physics and never driven in a browser. How his rooms look under the game's rule beside his lamps' look in Blender, how bright `--light-scale=` should make them and which way a door's leaf should slide are his to judge. **What the world places, taken away (the twelfth wave)**: the editor's World's own filter (Tools tab, `editor({ world: true })`) makes a click pick one of the world's own objects -- a building, a tree, a rock, a piece of a building's own furniture in that one copy -- and the Selected tab hides it for everyone (or with the ground its building flattened, from the next arrival), moves it (hidden, and a row of ours of its model at its place, picked for the gizmo, in one write) or copies it as a row, and switches whether it is walked through on that one thing, on this world's every copy of its model, or on every world's (the shared `solid.json`), with the client's own collision extent beside the switches where the packs carry one (flora's); the Here tab lists what is hidden round the camera, the world's standing people (Take away, Give back), the wildlife's sites, the seats of ours (Make permanent: a real chair or table row at its own place and turn that counts as the data's own) and the flora the client walked through. Everything goes into `public/placements/removed/<world>.json`, read by every browser at its arrival and landed live, one write and one undo a change: a hidden object stands nothing, collides with nothing, answers nothing and sounds nothing, takes its rooms' people, fittings and travel things with it, and a person or a site taken away never moves anybody else's `stood:` name or site. The library's People source puts one of his own people in hand, placed by the next click with the person dials' defaults (still, peaceful, back after 300 s) into `public/placements/people/<world>.json` and stood after everybody else under a name of its own, his own worlds included; `editor({ cast: true })` hands one over as a cast file's `at`. The no-spawn zone tool (armed from the Tools tab) draws a circle, a rectangle or an outline, or copies a place of ours, keeping the wildlife, the people and the people of ours out of it (no-spawn, never no-enter), its rim by the herd's own reach (`removals({ tune: { margin } })`). The walk grid follows a hide and a switch only once `navgrid` is baked again (the owner's answer: no run-time patch), which `status` asks for and the editor's bar says. `__debug.removals()` says all of it. Seen only in a headless tab of our own on Direct3D 11, in numbers: on Tatooine, Bestine's starport hidden live (245 things with it, its rooms' contents among them, four placed sounds taken up) with nothing under the crosshair and no collider on its line, and shown again; its walk-through switch on the thing and on this world's model leaving it drawn with the line through it, the thing's row winning over the model's; every world's switch put in `solid.json` and taken out with the file byte for byte as it was; a commoner taken away by key and given back; a Jawa squad's site taken away, given back, and kept off by a zone; a zone round the cantina keeping three people out; a streetlamp moved (hidden, and a row of its model at its place) and copied, each undone; one piece of the Lucky Despot's own furniture hidden in that one copy and shown again; a table of ours made permanent and the seats of ours then standing none there; and on Monument one of his own people placed beside the camera, standing at its mirrored place, and undone. No hide, switch or write built a program in play (the few built were people's own looks stood again and what the smoke's own teleports streamed in, as ever) or held a frame. How a hidden building's hole looks, how a moved one sits, whether the rim keeps a herd far enough off and how many people a zone should keep are his to judge. Its review round's mends -- a region tier the player walked away from no longer kept alive by the hide's records, a world row saying it is a seat walked through by the walk grid's bake and the editor's Solid as the game stands it, a follower kept across a landing of the people file going on as its own person, a zone put down live putting away the people of ours already standing in it, a building hidden taking its furniture's pieces that stand in no room, the walk grid asked for again only for a hide it can read, the whole world's models the client walked through listed on the Here tab with a switch each, a zone's Go to, and the zone tool's drag and the seat snap making nothing a frame -- were proven in node and never driven in a browser. **The editor's window and the Graphics page are laid out to be found** (wave UI, between the twelfth and the thirteenth): the Tools tab shows the Tools list across its top, a button a tool with its key, and below it only the armed tool's own settings and sliders, or with none armed the editing's own (snapping, the view, folding, the placing switches, the World's own filter), with the files, the walk grid's word and the camera's and window's sliders at its foot (`TOOL_SECTIONS`, one line a section); every section heading on the Tools, Selected and Here tabs folds, kept in this browser; and a tab drawn again or come back to stands where its scroll stood. The Graphics page is in six sub-tabs (Picture and effects, Shadows and distance, Flora, Water and weather, Space, Volumetrics; `GRAPHICS_TABS` and `GRAPHICS_TAB_OF`), the one open last kept, the Volumetrics sections folding (all but the master's start folded), and a find box filters every row by its label and hint across every sub-tab, Escape in it clearing it without shutting the menu. No setting, key, console helper, file or act changed (held against 90d089a's window and menu in node). Seen only in a headless tab of ours, in numbers and screenshots (`A:\swg3js\mapmaker2\ui-shots\`), never by the owner: whether the grouping, the sub-tabs' order and what starts folded suit him are his (each one table or one line). **Dressing his people (the thirteenth wave, the last of the pass)**: the editor's window has a Looks tab (no key) where a look is made on a figure of any species -- New (a bare body), Start from (a find over the catalogue's dressed people, whose look is copied), or Open (one of his own) -- dressed on the dresser's own Clothes and Shape and colours pages (the player's own two pages built afresh over the figure, with two tabs of their own: every garment by its picture, a piece the appearance table blocks for the species refused in words, a jacket put on over a shirt by the game's own arrangement rule, every slider, every colour through the dye picker), shown on a doll of its own context beside the tab's fields (its name, a note never shown, its species), and saved, saved as new or taken out, each one write and one undo, into `public/placements/looks/all.json`, one file every world shares, which ships with the build. Every browser merges the file into the creature catalogue at its boot as dressed bodies of their own (`own:<id>`, in a group of their own, each taking the size, moves, numbers, animation pack and flags of the dressed entry it was started from), so one of his own people (the library's Looks source, or `ownLooks({ wear })` on one placed already) or a story cast member (a cast file's `body`) stands in it, its look cached under its content's hash so an edited look is never served old; a look whose `from` this install lacks stands nobody, and one whose species pack is not converted stands its wearer in the `from`'s body, each named once. The light `library` pass draws each look's picture (its body and pieces at their bind pose, in the pieces' own colours) under that hash, which the Looks and People sources show; `status` names a look with no picture as it stands and never asks. With no looks file the catalogue, every look, the people, the cast and the player's own pages are c3d619d's (held in node by `Object.is`, and by a recorded session of the player's Clothes and appearance pages and the dye picker replayed through c3d619d's pages and today's). `__debug.ownLooks()` says all of it. Seen only in a headless tab of our own on Direct3D 11, in numbers and one picture (`A:\swg3js\mapmaker2\scratch\w13\`), never by the owner: near Anchorhead a look started from the moisture farmer, a jacket put on over its shirt, the jacket's colour and two eye sliders moved, saved, undone (the file gone) and done again byte for byte, worn by one of his own people placed there, who stood in it under its hash and again after a reload, the library's Looks and People sources showing its picture after the pass, the look opened again reading as saved; then the person and the look taken out and both files gone; no program built in play by any of it and no frame held. Two faults the smoke found and mended: with no equipment behind it the Clothes page took the shirt off for the jacket (its own group rule), and a piece's colour nothing held took a shared colour of the same name in the world's dressing (the eyes' onto the jacket), so the world dressed a look otherwise than the doll showed it. Its review round mended, in node and by mutation and never driven in a browser since: a body colour picked on the Shape and colours page (the eyes, the lips, the age, a beard's or a tattoo's colour, which no shared colour carries on any species) dropped from every saved look, the look keeping the colour it was started from; a piece another wardrobe folder lends under an id the figure's own wardrobe also holds (the human female's robe on a man, worn by 67 of the catalogue's dressed people) saved as the figure's own cut, colourless; a story cast member wearing an edited or deleted look kept the old body until the world unloaded; keys pressed on the dresser's own pages reaching the editor behind them (Delete took the picked rows out of their file); and `npm run story:check` warning of every cast member who wears a look. A relay that holds the story must be stopped and started before a cast file there names `own:<id>`, since it runs the cast reader it loaded at its start. How the doll is framed and lit, and how the looks read on his people, are his (sliders under `ownLooks`). **Map-maker II is complete** (waves 0 to 13). What each wave left is said beside it above; what is the owner's to see of the whole pass is every brush's and tool's feel, the lakes, rivers and falls, the places on the maps, a place's sky, his imported models and their rooms, a hidden building's hole, the zones' rims, and the dresser, every one a slider or a line. Not built: the outdoor walk grid follows the ground, the lakes, the rivers, the solid permanent rows and the removal file only once `navgrid <world>` is run again (a lake, a river or a cliff made in play is walked as the grid was until then), and it holds no plant painted or planted. How a fall reads (stood at its run's middle only its spray showed; at its foot its sheet is squashed to the drop), how a lake of ours looks against the world's own water, and whether a trace's metre over a raster's flat water floods too far are the owner's to judge, and so are how a river looks, how wide and fast one is drawn by default, where its slope limit belongs and how many and how big its fall effects stand (sliders under `rivers`). How the brushes feel -- their rate, their feather, how hard flatten and smooth bite, how fast a colour lays -- whether the wash and the anti-tiling switches look right, and how another world's family looks laid over this world's ground (it ranks above every family of this world's at a border, ours) are the owner's to judge. **The rooms' lights** (a wave after the pass, on the same branch; `A:\swg3js\mapmaker2\LIGHTS-notes.md`): the lights tool (**;**) marks every light of the building round the camera and those near it outdoors and hung on rows, picks one by its marker, moves, raises, turns, tips, nudges, duplicates and takes out a light of ours (each one write and one undo, the pool carrying it live), edits one of the game's own and the room's fill at a scope (this copy, every copy on this world, every copy everywhere), and puts presets down (the Tools tab, the library's Lights: a candle, a ceiling lamp, a desk spot, a cantina's warm fill, a failing tube, the room's sunlight) as lights of the room, outdoor marks that light nothing yet, or lights hung on a row; what it writes is `public/placements/lights/<world>.json` and the shared `public/placements/lights.json`, which ship as the other edit files do. Every browser lights its rooms by them: lights added (point, spot, directional) with their colour, brightness, reach, falloff, cone and a flicker (candle, pulse, strobe, broken) on the shared clock, and the Graphics page's Shadows and distance builds spot slots and casting slots (Room lamps, Room light shadows, both off), the pool built again behind a curtain; the rooms' global numbers (how bright a lamp, the ambient and its floor, the parallel light) and the flickers' are sliders under `roomLights`, and with no lights file every room is lit to the bit as before (held against 0e2e226's own lines over 6,989 rooms). Not built of it: an outdoor pool (outdoor lights are marks until the Light and shadow pass), a light's own shadow map size per light, and spot slots in the room's air beyond its glow (the air glows round a spot as round a point). Seen only in a headless tab of our own on Direct3D 11, in numbers and four screenshots (`A:\swg3js\mapmaker2\lights-shots\`): in Mos Eisley's cantina two lamps dimmed for that copy and one brightened for every copy, a candle flickering, moved, raised, duplicated and taken out with the file byte for byte through undo and redo, a ceiling lamp and a spot put down and casting after the pool was built behind its curtain (193 programs, 30 to 53 s on a cold profile), nothing built in play, and everything taken out after. Its review round's mends -- a casting lamp's map drawn by a shadow pass of the rooms' own (the sun's pass had drawn it, the street in it and the rooms only when that pass happened to show them, and its far plane stuck at the first reach), the parallel light's casting twin taken out (three's cascades lit it only in the near cascade's band), the occlusion counting a casting lamp as lit, a light moved, nudged or duplicated through a doorway written in the room it lands in, a game lamp's marker reading the narrowest edit as the pool does, the pool built again behind a travel's own screen rather than over it, and the frame's one read of the shared clock -- were proven in node and never driven in a browser. How bright the presets are, how a candle's and a tube's flicker read, and whether a casting room light is worth its cost are the owner's to judge.

**Map fixes** (on `claude/mapfix`, waves 1, 2 and 3, the whole pass; the design is `A:\swg3js\mapfix\DESIGN.md`, the waves' notes `A:\swg3js\mapfix\NOTES.md`). Built: the black smudge the owner found in Mos Eisley is gone -- it was the bombed house's blast decals (at `/loc 3331 -5001`), drawn hard and nearly black, solid, and shown in every pass across the street and through the floors of the Association Hall next door; every decal in the game (57 materials over eight packs) is now drawn the client's way, blended over what it lies on, casting nothing and never solid, and a thing bigger than its building is drawn only with its own rooms. The world builder's 109 no-flora rings are drawn and solid nowhere, the panes nobody sees cast no shadow, 61 props the layout already stood are not stood a second time, and lily pads float on the water. An arrival with no place of its own comes out on open ground clear of every building's own box (just outside Bestine's starport on Tatooine, outside Kaadara's on Naboo, outside Coronet's and Dearic's, and near every other world's centre; where no ground clear of every building is gentle enough, as at Kashyyyk's Kachirho, the gentlest dry ground clear of them, by a river outside its starport where it stood under the starport's deck); Echo Base's heroic arrives in its Main Hangar and the old tutorial in its hall's first room, among the tutorial's own 26 people and 7 of its 8 things (Core3's); E at Tansarii Point Station takes you into hangar bay 2, where the new players came in; Kashyyyk's zones carry the names of what they hold (the Kkowir Forest; Hracca and Bocctyyy; Blackscale Processing Camp and the Arena); going to space from a planet comes out at that world's own launch point beside its station, facing away from it (Core3's, for ten worlds, Rori's and Talus's in Naboo's and Corellia's zones); the ship menu's Land row offers a list of the places a station landed ships on the world below (29 over ten worlds, named for the town each stands in), a death after landing still coming round at the world's own arrival; a death at a cloning facility comes round in one of its own tubes (Core3's, 21 facilities); and `adventure1`'s centre is its busiest square rather than a starport sign. `__debug.aimAt()` names what is under the crosshair (its shader, whether it is a decal, its model, template, layout row, building and room, and what the physics meets along the same line), `__debug.decals()` tunes the decals' offset, and `arrival({ clear })` how open ground is looked for. The conversions it asked for were run on the owner's install: `core3-reference`, `fittings`, `spawns`, `space`, `adventure1`'s snapshot and nav grid, `objeffects`, `doors`, `conversations` and `library`. What the client's particle batches cannot do without a program or a batch of their own is keep a contained smoke or steam effect to its building's rooms (the bombed house's two are still drawn on the world's layers; NOTES.md says what it would take). **Wave 2 gave Kashyyyk and Mustafar their ways, from SOE's own data** (the owner's answer of 2026-10-31; the committed reference keeps them as names, numbers and places): each of Kashyyyk's zone gates lands where SOE's own door says, under one loading screen; a gate or a launcher into a grid zone sends the group (else the character) into its own copy of the slave camp, Hracca, Bocctyyy, the arena or the Rryatt Trail at that copy's start, and each copy is left by its own gate, or for Bocctyyy and the arena by E where it was come into; Kachirho's tree lift is E at any of its twelve doors, a menu of its four levels; the trail's 54 guides take you between its levels and back to its gate on E, the master guide to every level; a death on Kashyyyk offers the clone droid SOE's mapping names, your copy's own in a copy; Kachirho's starport and Mustafar's Mensix facility stand SOE's own terminal, collector and transport; and in a zone of copies the map and the minimap draw the picture of the copy you stand in over that copy's own ground. `__debug.ways()` says all of it where you stand. The conversions it asked for were run on the owner's install (`nge-reference`, `spawns`, `travel`, Mustafar's `pois`, `maps`, then `objeffects`, `conversations` and `library`). Not built of it: Mustafar's battlefields (the data names no way in), a way out for a character reloaded in the three building copies, and a group following through a gate was never driven; Kachirho's collector stands on the ground under a floor 3.8 m over it and its transport parks with its origin 1.85 m over the pad, SOE's own numbers, for the owner's eye. Its review round made a tree lift's ride and a guide's step keep the player's health and leave the spawn, stood a port's place on Mustafar (the map's teleport, the galaxy tab, a ticket put down on foot) on the deck rather than in the valley under the facility, held a way into or out of a copy aside after a blow and beside a companion lying down, kept the death card up when the clone droid's zone is not converted, brought the plain Respawn in a grid copy round at that copy's own start (a character reloaded in one included), and stopped `status` asking for `travel` again after one run with `--nge=none`; proven in node and by mutation, never driven in a browser. **Wave 3 stood what SOE's older publishes placed, the holidays' dressing and what the client walked through.** SOE's earlier objects are a world state of their own on ten worlds (Esc → **Gameplay** → The world → SOE's earlier objects, "As SOE left the worlds" by default, "Stand them again" the other; on a server it is the admin's word for everybody, as Restuss's is, and the relay must be restarted for it), taken at the next arrival: every older copy of a world's snapshot the install holds, read against the final one, 3,358 roots of which 1,343 are left out because they would stand inside or on top of something of today's world (or a newer earlier one, or a room's furniture), 1,964 objects standing in all -- Tatooine's 1,398 among them, its old Mos Eisley walls, pillars and debris, a cantina's old furniture -- on the final ground, never forced: 181 of those standing alone are buried by it and 91 left floating, measured and listed in the notes. The holidays' dressing stands from the game's own event tables, each behind a switch of its own beside Life Day's in the Weather section (In season, Always, Never, their seasons in the hint; a setting of this browser's, never a server's word): the Galactic Moon Festival's Halloween in Mos Eisley and Moenia (570 objects: lanterns, burning men, gravestones, dead trees, spiders, ghost fog), Life Day's trees (41, with the Life Day switch), Love Day's three and Empire Day's 73, the crash sites and ceremonies. And the owner's shared walk-through file, `public/placements/solid.json`, makes 80 templates walk-through wherever they are placed (Kashyyyk's ferns, plants, roots, mushrooms and wroshyr trunks, Echo Base's floor cables, 8,843 placements; the Rryatt Trail's level-four root path, on the lead's list, stays solid, since SOE's own people stand on it 139 to 165 m over the canyon and fell through it), the walk grid's bake reading the same file so the people walk through them too, and every waterfall's falling water is walked through while its rock stays solid. `__debug.earlier()`, `__debug.holidays()` and `__debug.solid()` say all of it. The conversions it asked for were run on the owner's install: ten worlds' snapshots, their places, placed sounds, travel, fittings, spawns, every world's walk grid (Kashyyyk's zones the most changed), objeffects, doors, scenes, conversations and library. Not built of it: the editor over the walk-through file (Map-maker II's), the holidays as a server's word, and a terrain layer for an earlier object (they stand on the final ground). A build from before this wave (main at 5ccc9dd) reading these packs takes the new keys at their blocks' own defaults, so on their own worlds the earlier objects and the holiday rows lie dormant, but it misreads three things: off Naboo it takes `soe` for a key nobody knows and lists Naboo's earlier shuttleport among the ports a terminal offers; with no `laterRows` it names Rori's heralds and First Brigade 25 places on and Lok's heralds 5, so on a server their bodies are shared under names this build does not give them; and its `status` keys a walk grid without the walk-through file, so it asks for the sixteen worlds the file reaches to be baked again and bakes them without it (the plants and the falling water solid to the people again). As a build before wave 1 refuses the fittings packs (version 2 since that wave) whole, any other checkout reading the same `assets-private` must have the pass merged first. Wave 1's review round mended a landing that left the spawn 700 m up, Fold now drawing the markers again, Kachirho's arrival under its starport, a fitting stood down by a thing put down in play, the launch facing its station, and `arrival({ go })` on a room arrival. Seen only in a headless tab of our own on Direct3D 11, never by the owner: wave 3's Halloween and earlier objects at Mos Eisley, a fern and two wroshyr trunks walked through on Kashyyyk and a tree wall and Kachirho's loom stopping the body, a boar wolf running straight through a fern in the Kkowir Forest to reach the player, the Rryatt Trail's webweavers standing on its level-four root path 137 m over the canyon, a Moenia waterfall's water and a Corellian geyser's falling water passed by the physics with the geyser's rock met, and the Rryatt Trail's level-one walkway holding a body dropped on it while a canopy piece let one fall through, in numbers and four pictures.

**The race drawer** (on `claude/mapmaker2`, waves 1 and 2 of 4, going to main together; the design is `A:\swg3js\racedrawer\DESIGN.md`, its notes `A:\swg3js\racedrawer\W1-notes.md` and `W2-notes.md`). Built, on the dev server alone: the world editor's **Course tool** (**'**, while the race pass is on; a space zone and the world of copies refuse it) draws a race course node by node on any ground world, a real-elevation one included -- a click a node at the land's height (or a drawn road's surface), **V** a curve or a straight for the edge being drawn, a click near the first node a circuit of three laps, near the last a sprint -- its road a spine through its nodes (centripetal curves joining straights tangentially, each node's height, width, bank and shoulder carried smoothly along it, never overshooting a node), a shortcut begun on a picked course's road joined to it there, and at its end too where it is finished on its road, and moved with it, and its gates placed for it (a start, one every 200 m and one at every sharp corner, as wide as the road and tilted with its bank) that follow the road as it is reshaped, each anchored to its stretch so a gate on an apex stays on it; in Edit a node's diamond is dragged, its width line's grabbers widen and bank it, **F** raises it, **R** banks it, **C** with the wheel steps its shoulder, a click on the road puts a node in and **Delete** takes one out; in Gates a click picks a gate and only picks it, one dragged, raised or turned stays where it was put, one widened keeps following the road at its own width, or it is taken out, **Place gates again** and **Follow the road** on the Tools tab; a gate-only track draws its course from its gates, asked twice (the first press says it will carve that world's ground once committed); the tool notes a full group's grid slots that stand off the road. Every change is one write into the world's track file, `public/tracks/<world>.json` (a `course` block written one node a line beside the gates), through the editor's batch and one undo of its history; the Gates view's save and take-out go through the same batch whether the editor is on or off (an action of its history while it is on), and a save whose track in hand follows a road the tool has drawn again since is refused in words; with no course in a file every track reads, hashes, races and keeps plants off exactly as before (held against afcf394 over the owner's own file). `__debug.course` drives all of it and the debug menu's `course` sliders hold its numbers of feel. **The second wave carves it into the ground, for every player once its file is committed** (the carve is the world's, whatever the race switch says): on any converted world and on the real-elevation ones its road stands at its own surface and its shoulders are feathered to the land, under the sculpt's edits; where it crosses itself or another course the lower road is carved through under the upper, with an abutment either side (or a cutting with `crossing: 'slope'`), and every other meeting blends with no step; it re-carves live as a node is dragged and a frame's share at a time after any change; trees, rocks and procedural props are kept off its road plus `clear`, the grass and far cards off its road alone where its course keeps its grass clear (the default), and the world's creatures, people and people of ours off its road plus `clear` (no-spawn, never no-enter); rows of ours on the ground are carried with it in the same action; **Fit to land** puts a path on the land, **Fit a bridge** stands the thing in hand as a permanent row over the crossing nearest the aim (along the upper road, scaled to the gap, its top on the deck, and says where an end stands off a road that climbs across the gap), **Clear old touch-ups** writes to nought the sculpt's deltas a moved road left on this world (`touchUps: 'clear'` does it with the move); the notes say each crossing and its clearance, a gap nothing spans, a bridge off the deck at its middle or either end, a hairpin carved through, how many of the people's posts and wild sites the road keeps away, and what of the world's and the players' stands on a road; a drag let go where it was grabbed carves the file's course back, and a track file that cannot be read keeps the courses standing; a pod tracks which road it is on through an underpass, its far ray reads a bridge ahead, and with `course({ tune: { deck: 'drawn' } })` the upper road's own deck is ground with no bridge at all. `course({ at: [x, z] })` answers in a build too. The walk grid follows a drawn course once `navgrid` is run for its world, which `status` asks for; no committed track file holds a course yet, so nothing is asked. Seen only in a headless tab of our own, in numbers, never by the owner: on Dantooine a figure eight's raised crossing carved with the lower road winning 10 m under the deck, its 16 colliders on the road gone, the grass's road mask laid, a node dragged in Point re-carving five times with no frame over the game's watch, a sculpt touch-up left standing by a moved node and named in the notes, a library bridge fitted over the crossing, the race switch off with the ground still carved, all of it undone byte for byte and nothing built in play, and a pod spawned and mounted on the figure eight's upper and lower roads, which the tab's own hand could not keep on the road, so racing under and over the crossing was proven in node only (`courseRide.test.ts`, the real drive, vehicle and Rapier at 20, 60 and 144 fps); on Helensar a 616 m sprint carved through its ground with the 12 rocks on its road gone, and undone. How the carve looks (its shoulders, abutments and blends), how a pod feels over a course, and whether a bridge from the library sits right are his to judge. **Wave 3 gave a track its walls** (uncommitted, `A:\swg3js\racedrawer\W3-notes.md`): the Course tool's **Walls** button draws walls of the track picked, free of its road -- a click a point, **V** an arc for the edge being drawn, a click by the last point or Finish the wall to end it, a point's diamond and an edge's middle dragged, **F** held a point's foot, **R** held the lean (to 60 degrees), **C** with the wheel the height, **Delete** a point -- written into the track's file beside its course, one write and one undo a change, and edited on the Selected tab. Only the race drive meets one, and by default only in a run on its own track (`course({ tune: { walls: 'racing' } })` any race-driven hull, `'off'` none): met square a pod crashes and is put back, met at a glance it scrapes along, and its look ahead stops at the wall; nothing else in the world sees one. `race({ tune: { aheadSteepCap } })` holds the look ahead level across steeper ground (90, off). Seen only in a headless tab of our own on Dantooine, in numbers: two walls drawn, armed, edited and undone, and a pod in a run driven square at a wall at 71 m/s that went down the step its front reached it and was put back 2.5 s later, with nothing built in play. How the walls read on the screen, how hard a wall should bite and where a wall belongs on a course are his. **Wave 3b gave a course its start, its following stretches, gates of any size and ground of its own** (uncommitted, `A:\swg3js\racedrawer\W3b-notes.md`): a gate's **Start here** (the Gates mode's Selected tab, `course({ start })`) numbers a circuit's gates again from it and lays the grid behind it, or takes out a sprint's gates before it, and **Finish here** a sprint's gates after it; a node's **Here the road follows the land** (and **Follow the land** / **Cut the ground** over a range from a marked node) leaves the ground as it stands there, its height the land's, easing into the cut by the spine's smooth step; a gate may be 2 km wide and 1 km tall (C's notch grows with it); the **Ground** mode puts down holes, mounds, ramps and trenches on the picked track's road (a click on the road, the handle dragged, F its depth or height, R its turn, C with the wheel its size, Delete), and the sculpt brush's **This track** switch strokes onto the picked course instead of the world's heights; both are kept in the track in the road's own frame and laid in the carve, so they move with the road and every reader of the ground and the drive meet them. Seen only in a headless tab of ours on Dantooine, in numbers: all of it written, a hole and a stroke moved with their road, undone byte for byte, nothing built in play. How the shapes read, how deep is fun and whether the drive's pit bridge gliding over a short hole is wanted are his. **Wave 4 gave race maps worlds of their own** (uncommitted, `A:\swg3js\racedrawer\W4-notes.md`): the Tools tab's **Worlds of yours** makes a **New world** (a size; a flat, test-noise or converted-raster ground; a converted pack's ground pictures with a base family and optionally a world's elevation rules; a sky in parts -- its colours from one converted sky or the procedural dome's own, its cloud layers from another or none, up to three suns and four moons pictured out of any converted sky -- a weather kind and climate, a water colour, gravity, arrival and name) or a **Copy** of the world standing (its converted terrain and buildings and nothing else), each one row of `public/placements/worlds/all.json` merged into the planet list at boot after the game's own (with no file the list is the game's to the object), reached by the galaxy tab's cards and `__debug.travel`, kept off the galaxy and the creator unless **Listed**; every edit file is named by `editKeyOf`, so a copy's edits are its own and nothing of an existing world is touched, and a copy's walk grid is its own (`navgrid <id>`, which `status` asks for). Seen only in a headless tab of our own on Direct3D 11, in numbers: a new flat world on Lok's ground with Tatooine's colours, Naboo's clouds, two suns and a moon made, travelled to and stood on at its flat height (a reload brought the character back to it), a short course drawn on it into its own track file and carved, then undone; a copy of Dantooine made and travelled to, from Dantooine and by a reload, its 478 placed objects standing and no people, mobiles, terminals, collectors or fittings, its sea and lakes drawn in the colour its row was given; a worlds edit undone and done again byte for byte, both worlds taken out and the file gone; nothing built in play and no frame waiting on a link. How a world of yours looks, its sky's parts together and a copy's emptiness are his to judge.

**Terrain to the horizon** (on `claude/mapmaker2`, waves 1 to 6b, then the rings switched on for everyone on 2026-11-09 by the owner's word: `farRings` true in the settings' defaults, `HORIZON_STATUS_ASKS` true so `status` and the launcher bake every offered world after `radial`, a world with no bake drawing the far tiles as before; and the landmarks' size rule a dial of its own, "Big things by size", collected behind a short curtain and remembered as `swg.landmarks`, still off by default; the design is `A:\swg3js\horizon\DESIGN.md`, the waves' notes `H1-notes.md`, `h2-NOTES.md`, `h3-NOTES.md`, `h3b-NOTES.md`, `h4-NOTES.md`, `h5-NOTES.md` and `h5b-NOTES.md` beside it). Built: the `horizon` command bakes a world's far ground once by hand (Tatooine, Naboo and Mustafar are baked here), and the Graphics page's **Far ground: rings to the horizon** (off by default until the pass's last wave) draws it as rings to the **Terrain distance** in place of the far tiles, changed in play behind a short loading screen either way with nothing built in play: the owner's heights file and the drawn courses laid over it at an arrival and again when a file lands late, the paint, a dropped building's ground and Life Day made again from the live generator, the far grids kept within two tiles for the far tree cards (standing on the drawn ring) and the race, and `heightIfCached` answering the bake past the blocks, so a far body and the editor's aim find ground kilometres out. Inside the page's **Ground detail distance** (2.5 km unless changed) the rings draw the near ground's own composition (its pictures and their alternates, the masks between them, the tint, the bumps and the gloss) and fade over its last half into each tile's mean, so the edge of the detailed ground no longer shows as a change of pattern; the program now takes Direct3D 11's sixteen samplers exactly, and draws there with no warning. `__debug.horizon()` reports it. Wave 3b gave the ground up close: steep faces (40 to 60 degrees, the start drawn exactly as before) take the pictures from the side as well as from above in the near ground and in the rings' near look, and within 6 m of the eye the near ground's pictures stand in relief from the height the terrain packs carry, both on and live through `__debug.ground({ tri, relief })` and the debug menu's `ground` sliders, with no measurable cost on either backend; and with the rings on, their shape out to the Graphics page's **Terrain shape detail** (0.7 km unless changed, which is also as far as it goes, the reach of the heights kept round the eye; 0 off) is the near ground's own 2 m heights, made by the live generator round the eye, on finer ring levels of 2 m and 4 m, lit by their own smooth lean and with the far tree cards standing on them, at about 330,000 triangles and 0.15 ms of selection on foot. Wave 4 has the rings follow the world editor's edits as they are made: a sculpt stroke's stamps, an undo and a landed heights file lay the far ground and the shape window under them at once, a course's change is laid nearest the eye a couple of milliseconds a frame, a paint stroke's tiles are made again on the rings within about a second of being let go, a lake landed stands the far cards again, and a copy of a world draws its source's bake with the copy's own edit files laid over it; nothing is built in play and a steady frame makes nothing. Its review round stood a course's far cards only once the far ground under them is carved, kept a held drag to one waiting piece a tile, laid a landed file's union tile by tile, and measured the frame's GPU time with a 200 m raise held on Direct3D 11 (4.09 to 4.22 ms against 4.44 to 4.50 still at the same view; W1's still (c) 4.25), the stroke 430 m out rather than 2 km since the editor's aim finds the ground only where its blocks are cached. Wave 5 took the rings to the horizon: with them on, the far plane, the sky, the far sea, the god rays and the flare follow the reach in force (with them off, today's numbers to the bit), the reach grows with the eye's height (`heightReach`, 4 m a metre: a ship 2 km up over Naboo sees 16 km where a body on foot sees 8), the horizon's edge is a switch of the sky's colour, the fog's or none, and the Graphics page's Flora section has **Far tree cards to the horizon** (off), drawn on the card from a side file beside the bake (`horizon --cards-only`, format 2 since the wave's review round: Tatooine's, Naboo's and Mustafar's are made again, and every other world's from before is refused as stale until it is), past the far tiles' cards and never inside the near ground's square, each on the ring as it is drawn there; status, the README's list and the launcher's words are built for the rings by default, and ask nothing while the switch stays at 'tiles'. Wave 5b keeps big things seen far away: with the landmarks' size rule on (off by default: `localStorage['swg.landmarks'] = '1'` at load, or `await __debug.landmarks({ on: true })` behind a short curtain), every placed thing out in the open with a half-span of 12 m or more is drawn at its lowest level of detail past its tier's own range, out to 400 of its half-spans and never past the reach in force (the rings', or the far tiles' 3.3 km), hidden wherever its own tier draws it; and a permanent row may carry `landmark` (seen from far off whatever its size) or `drawDistance` (metres, over the size rule both ways) from the world editor's Selected tab, which work with the rule off (a row in a building's rooms takes neither); the least half-span (12 m, never less) is a slider taken behind the same curtain (`await __debug.landmarks({ apply: true })`) or at the next arrival, never by a frame. Their models are loaded behind the arrival's loading screen (Tatooine 689 of them in 112 groups, about 6 s of loading of which 2.5 s holds the screen; Naboo 451) and nothing of them is built in play; at a view of Bestine 1.9 km off they cost a tenth to three tenths of a millisecond. Not built: real-elevation worlds (W6), a landmark's shadow (Light and shadow's), landmarks in space, the gallery and the copies, and the near ground's 2 m tint grain past its edge (the bake carries 8 m). Seen only in headless tabs of ours, in numbers and screenshots, never by the owner: whether the seam reads as one ground from the ground and from 500 m, and whether 2.5 km is the distance he wants, are his to judge, as are wave 3b's side pictures on a cliff, the relief on sand and the rings' shape at the handover, and wave 4's strokes and courses seen from far off while they are made, and wave 5's horizon edge, the reach from a ship and the far tree cards out to it, and wave 5b's landmarks: whether a town seen from far off reads right at its lowest level, whether 400 half-spans is the reach he wants, and whether the rule should ship on. Wave h6a ("finer shape far out", `h6a-NOTES.md`) built two options, measured so he chooses: a Graphics choice of the shape window, 1,024 samples a side (today's, the Terrain shape detail to 0.7 km) or 2,048 (to 1.55 km, made again in play behind a short screen), and a bake of 4 m (`horizon --step=4`, Tatooine and Naboo re-baked so on his install) whose own samples carry the 4 m level past the window to twice the Terrain shape detail and 8 m to twice that; with the default 8 m bake and 1,024 everything is as before. A 2 m whole-world bake is not built (about 340 MB on the card for a 16 km world). How each looks, and whether two and a half million triangles and a 1.3 ms selection at the 2,048 window's top with a 4 m bake are worth it, are his. **Wave h6b**: every ground world with no pack has rings now, made in the browser behind its arrival's screen from its raster or its recipe (no bake, nothing on disk): Monument and Helensar, the worlds of yours in all.json whatever they start from, and a raster not served on the planet's noise, with the world's lent ground's families and wash or, naming none, its palette; the heights, the carve, the paint, live edits, both windows and a paint's far cards follow as on a baked world. Measured in a headless tab of ours on Direct3D 11: Helensar's 24.6 km raster 0.76 s in the worker and 41 ms on the main thread, 63.5 MB on the card; a 32 km flat world 0.68 s, 86.7 MB (16 m, 2,177 a side); a 32 km test terrain on lent ground 4.0 s; a 16 km flat world on lent ground 1.5 s, 93.1 MB. Not built: a wash moved in play reaches the rings at the next arrival, and the far tree cards to the horizon are a bake's alone.
