Nothing of it has been seen by the owner. **The selection's room** (21 November, on `claude/rings-fix`, uncommitted on 8a7de51; `A:\swg3js\horizon-cull\north\fix\`): the owner's view at Kaadara with Finer levels on, the far settings at their tops and the Morph share dial at 0.6 selected about 34,300 patches against a room of 32,768, and the patches the walk came to last were dropped (the ground just ahead looking south, the farthest looking north, half of east and west); the room is now 163,840 (everything the offered settings and dials select all round), the roots are walked nearest first, and a room run out by a console tune is cut at one distance all round and said once in the console. Measured at his view on WebGPU: the card 3.1 to 3.5 ms either way, the selection 2.6 ms against 5.0 for the old room with the cut. His Mac's lines are not judged (the finer levels' seams meet exactly in the vertex stage run as script; the lesson names the one candidate left and what separates it).

**Three 0.187** (on `claude/three-187`, not yet on main; the second step of the port toward WebGPU, `A:\swg3js\port\STEP2.md`). Built: the game runs on three 0.187.0 with the WebGL renderer and draws what it drew on 0.185.1. Three's changed lighting, shadow lookups, environment filter and bloom blur are held to 0.185's (twelve holds, 0.185's `PMREMGenerator` carried, the bloom's blur material; the lesson above), three workarounds 0.187 made needless are gone, and `threeAnchors.test.ts` names every place the game matches three's text or reaches under it, so the next upgrade says what moved. Its review found one break every gate had passed: the water's mask twins still called three's `computeMultiscattering` as 0.185 declared it and compiled in no browser on 0.187, so with Effects on every lake and sea had lost its reflection; the call is mended, and the test now holds every function of three's that the game's shader text calls and puts each injected program together whole, the water's own among them (the lesson above). Measured only in headless Chrome of our own, the two builds side by side in one session (0.185 from main at 87280ee), never on the owner's machine. The picture, read off the effects' scene target on Direct3D 11 at fourteen kinds of view (a Mos Eisley street at noon, rooms, Theed and its lake, a Yavin 4 forest, Tatooine's far rings, Mustafar's lava, Tatooine's orbit, night with a lit saber, forced rain, the volumetrics master on, a world of imported models, the select screen, the ship edit page, the map) and at four of them again on Vulkan: every view differs between the builds by no more than two sessions of 0.185 differ, or by under one pixel in a hundred (the street 4.0% of pixels by more than two levels of 255 against 2.8% between two sessions of 0.185, all of it the cloud sheets, the figure, the lamps and the animated screens; a room 0.26% and 0.08%; the forest 0.44%; the orbit 0.73%). Water, whose reflection is the effects' own pass and so in no scene target, was held after the mend on the whole frame from above its surface, the pass drawing, in sessions started together: Theed's lake from its bank at noon, at a grazing angle, across it, under a low sun and at night differs between the builds over 0.16 to 0.87% of the frame and by more than two levels over 0.03 to 0.08% (mean under a hundredth of a level), with the volumetrics master on (the current variant and its twins) over 0.15% below the sky, and Corellia's swelling sea from its shore over 7 pixels of 1.3 million at noon and 0.68% under a low sun against 0.48% between two sessions of 0.185; on Vulkan three of the lake's views differ over 0.94 to 1.62% (0.10 to 0.38% by more than two levels, at shadow and geometry edges, none of it on the water). Every console self-test answers alike on both builds, every value within 0.2%, with reversed depth on and, for the grass's and the leaves' shadow tests, off; `fxCheck` is clean and no shader error stands in either build's console at every water view (before the mend 0.187's was not clean wherever water was drawn). The cost, in turns: the frame's median at Bestine, on a Mos Eisley street and in a room 19.6, 17.1 and 13.3 ms on 0.185 against 19.6, 17.0 and 13.1 on 0.187 on Direct3D 11, and 17.3, 18.2 and 14.5 against 17.6, 18.9 and 14.2 on Vulkan (each the mean of the builds in either of two tabs); the 0.2 ms more first read in the street's world pass on 0.187 did not hold up: measured again in review with the people out and each view waited on until its draw count stood still, the builds stand within about a millisecond of each other either way round at a town, a room and a forest on both backends (the world pass 6.51 against 6.45 ms on the street on Direct3D 11 and 6.59 against 6.79 on Vulkan), which is what two tabs differ by, and no steady cost of 0.187's was found; after the mend, over water, the frame's median is 6.5 against 6.6 ms at Theed's lake and 8.3 against 8.3 at Corellia's sea, the effects' pass on the graphics card 0.87 against 1.07 ms at the lake and 0.49 against 0.50 at the sea; an arrival's screen 20.7 against 20.1 s on Direct3D 11 with 114 programs each, and four arrivals 59.1 against 63.9 s on Vulkan and 58.9 against 56.8 s with the builds swapped between the tabs; a two-minute run round Bestine built two programs in play on both builds on both backends, with no frame over the link-wait line on Direct3D 11 and two on Vulkan on both; the light churn nought; the textures, geometries and the effects' 60 MB alike; and a sky capture still makes no new target (one made after more than a hundred and fifty captures). Not held, and so three's new look the day something uses it: sheen, iridescence, anisotropy, transmission, light probes, area lights, diffuse roughness, and a Lambert or Phong material under an environment. Held though it was 0.185's own fault: the sun's shadow bias added under reversed depth. Nothing of it has been seen by eye: whether any surface, shadow edge, reflection or glow looks other than it did is the owner's to judge.

**WebGPU behind a switch** (on `claude/three-187`, not yet on main; the GPU port's third step, `A:\swg3js\port\STEP3.md`, both makers' halves: the switch, WebGL kept, the game on WebGPU, the stand-ins, the portal passes, the sun's cascades, the console and the previews; then the warm-up, the region tiers' render bundles and the four private patches of three's; then a fix round). Built: `?renderer=webgpu` (kept on the address) or `localStorage['swg.renderer'] = 'webgpu'` and a reload draws the game with three's `WebGPURenderer` on its WebGPU backend, and where WebGPU cannot start the game draws on WebGL and says so once on the message line. WebGL was the default until 2026-11-18 (WebGPU is now, `?renderer=webgl` the old one) and is unchanged: its boot loads nothing of the port but the switch (751 of the game's modules, none of them WebGPU's), and on a Mos Eisley street, its cantina and Theed's lake every program it built is one the build before built, source for source with the material's name taken out, but for a passing variant of a material both builds draw (the fix round's two passes over the route: 397 of 398 alike each way, a variant of an antenna's program in this build alone and of a field of points' in the one before, and the variants of both held at the end alike), with no shader error and `fxCheck` clean. On WebGPU every classic material draws through three's own node copy of it, with none of our injections (the client's highlight, the wet look, the detail maps, the leaf cards' light, the sway); the sky dome, the lava and the ground's tile program are plain stand-ins (a gradient from the hour's horizon colour to its zenith, the ramp's middle colour, the colour map's tint over a grey; dials in `GPU_LOOK`, `__debug.gpu({ look })`); the doorways' depth reset is its own stand-in, so a room and the street through its door draw as on WebGL, a room seen through its door from the street included (by day such a room is unlit on both renderers, the rooms' pool lighting only the building the camera is in, and WebGL's sky reflection on a metal cabinet is all that shows there, which WebGPU, capturing no reflection this step, leaves black; by the torch at night it is the same room on both, `s3-fix-door-torch-gpu.png` and `-gl.png`); the sun's three cascades are three's `CSMShadowNode` at the game's own breaks, distance, bias and softness, and a room's lamps cast when the setting asks; the water is drawn flat (its simulation and its reflection held off); the plain dome of a world with no converted sky stands in as one colour, the fog's; and every other `ShaderMaterial` draws nothing and is named by `__debug.gpu()`, by its own name or its first uniforms where it has none: every particle effect, every lit lightsaber blade, the cloud sheets, the stars, the grass, the marks, the room motes, and in space the nebulae. Held off and said so: the effects chain, the far rings, the volumetrics, the shadow hold and batches, the leaf skip. The previews (the doll, the select screen, the ship edit page, the map's pictures) still draw on WebGL in contexts of their own. Every pipeline a loading screen's sweep, a body made ready or a tier streaming in would need is listed by three's own walk, every sun cascade's included, and built off the frame (`gpu({ warm })`); the region tiers' still instanced meshes draw from one render bundle a tier (about 1,250 draws to 510 on the Mos Eisley street, `gpu({ bundles })`), recorded again when a mesh's copies, its material or its pictures change; a still caster's shadow program is not refreshed in every cascade every frame (`gpu({ patches: { shadow } })`); and the game's dynamic-draw hint is dropped, so what is rewritten in play is written to the GPU when it changes (`gpu({ dynamicHint })`). The fix round found that nothing rewritten in play had been reaching the GPU after its first frame (a level group's copies, the flora batches, a folded row hidden in place: the lesson above) and mended it. Measured in headless tabs of our own on the RTX 5080, the two renderers in turns in one session (the idle tab frozen, three turns, absolute times wandering by half again between runs on that machine, so only turns compare): on the Mos Eisley street WebGL's frame median 7.9 to 9.7 ms and its render calls 4.7 to 5.3 ms against WebGPU's 11.1 to 12.7 and 10.1 to 10.4 (draws 660 to 820 against 510 to 530), WebGPU with its bundles off 15.8 to 18.4 ms of render calls, its shadow patch off 9.9 to 12.3, its still and warm patches off within the noise (8.3 to 10.9; the still patch tells nothing this step and the warm one changes only compiles), its stencil patch off (the bundles held off with it) 15.1 to 16.7, and the dynamic hint kept 16.7 to 20.8; on Bestine's square WebGL's render calls 6.0 to 7.0 ms against WebGPU's 6.3 to 10.2, bundles off 10.8 to 13.6, hint kept 13.4 to 14.8. After an arrival on a profile that had been there before, a two-minute walk of teleports along the Mos Eisley street made no pipeline at a draw on WebGPU (677 made ahead of their draws, 8.3 s of the main thread's node building spread over it; frame median 9.0 ms, p99 37, 33 frames over 50 ms, worst 241) where WebGL built 22 programs in play (median 9.0, p99 26, 22 frames over 50 ms, worst 135); a first arrival on a cold profile waits minutes behind its screen (pipelines land about one a second); still made at a draw in play, now and then (none to thirteen over a session of every view below): the flora's batches and a held weapon the warm-up is never handed, a piece of a building's furniture drawn in a room's pass it was warmed only for the world's, and on reaching Tatooine's orbit by `travel` dozens more (66 once, the sky's bodies warmed only for the rooms' pass). The patch telling a still thing's program it carries no node tells nothing yet (every classic material's node copy carries none). Measured only in headless Chrome of our own on an RTX 5080 (Chrome's own WebGPU, Direct3D 12): the select screen, a Mos Eisley street (about 400 to 1,000 draws and 0.35 to 1 million triangles a frame), the cantina from inside, a player house's room seen through its door from the street, Bestine, a landspeeder ridden 265 m, an X-wing taken to Tatooine's orbit by `travel`, flown there at 650 km/h and brought down by the ship menu's land, Theed's lake, a Yavin 4 forest, Mustafar's lava, a death and coming round, the ship edit page, the map's two pictures and the Graphics page, with no uncaught error and no WebGPU error; screenshots `A:\swg3js\port\shots\s3-*.png`. The console's warnings on WebGPU alone: three's "Vertex attribute uv not found" twice on reaching a space zone (the space dust's points: WebGPU draws a point a pixel and finds no coordinate to sample its picture by, and the stars a pixel too), Chrome's "Draw with an index count of 0" once (a far tile whose cut keeps no quad: three's WebGPU submits the empty draw WebGL skips), and Chrome's note that it ignores `powerPreference` on Windows; the X4122 warnings of the ship edit page's WebGL preview and three's PropertyBinding warnings in the cantina come on WebGL alike. The picture is plainer than WebGL's (no grade, no effects, no client highlight, no sky reflections) and Mustafar much redder. Not built: the effects, the far rings, the volumetrics, the water's look, the grass, the marks and every injected look on WebGPU (later steps, and the remakes that are the owner's). Nothing of it has been seen by the owner; whether WebGPU becomes the game's renderer is his to decide in his visible Chrome.

**The models' own looks on WebGPU** (on `claude/three-187`, not yet on main; the GPU port's fourth step, `A:\swg3js\port\STEP4.md`, its first maker's half: the client's highlight, the reflections and the detail maps). Built: on WebGPU every converted model draws the client's own highlight (each shader's mode, colour, power and mask, the band, the cap, the gate and `spec({ use: 'three' })` live), our sky's reflection on every registered material (captured every four seconds into one kept cube by three's WebGPU generator, the black stand-in while a world has none, the 'game' source through a generator of its own) and the detail maps on the second coordinate set; each is on, `__debug.gpu({ look: { spec, env, detail } })` takes it off live and `localStorage['swg.gpu.spec']` (or `env`, `detail`) `= '0'` with a reload leaves it out at load. WebGL is unchanged: over a Mos Eisley street, its cantina and Theed's lake every program the build before built is one this build built. Step 3's stand-ins read their colours and dials in the shared render group now, which mends every WebGPU reflection being the first capture's sky. `__debug.specSelfTest()` on WebGPU draws the client's term within 0.003 of the mirror, and the switch to three's GGX and the look switch exactly, building no pipeline. Measured only in headless tabs of our own on the RTX 5080, picture pairs against WebGL at the same views with each look on and off (a house wall, a block's wall with a detail map, a hull with a reflection cube at Mos Eisley and at Theed): over the pixels each look moves, the look on stands nearer WebGL's picture on 69 to 89% of them for the detail maps, 59 to 76% for the reflection and 32 to 65% for the highlight (three 0.187's diffuse and its own filter of the environment are not 0.185's, and the sky captured is step 3's stand-in); the doll drew with no error. Not built: the wet film's weather cases (the wetness is a remake of the owner's), the 'scene' comparison of the dome, the water's reflection, the previews' own studio (still WebGL), and the rest of the step (the second maker's). Nothing of it has been seen by the owner.

**The leaf cards on WebGPU, what the node copies carry, and nothing built in play** (on `claude/three-187`, not yet on main; the GPU port's fourth step, its second maker's half). Built: on WebGPU every leaf card draws the client's own light (the ramp about the sun's axis, the ambient alone for its indirect light, the emissive and the fills, carried back through the inverse of three's tone curve before three's own tone mapping, which on WebGPU comes after it in three's output pass), the sun's shadow taken from the cascades' own lookup and fade to lower its front row, the stored mirrored backs, `pointFlat` under a point light, the rooms' pass putting the card light out for its own draws, a blended card's alpha raised by its mip level and its cut where three makes it, and the leaf skip (the lookup moved toward the sun by the ray's way out of its own crown, and the acne floor on every cascade's bias); `__debug.gpu({ look: { card: false } })` takes it off live and `localStorage['swg.gpu.card'] = '0'` with a reload leaves it out at load, which also holds the leaf skip off. `__debug.cardLightSelfTest()` and `leafShadowSelfTest()` run on WebGPU through the node graph against `cardLightMath.ts` and `leafShadow.ts` (within a tenth of a byte, the skip's three cases as WebGL's). `__debug.gpu()` says what stays as step 3 left it (`stays`) and `gpu({ carries: true })` checks what three's node copies carry of the scene's materials (the person keys, the emissive splits, unlit, decals, flip-books and scrolls, the stencil reference: all carried wherever met, at Mos Eisley, Theed and a Naboo forest). The flora stand-ins are built as the batches draw, receiving the sun's shadow, so a species first met in play no longer builds its pipeline at a draw. Measured only in headless tabs of our own on the RTX 5080 (Direct3D 12): WebGL's programs over a Mos Eisley street, its cantina and Theed's lake are 1446f2a's (every one of its programs built, one more each run that a plain material compiled before its cascades' adoption accounts for, and that run's base built the first run's extra itself), with no shader error and `fxCheck()` clean; at a Naboo forest view the card part on stands nearer WebGL's picture on 86% of the pixels it moves; two-minute walks after a warm arrival made one pipeline at a draw at Mos Eisley (a pistol a person drew), none at Theed and three in a Naboo forest (a ShaderMaterial's stand-in and a blaster bolt's two materials), none of them a card's; switching each look off moved neither the frame nor the render call's time beyond the noise. Not built: a held weapon drawn in play, a bolt's colour and a ShaderMaterial's stand-in still build at their first draw (step 3's warm-up never sees them); the flora's sway and wetness are still the owner's remakes, so no card sways on WebGPU; and a long session that had travelled once drew no near flora batch at a Naboo view, which a fresh session at the same view did and which is not understood. Nothing of it has been seen by the owner.

**The fourth step's fix round** (on `claude/three-187`, not yet on main). The review's brighter sunlit cards on WebGPU are WebGL's fault, not the port's: with reversed depth WebGL's card shadow lookup is turned round (its offset, acne floor and skip all push away from the sun, and its own `leafShadowSelfTest()` fails there), while WebGPU's matches WebGL's with `?norevz` to within 0.7 of a level on average over a Naboo forest's cards; it is not mended here, since the mend is a WebGL line. On WebGPU the client's highlight now gives back the diffuse three 0.187 cut for its own highlight, so a Theed room's floor under its lamps draws 57.5 where WebGL draws 57.4 (55.6 before); the warm-up's flora stand-ins cast as well as receive, so a teleport across Naboo built no flora shadow pipeline at a draw where the review saw two; `specSelfTest()` gained a point light on black and on grey and an ambient light (worst 0.0015 of a white surface), `leafShadowSelfTest()` the same three cases through three's real cascades (111.4/148.8, 148.8/148.8, 111.4/148.8, as the plain stage), both refuse in words with their part left out at load, and every self-test's pipelines are counted apart (`forSelfTests`) and never as made in play; and a pipeline a draw makes in play now says the pass, scene and lights it was drawn in. Not mended: people's clothes built at a draw in a building's rooms pass while a town settles after a teleport (eleven pieces at Mos Eisley, all clones of looks the warm-up never listed, drawn in the rooms' proxy scene: the review's stormtrooper case, now named by its pass); a weapon a person draws, a bolt's or a basic material's first draw, which step 3's warm-up never sees. Measured only in headless tabs of our own on the RTX 5080 (Direct3D 12 and ANGLE Direct3D 11). Nothing of it has been seen by the owner.

**The ground and the far rings on WebGPU** (on `claude/three-187`, not yet on main; the GPU port's fifth step, `A:\swg3js\port\STEP5.md`, its first maker's half: items 1 and 2). Built: on WebGPU the near ground is drawn by the tile program's own composition (every family's picture in its corner's alternate, the client's masks, the colour map's tint in its four modes, the bump and the gloss, the flat shine, the cloud shadows, the steep faces' side pictures and the relief up close) and the version-3 ground by its own, both on unless `localStorage['swg.gpu.ground'] = '0'` with a reload, `__debug.gpu({ look: { ground: false } })` putting step 3's plain ground back live; and the far rings are drawn at 'rings' (the Graphics page's switch, now offered on WebGPU) with the bake's heights, the shape window's 2 m heights, the near composition inside the ground detail distance and the albedo past it, the composition and tint windows, the horizon fade, the fog's cheap path and every console view, unless `localStorage['swg.gpu.rings'] = '0'`. `__debug.groundSelfTest()` on WebGPU holds the program to `groundMath.ts` within 0.0047 on all eleven runs with nothing built on a knob, and `__debug.horizon({ selfTest: true })` holds the layer means to the card's top mip, counts the ring's samplers off its WGSL (2 in the vertex stage, 15 of 16 in the fragment, 14 in one Naboo session) and holds its level, edge, morph, reach, drop and height arithmetic to `horizonMath.ts` on the GPU (worst 3.4e-7), its whole vertex stage over 330 patch and skirt vertices to `HORIZON_RING_VERTEX` written out (worst 3e-5 m; a 2% skew of the ring's z placement fails it by 4 m) and the bake's and the shape window's heights to the CPU's (0.0011 to 0.0023 m and 0.0001 m). WebGL is 28fdb7c's: over Mos Eisley's street, its cantina, Theed and Mustafar's lava 404 of 28fdb7c's 405 program sources are this build's (the eighteen others named for the props and people each run stood, the ground's and the ring's among the 404), with no shader error and the effects clean. Measured only in headless tabs of our own on the RTX 5080 on Direct3D 12 against WebGL on ANGLE Direct3D 11: Mos Eisley's street differs from WebGL by 0.45 of a level on average, every one of `__debug.ground`'s switches moves both renderers alike (the shine 0.88 against 1.08, the mirror 12.3 against 11.8, the client's tint 62.3 against 62.1, the channels 8.3 against 8.1, the opposite rule, the masks, the order, the side pictures and the relief within a few tenths), and the rings' own pixels behind Mos Eisley differ by 2.9 of a level on average. Not built: the wet ground and the snow on WebGPU (the ground draws dry), the far tree cards to the horizon, and the rest of the step (the second maker's: the warm-up's hand-over of these graphs, the walks and the cost). Mended in the step's fix round: from 500 m over Naboo the rings on WebGPU drew a thin dashed black line along every ridge past the fine extent where WebGL drew none, a NaN normal from the flat face taken inside a branch (the lesson above); it is gone, and the ridges there differ from WebGL by 2.3 levels on average. Nothing of it has been seen by the owner.

**The lava, the marks, the blades, the particles and the tunnel on WebGPU** (on `claude/three-187`, not yet on main; the GPU port's fifth step, `A:\swg3js\port\STEP5.md`, its second maker's half: items 3 to 7). Built: on WebGPU the client's lava (the noise volume, the ramp, the crust and the glow, three's own fog), the marks (both sheets), the lightsaber ribbons, the client's particles with their ribbons, flip-books and solid quads, the weather's falling effects in their own variant, and the hyperspace tunnel are each drawn by a node graph of its own (`src/core/gpu/gpuPrograms.ts`) that reads the drawn material's own uniforms at every draw, each on unless `localStorage['swg.gpu.lava']` (or `marks`, `blades`, `particles`, `tunnel`) `= '0'` with a reload; `__debug.gpu()` says each graph's count (`programs`) and what the port leaves for the owner's remakes and step 6 (`notPorted`: the sky, water, clouds, volumetrics, wetness, sway, grass and the far tree cards, Force lightning and every Force power's own effects, the vehicles' dust, the rooms' motes and beams, the previews' depth of field and every effect pass). No other `ShaderMaterial` the game draws is outside that list. On WebGPU alone: a modulate particle batch blends by the client's own rule (three refuses the multiply it was given on both renderers; WebGL's is left as it stands), a batch of the world's carries the portal renderer's stencil from its making, the weather has twins of its warm batches in both of its stencils, and the noise volume goes up with no mip chain (three's WebGPU cannot build one for a 3D texture). WebGL is 28fdb7c's: over Mos Eisley's street, its cantina, Theed and Mustafar's lava 415 of 28fdb7c's 416 program sources are this build's (the hyperspace tunnel's and the lava's among them; the base's other a second variant of the heat haze's lava program differing only by `HAS_NORMAL`, for a lava mesh that run met; this build's two others a point cloud and a prop its run stood), with no shader error and the effects clean. Measured only in headless tabs of our own on the RTX 5080 on Direct3D 12 against WebGL on ANGLE Direct3D 11, the effects off: two-minute walks after a warm arrival -- Mos Eisley's street, a Naboo forest, Theed, Mustafar's lava, a lit blade swung, the DL-44 fired at fighters, and hyperspace jumps across Tatooine's orbit -- made no pipeline at a draw for any of the step's own programs (0 on the street, Theed, the lava, the blade and the jumps; the forest's 2 and the fight's 3 were basic materials, a room's clothes and an invisible collider, step 3's and step 4's known gaps); the marks differ from WebGL by 2.0 levels on average; the lava draws its noise, ramp and crust (32.6 on average against a WebGL frame of another moment of the flow), and its glow and brightness switches move both renderers alike (11.7 against 11.0, 15.8 against 19.9); the tunnel's glow switch moves both alike (30.5 against 30.4). Each item's cost, read in turns off WebGPU's own timestamp queries (`renderer.backend.trackTimestamp`, the median render-pass time of 40 frames hidden and shown, six rounds, 1584 by 805), where the frame's pacing showed nothing: the near ground 0.08 ms and the rings 0.15 ms on Mos Eisley's street, a brazier's fire and smoke 0.08 ms, the marks, a lit blade, the lava and the tunnel each within 0.01 ms of nought (the lava costs no more than the ground it covers, the tunnel no more than the world it hides). The fix round took the lava's crust and ramp samples out of the crust's branch (the lesson above). Not mended: an additive fire blends in linear light on WebGPU, where WebGL tone-maps each quad and adds the shown values, so a brazier's fire draws much fainter on WebGPU than on WebGL, which no graph can carry; a blue blade's glow drifts violet through the inverse of three's tone curve; and two `THREE.AttributeNode: Vertex attribute "uv" not found` warnings, met in Mos Eisley and in space, come of no graph of this step's (every geometry they draw carries its `uv`) and were not traced. Nothing of it has been seen by the owner.

**The effects chain's bones, the bloom and the colour grade on WebGPU** (on `claude/three-187`, not yet on main; the GPU port's sixth step, `A:\swg3js\port\STEP6.md`, its first maker's half: items 1 to 3). Built: on WebGPU with Effects on the scene is drawn into the chain's own linear half-float target, the sanitize zeroes what is not a number and holds the brightest at 256, the bloom (three's pass with 0.185's blur, five levels) and the colour grade (the planet's look, the hour, the rooms' share) are the WebGL passes' own objects each drawn by node graphs over their own uniforms, and three's output pass tone-maps at the game's exposure and encodes sRGB; the Effects setting, the bloom's strength and the grade's knobs move live, and Effects switched in play goes behind the WebGL path's own notice and builds nothing at its first frame; with Effects off the scene goes to the canvas as step 5 left it. `localStorage['swg.gpu.chain']` (or `bloom`, `grade`) `= '0'` with a reload leaves the chain, or one pass, out at load, and `__debug.gpu().chain` says what is drawn, what each graph binds and the switches. Every warm-up compile on WebGPU is now made with the target's own depth and stencil (`WARM_DEPTH`, the lesson above). WebGL is 8d0c759's: over Mos Eisley's street, its cantina, Theed and Mustafar's lava every one of 8d0c759's 414 program sources is this build's, this build's three others a point cloud and two Theed surfaces its run met, with no shader error and `fxCheck` clean on both. Not built: the ambient occlusion and the fire (the step's second maker), and no other effect pass is on WebGPU (the blade glow and its shadows, the god rays, the motion blur, FXAA, the depth of field, the light shafts, the water's reflections, the heat haze, the lens flare and the grain and vignette, each a design session of its own); with Effects on nothing smooths the edges on WebGPU (no FXAA, the scene target not multisampled), where with Effects off three's frame-buffer target is multisampled four times. Measured only in headless tabs of our own on the RTX 5080 (WebGPU on Direct3D 12 beside WebGL on ANGLE Direct3D 11, 1584 by 805), in numbers and screenshots (`A:\swg3js\port\shots\s6-*.png`): the bloom drawn by both renderers over one picture differs by at most one half-float step (0.001 at strength 0.35, 0.004 at 1.5); `gradeSelfTest` 0.00094 on WebGPU against the mirror, 0.00095 on WebGL; picture pairs of the lower half of Mos Eisley's street at dusk, the bloom's own change 3.69 on WebGL and 3.62 on WebGPU and the grade's 9.92 and 9.90 (parted by 0.5 and 0.6, each pass toggled between shots of a moving scene), the cantina's grade 0.86 and 0.83, and Mustafar's frames moving more between two shots than either pass changes them; costs, the bloom 0.094 ms and the grade 0.011 ms in turns off WebGPU's timestamps against 0.089 and 0.010 ms off WebGL's own timer per pass. Nothing of it has been seen by the owner.

**The ambient occlusion and the fire on WebGPU** (on `claude/three-187`, not yet on main; the GPU port's sixth step, `A:\swg3js\port\STEP6.md`, its second maker's half: items 4 and 5, the words for what is not ported, and the step's proof). Built: on WebGPU with Effects on the ambient occlusion is the WebGL pass's own object drawn by node graphs over its own uniforms, half or full resolution, with its sky and rooms' light sets read from the portal stencil, the torch, the rooms' pool (its spots and casting points), the sun's cascades taken off WebGPU's own sun, its two products (the half distance and the half normals) and the debug view, so `__debug.ssao({ split, view })` works as on WebGL; it is kept off the water by drawing the water's twins' geometry into its region target, which WebGL reads from the water mask product WebGPU has not got (`__debug.ssao().water.webgpu`). With Effects off every material is now tone-mapped and encoded in its own output and summed on the canvas, as WebGL draws it (the inline output), so a brazier's flames, sparks and glows and its smoke add up as on WebGL, and the blue blade's glow no longer drifts violet. `localStorage['swg.gpu.ssao']` or `['swg.gpu.inline']` `= '0'` with a reload leaves either out; `__debug.gpu({ inline: false })` moves the inline output live. `gpu()` and every Graphics row of a pass not ported say "not on WebGPU yet", naming the blade glow and its shadows among them. Measured only in headless tabs of our own (WebGPU on Direct3D 12, WebGL on ANGLE Direct3D 11): the occlusion's own views against WebGL's differ by a mean of 0.05 to 0.73 levels on Mos Eisley's street, the cantina and Theed's lake (the multiplier white over the water on both), and 0.39 to 0.47 on Mustafar but for a band of far ground at the horizon that WebGPU's far cascade takes for shadowed (its fraction 0.85 against 0.14, the sun's own cascades, not this step's); a brazier's flames at night, their brightest twentieth 255 on WebGL with Effects off and 255 on WebGPU with the inline output, 206 without; the blue blade at Effects off red over blue 0.128 on WebGL, 0.13 with the inline output, 0.305 without; the occlusion 0.08 ms in turns off WebGPU's timestamps at 1600 by 900 against WebGL's 0.126 (with its products 0.147); the Effects switch in play, after the step's fixer, built no pipeline at a draw in four switches of a fresh session, in four switched straight after a teleport into Mos Eisley (51 before the fix, where WebGL built two programs in play), and in six after the inline output's console toggles (whose own first draws build the canvas's output, as said), the chain's own graphs none at any (the water's coverage was built at every new chain's first frame with water in view before); two-minute walks at Mos Eisley, Theed, Mustafar's mining cantina and its lava built no pipeline at a draw from this step's graphs; and over the street, the cantina, Theed and the lava all 424 of 8d0c759's WebGL program sources are this build's, `fxCheck` clean. Nothing of it has been seen by the owner.

**The WebGPU frame's main thread** (on `claude/three-187`, not yet on main; the GPU port's performance step, `A:\swg3js\port\STEP-PERF.md`, its first maker's half: items 1 and 2). Built: the frame report on WebGPU, `await __debug.gpu({ perf: true })` (`src/core/gpu/framePerf.ts`): the main thread and its sections with the sun's three cascades timed on their own, a second window counting a frame's render calls and passes, objects drawn, refreshes by kind, pipeline and bind-group switches, buffers set, bundles replayed and recorded, bytes written, submits and anything made, then the card's time where three's timestamps answer; and two switches that take WebGPU's main thread down, each on, its off step 6's draw (`src/core/gpu/drawCost.ts`): `replay` (`patchReplay.ts`, a fifth private patch of three's, pinned in `patchTable.ts`) asks three about a bundled object only where its answer could be other than nothing, and `shadowCull` (`shadowCull.ts`) culls what casts no shadow from a shadow pass before three tests, lists and sorts it. Measured only in a headless tab of ours on the RTX 5080 at 1600x900 with the frames held to 72 a second, both on against both off, interleaved in one tab: the draw's main-thread time 0.37 ms less at Mos Eisley's street, 0.22 at Bestine, 0.18 at Kachirho, 0.12 in the cantina, 0.11 at Theed and nothing at Mustafar; profiled at the street, three's `needsRefresh` 0.22 ms a frame less and the cascades' listing 0.1 to 0.14 less. Before the step, so held, WebGPU's frame stood at or under WebGL's at most views and over it at Bestine (p50 4.8 against 4.4 ms) and Mustafar (4.0 against 3.4); the machine was too busy afterwards (the owner's own Chrome, another worktree's tests) for a fair absolute after at every view, so the switches' worth is the interleaved figures. Telling the plain shadow programs they carry no node measured nothing and was taken back out. Picture pairs at the street: the switches alternating differ by no more than the scene's own motion between two shots. Not built: the cascades' cadence, fewer render calls, the render-group buffers written once a frame and the bones once a frame, which are the gap left and the next maker's to weigh. Nothing of it has been seen by the owner. Its second maker (items 3 to 5): a third switch, `sweepPace` (on, its off step 6's pace), paces the Effects switch's sweep in play on WebGPU by a slice of listing a frame where step 6 waited a frame after every batch of eight: at Mos Eisley's street the switch took 2.4 to 3.5 s on a quiet machine (11.3 s the session's first) where it took 13.5 s, and WebGL's 13.4 to 13.6 s (41 s its first), with nothing built at a draw. Arrivals, cold and warm profiles, in headless tabs of ours: Tatooine's first arrival 23 to 28 s on WebGPU against 52 to 73 s on WebGL cold and 26 to 30 s against 32 s warm, a cold travel to Naboo about 33 s against 50 s, with no pipeline built at a draw; two of three WebGPU arrivals on Naboo straight after a reload waited out the warm-up's 30 s landing bound (82 and 93 s against 29 s and WebGL's 71.5 s), which is not mended. Four two-minute walks (the street, the cantina, Theed, Mustafar's lava) built no pipeline at a draw. The climbing course stood on WebGPU with nothing built, a body walked the stairs and climbed a wall as on WebGL, and the climbing's own cost a frame (the `player` section) is the same code on both, 0.2 to 0.6 ms; grabbed with E (the use key, the grab's own), a braced lip hangs and a wall climbs alike on both renderers (the fixer's run; this maker's runs landed no grab, and Space, a Jedi's Force jump, grabs nothing). The frame on a quiet machine: WebGPU at or under WebGL's median at the street and the cantina, 0.4 ms over at Bestine, and 3 ms over on the street looking low across the climbing course (not isolated); under load at Mustafar and Kachirho well under WebGL's, at Theed and the forest 0.5 to 1 ms over at the median with a better p95; `A:\swg3js\port\PERF-findings.md` is the written finding for the remakes. Its fixer: the card's time on WebGPU was three's own answer, often a frame's first few passes; it is now every timed pass of whole frames (`completeCardFrames`), 0.8 to 1.8 ms at the step's views, and the report says which passes go untimed (three's `clear()` as the frame begins); WebGL's timer queries count the main thread's issuing and are said beside it, never compared. In the fixer's quiet session (both renderers in one tab, the step's switches on): WebGPU's median at or under WebGL's at the street (6.0 against 6.2 ms), the cantina (5.0 against 5.2), the street over the climbing course, Theed (5.4 against a settled 5.8), the forest (4.7 against 4.6, even), Kachirho and space, 0.2 ms over at Mustafar's lava and 0.3 over at Bestine, where the cascades (1.30 against WebGL's shadow pass's 0.76) and the effects chain (0.38 against 0.20) outweigh a world pass 0.5 ms cheaper; the scene's walk, which the review read as a WebGPU premium, is the same cost on both in a settled tab and rises with a busy one. `shadowCull` alone is about 0.07 ms a frame at the street by the profiler and nothing measurable at Bestine: kept, and the owner's to keep or take out. Not mended: a same-world teleport can build a person's pipelines for a room's pass at a draw (the warm-up's, either way the switches stand), and three arrivals straight after a reload waited the warm-up's landing bound out (76 and 129 s on Tatooine, over 300 s on Mustafar). Nothing of it has been seen by the owner.

**Pass 1's first wave: the measures** (on `claude/three-187`, not yet on main; the GPU port's pass 1, wave 1, `A:\swg3js\port\PASS1-W1.md`, its first maker's half: items 1 to 7; nothing of the picture changes). Measured: Tatooine's arrival at Mos Eisley holds 651 pipelines 30 s in, and a morph attribute alone separates 380 of them (every crowd body has a geometry of its own and three keys a program on its morph attributes), bone count 18; baking the morphs would take 348 away, the bone pool alone 15, both 370, so wave 4's target is at most 300; the bake costs about 1 MB at the street, Bestine and the cantina against 13 to 16 MB of morph textures held today; and no clip the game plays keys a morph weight (20,761 GLB files and the imported pack read: no `weights` channel). The numbers, the baselines at the four views on both renderers and the commands that made them are kept on the owner's machine (`A:\swg3js\port\PASS1-census.md`, `PASS1-baselines.md`). The wave's review found the anchor scan stopping at a call's first bracket and a statement's end, so a uniform made of a world matrix inside a nested call, the camera's place, an eye's components and a place reaching a 32-bit array a statement later all passed; it now reads each call's whole argument and follows a name filled from a world place to the end of its block, with every form the review found planted in its own check. **Pass 1's first wave: the small things** (on `claude/three-187`, not yet on main; the same wave's second maker's half: items 8 to 11, which change both renderers alike). Built: **H hides the interface** (`hideHud`, rebindable on the Controls page, where the help's row was): `#ui` wears `ui-hidden` (not `hidden`, whose page rule would take the menus down too) and the stylesheet hides every child of it by `visibility` but a named list of what stays -- the panels and the map, the loading screen, the travel curtain, the death card, a conversation, the emote wheel, the debug menu, a trade's window, the creator's bar, the chat line, a group's question and the editor's window -- while the frame writes none of what it hides (each piece's `rest` closing its second of writes, so `__debug.hud()` reads the hidden second's own 0) and every piece is written afresh when it comes back (`refresh`: value records let go, two-state records taken from the page). Not kept across a reload; hidden stays hidden through travel; the world editor's H is still its key list, and the editor coming on shows the interface. `__debug.hud({ hidden })` does the same from the console. **The help panel is gone**, with its action (a saved `help` choice is dropped on load), its corner word and the kits' help lines. **The class words are gone** from the display, the creator (a new character is made with the Jedi kit) and the select screen (the list's line, the card's row and line), and the Interface page names its crosshair switch by the saber and the Force; C still switches the kit and a saved Bounty Hunter keeps it. **The address** keeps every switch as typed and takes an old link's `planet`, `zone` and `class` off (`src/core/address.ts`); the old one wrote over the whole address at each arrival and lost `?server=` and `?word=` on a reload. **Pass 1's third wave: the sun's cascades held on WebGPU, its maker** (on `claude/three-187`, not yet on main, built on e9b8e9d; the brief `A:\swg3js\port\PASS1-W3.md`, the tab's section 1 and its "Wave 3: small questions", whose five defaults the owner kept). Built: `src/core/gpu/heldCsm.ts`, three's `CSMShadowNode` subclassed and run by the world's own holds (`shadowCadence.ts`), made by `sun.ts` in place of the plain node at three's development commit (`heldCsmApplies`; at any other revision the plain node draws every cascade every frame and `gpu().forcedOff` says so). `World.updateShadows` arms it once a game frame where WebGL decides (the WebGL lines in the `else`); the first render after that runs three's own fit and `ShadowHold.decide` through an adapter (the node's breaks, its light's own direction, its reach), held lights and targets go back where their maps were drawn with their world matrices, and every later render that frame and every precompile of the warm-up's changes nothing. Each cascade's draw is told to the hold as three makes it (`drawnOne`, through a permanent wrap of the cascade's own `updateShadow`, under the frame report's temporary one), and a cascade asked for and never drawn is dirty at the next arm (`undrawnDirty`). The world's one hold is bound to the node's lights on WebGPU, so `SHADOW_PASS_TUNE.cadence`, the `shadowCadence` switch, the `shadowPass` sliders and console and every `dirtyAll` of the world's reach it, and the frame report's `cascades drawn` and `holds refused` count its decisions; `forceGpuOff` no longer holds the cadence off (the shadow batches it still does); `gpu().sun.hold` counts the frames armed, the renders and precompiles that reached the node, the decisions and each cascade's draws. Held on from the start on WebGPU (the tab's first default), WebGL's ages and fast-mover dial unchanged. One allocation mended in the guard both renderers share (`lengthSq()` written out, the same sum to the bit: 32 B a frame in 2 of 48 processes before, none in 84 after). Proven in node: `heldCsm.test.ts` (new, in `test:fx`, 55 checks) over the game's own `makeSun`, the world's holds, three's own fit and three's own `ShadowBaseNode.updateBefore`, the draw alone stood in: one decision a game frame over 1,440 frames of one to four renders with precompiles among them, nothing moved after the first; at 144 Hz standing the far map every sixth frame and the middle every second, four renders a frame drawing exactly what one does; held lights, targets, world matrices and sampling matrices as their maps were drawn, and the occlusion's cascades reading them; a cascade asked inside a frame three had drawn found dirty the next; the frame report's real timing wrap over the permanent one, every draw told; the switch, a world `dirtyAll` and the menu's own age dial; a fast mover drawing the far map; the coverage proof made again against the node's own fit, which stands within 2.3e-13 m of the classic cascades' with the same boxes, its shader's band 5 cm further out (counted from the near plane): 15,552 holds with the game's splits (12,416 pass) and with three's practical split (12,331, the classic proof's 12,328), no ground lost across the light or past a deep end, `decide` agreeing with `holdCovers` on every hold (13 and 12 exact ties), so no guard needed mending; the refusals by their own reasons; turning at 90 degrees a second the middle drawn on 50% of frames and the far on 17%; and 0.114 B a frame in 12 of 12 processes. `threeAnchors.test.ts` holds the fifteen methods of three's it overrides or calls to their text at `188dev` (section 6b), `gpuOff.test.ts` the new sites; tsc, the build and the 255 suites that read a touched file pass (`shadowWiring` and `skeletonShare` moved in their own words to the new lines). Its browser proof is the next paragraph's; `shadowPass({ compare })` is WebGL's and answers in words on WebGPU.

**Pass 1's third wave: the browser round, the review and the fixer** (20 November, on `claude/three-187`, not yet on main; a prover, a reviewer and a fixer, every Chrome headless on the NVIDIA adapter, started directly and visibly through the tool's own background run, none refused; the scripts and outputs in `A:\swg3js\port\scratch\p1w3-prove\`, `p1w3-review\` and `p1w3-fixer\`, the pictures `A:\swg3js\port\shots\p1w3-pair-*` and `p1w3-fix-*`). **The hold stays on** (the tab's fourth default, applied by the fixer). Saved on the main thread, held against `cadence: false` in 16 and 16 alternating blocks of 48 frames held to 72 a second in one tab: the frame's median 8.2 to 7.4 ms at Mos Eisley's street (the review's own run 6.9 to 6.3), 5.4 to 4.9 at Bestine's square, 6.8 to 5.9 at Kachirho and 5.8 to 5.1 in the Naboo forest, the portals 0.5 to 1.1 ms less, the frame report's sun shadows 1.4 to 2.1 ms down to 0.9 to 1.3, cascades drawn a frame 3 to 2 at 72 a second and 3 to 1.66 at the browser's own rate; WebGL at the four views within noise of wave 2's, its holds deciding as before, and its program sources at Mos Eisley over two runs a build each the same as an e9b8e9d build's but for variants of water programs without the cascade defines, compiled by timing on either build. One decision a game frame at most over 5,400 frames walking and turning outside and inside Kachirho, in the cantina and three times through its door, every cascade drawn at most once a frame and the stagger advancing once; in play the node met only 0 or 1 renders a frame (once in the fixer's round, on the way into the cantina, a render with no arm since the last decision, which decided nothing), so the many-renders case rests on `heldCsm.test.ts`. The review's probe rebuilt each cascade's sampling matrix from where its light stood after every frame: 0 differences and 0 held lights off their record over 6,600 frames. Fast movers: a starport shuttle at Mos Eisley over 11,000 frames reached the middle band on 926 frames and the far on 687 and was drawn on every one, the reason `mover` each time; an X-wing flown low past Bestine at up to 133 m/s, 687 of 687; the review's shuttle 66 of 66; a ridden speeder casts into the near map only, drawn every frame. Not staged: a riderless speeder past the camera over 20 m/s, and a ship landing from height (`landing({ up: true })` did not lift a spawned X-wing standing on the ground). Pipelines: Tatooine's arrival 631 (a quiet reload, the prover), 645 (the review) and 645 (the fixer's cold arrival, 38.8 s), inside wave 2's 631-649, none by a draw in play but, on the prover's cold arrival, one weapon's colour pipeline never warmed, as wave 2's base and pin showed; a two-minute walk at Mos Eisley 1 and its settle 2 (colour pipelines of the rooms pass and a weapon never warmed), the travel to Naboo 0, to its space 2, the jump 10, back down 0; not one a shadow pipeline; no GPU error and no "Shader Error" anywhere. The pictures: the browser round's pairs drew the held frame and the next one fresh, so a frame's own step stood in every pair (a fresh pair at the same view put 0.004 to 0.73% of the pixels over 8 levels) and the hold could not be read apart. The fixer's pairs draw the held frame, two fresh redraws and their floor at one state of the world, the clock crept from the held frame on (`docs/lessons/testing.md`), the view turning at 90 degrees a second up to it, the sun at the game's own rate, the middle held at 9 to 12 ms and the far at 34 to 38 (within a frame of its 41): pixels over 8 levels 0.090% at the street (worst 54), 0.005% at Bestine (50), 0.023% at Kachirho (89), 0.163% in the forest (51) and none in the cantina (worst 9), each floor between two fresh draws 0 to 0.004%; WebGL's shipped hold at the street under the same harness 0.100% (worst 47), and with the view still 0.049% against WebGPU's 0.047%. Every one of those pixels lies on a thin edge where a surface shades itself (roof lines, rails, the crest of a slope) or on the far canopy's sway: both renderers snap their cascades in a frame turned with the sun about the world's origin, so the sun's own slow turn re-quantises such edges a little every fresh frame and a held map shows it up to its age late (`docs/lessons/performance.md`). With the WebGPU sun's direction stood still for the run the same pairs read 0.004% at the street (worst 23), 0 at Bestine and in the cantina, 0.003% in the forest (20) and only the far canopy's sway at Kachirho (0.016%, worst 85). So no pair is within 8 levels pixel for pixel while the sun moves, on either renderer, and WebGPU's hold matches WebGL's; with the sun still it is, but for what moves within the age. The fixer read the fourth default by that (the saving three to four times its line), kept the hold on, and leaves the strict reading to the owner (`shadowPass({ tune: { maxAgeMs: [0, 0, 0] } })` or the `shadowCadence` switch to compare by eye). The review's two findings: **the stagger shows a due map past its age** -- reproduced through the real `dueCascades` (at 72 a second, whose frame is longer than 13 ms, the middle is shown at 13.9 ms on 800 of 2,400 frames; after a 61.5 ms frame one map at 61.5; the review saw the middle at 13 to 21 ms in play and 68 after a hitch, the far never past 41); the rule is the performance pass's own and WebGL's, so it is mended in words, not changed: the README's `shadowPass` row, the age dials' note, `maxAgeMs`'s comment and `performance.md` now say the stagger shows the other map one frame more, and `{ tune: { stagger: false } }` draws both, the owner's call; and **this account was out of date**, mended here. Node over what the fixer touched: tsc, the build, `shadowCadence`, `shadowHold`, `heldCsm`, `shadowWiring`, `gpuOff` and every suite that reads a touched file; the whole run (`npm run test:par`) is the fixer's report's.

**Pass 1's fourth wave, its first maker: the morph bake and the real-body stand-ins** (20 November, on `claude/three-187`, uncommitted on 499c5d5; the brief `A:\swg3js\port\PASS1-W4.md` items 1 and 3, the tab's sections 2 and 3, the owner's defaults of "Wave 4: small questions" all standing; items 2, 4 and 5, the bone pool, the bones patch, the older stutters and the creator's preview figure, are the second maker's, built on this). Built: `src/core/gpu/morphBake.ts` (switch `GPU_KEYS.morphBake`, `src/core/gpu/gpuKeys.ts`): in play on WebGPU every mesh with morph targets the world adopts (`World.adoptMaterials`, through which every actor's preparation, the arrival's sweep and the scan pass) is drawn from a bake of its own, its positions and normals worked out by three's own CPU morph (to the float, held in node against `Mesh.getVertexPosition`) and every other attribute and the index its own objects over the source's arrays, shared by its clones (`cloneShared`); the frame (`PortalRenderer.render`, before the bundles) bakes again in place any body standing in a scene whose influences moved (a slider in play, the next frame, nothing allocated); play begins before the arrival's sweep (`play()`) and ends with the world, so the creator's figure and the select screen morph live; every preview's clone (`CharacterPreview.refresh`, `testPicture`) is handed the live source; `__debug.gpu({ bake })` moves it live. `src/core/gpu/bodyStandIns.ts` (switch `GPU_KEYS.realStandIns`): on WebGPU the person warm's stand-ins (`World.personStandIns`) are learnt off the real bodies the scene holds, a triangle of each body's very layout on its bone count, casting and receiving as it does, wearing its material's copy with one-texel twins of each picture's sampler state, held in node to three's own material and geometry keys run as the build's text. **Measured** (headless Chrome on the NVIDIA adapter through Direct3D 12, fresh profiles, `arrive.js`, the owner's dev server and his own Chrome open and idle, nothing else of ours): a cold Tatooine arrival held **645** pipelines 30 s in with both switches off (wave 1's 651), **241** in each of three arrivals with both on (the target 300), 265 with the bake alone; nothing built by a draw in play; the screen 50.9 s against 23.9 to 24.5 s. What merged: every morph attribute's split (395 pipelines apart by it, 380 by it alone), the one-bone stand-ins' 36, the shadow pass's 133 to 15, the world's 248 to 105 and the rooms' 233 to 90; skinned pipelines 449 to 57, by bone count now (53: 25, 50: 10, 38: 8, 30: 6, 39: 5, 41: 3; the pool's projection 23 more). The bake at the arrival: 200 bakes, 1.6 MB baked (24 B a vertex: the census estimated 0.88 MB at the street), 5.0 MB on the card whole, against the 12.7 MB of morph textures it spares; the longest bake 0.4 ms. A slider moved in play rebaked the player's seven parts the next frame and the body showed it; a doll made over the baked player drew the live sources and followed a slider; the switch off and on in play moved 304 meshes each way with no error. Not yet seen: picture pairs of bodies against the base, the frame report's A/B, warm arrivals, the walk, travels, jumps and Kachirho (the second maker's and the fixer's).

**Pass 1's fourth wave, its second maker: the bone pool, the older stutters and the creator's doll** (20 November, on `claude/three-187`, uncommitted on 499c5d5 beside the first maker's work; the brief's items 2, 4 and 5). On WebGPU every body's bones are one storage buffer written once a frame (`src/core/gpu/bonePool.ts`): a slot a skeleton, this frame's bones then last frame's, each matrix the body root's inverse times the bone's world matrix times its inverse bind matrix worked in doubles (body space), written after the scene's one walk in `PortalRenderer.walkOnce` in one `writeBuffer`; a body a draw meets that the frame's write did not reach is written there and then with previous equal to current, a still body is written once more and skipped, and a skeleton no draw meets for 120 frames lets its slot go. The sixth private patch, "bones" (`patchBones.ts`, 188dev, hashed in `gpuPatches.test.ts`, its reliances said in `threeAnchors.test.ts` 6c), wraps the node manager and library on the live renderer: a pooled body's graph key is three's own with the skeleton read as "pooled", its materials set up by three's own `setupPosition` with the body read as unskinned and then skinned from the pool, its observer told it is not animated, and three's refresh question touches the pool. Three's skeleton and bones are untouched, so the CPU's readers and the previews read them as before; nothing poses a bone between `walkOnce` and the draws (the feet, the rest pose and the look are the player's update, before the frame; the render hooks write draw ranges and uniforms). The warm-up's builds now take a render id of their own (`patchWarm.ts`): at the development commit three keys a render object's environment once a scene and lights per render id and puts the id back to nought after every render, so every build between frames read the key first worked out there and built the graph cached under it, for the scene as it stood then; that was the rooms pass's people after a travel back and its sky stand-in, and most of Theed's hangar (the browser proof still saw it built once at the forest view), but not the jump's stutters (the jump measured here carried nothing across: a jump carried to another system still built 12 to 16 at a draw until the fixer's mend, in the fixer's paragraph below). On WebGPU the creator's doll holds the world's WebGPU-filtered cube off what its own WebGL context draws (`ForeignEnvironment` in `characterPreview.ts`), which threw every frame once the creator's place had loaded. Cold Tatooine arrivals 30 s in: 213, 213 and 213 pipelines (242 with the pool off in the same conditions, the first maker's 241; the target 300), the skinned 29 all keyed "pooled", shadow 15 to 7, world 105 to 95, rooms 90 to 80; screen 23.4 to 23.8 s; nothing built by a draw in play over the arrival, a two-minute walk at Mos Eisley, Bestine, a travel to Naboo and the forest view (7 before the render-id mend, 0 after), a travel back and the cantina, space, a hyperspace jump that carried nothing across (`jumpState().carried` false: 0; the carried jump of waves 2 and 3 was not measured here and still built 12 to 16) and back, and Kachirho. The frame report at Mos Eisley's street, three blocks a side interleaved: portals 4.16 ms against 4.70, 422 full refreshes a frame against 512, 324 buffer writes (578 KB) against 445 (659 KB). Same-state pairs (clock crept, the cascades redrawn before each picture) pool against three's skinning: 0.0046% of pixels over 8 levels at the street, 0.0018% at the cantina's door, 0.0129% at Bestine, 0.0005% at Kachirho, each within what the switch itself moves (pool against pool after a switch off and on 0.0049%, 0, 0.0172%, 0.0006%), all at shadow edges. Not yet seen: the Naboo forest's pair, people climbing, in a saddle and ragdolled in a pair, the census at the other three views, WebGL's program hashes against a base build (held in node by `gpuOff.test.ts`; a WebGL arrival built nothing in play and `fxCheck` was clean), warm arrivals measured three times a side, and `npm run test:par` (the fixer's).

**Pass 1's fourth wave, its browser proof** (20 November, the wave's prover, on the two makers' uncommitted work over 499c5d5, nothing of theirs changed but two lessons' words; headless Chrome on the NVIDIA adapter through Direct3D 12, one game tab at a time while another session's headless Chrome stood on 9982, the owner's dev server and viewer idle; drivers and every number in `A:\swg3js\port\scratch\p1w4-prove\`, shots `A:\swg3js\port\shots\p1w4-*`). **Arrivals, back to back, each side alternately, a fresh profile a cold one and its reload the warm one**: the base build (a `git archive` of 499c5d5 with its own `npm ci`) held 645, 631 and 645 pipelines 30 s in cold (screens 28.6, 28.4, 27.2 s) and 503, 631, 645 warm (114.7, 29.1, 26.6 s; the 114.7 s one built a pistol's pipeline at a draw); the wave held **209, 213 and 213 cold** (30.9, 25.1, 23.9 s) **and 209, 213, 213 warm** (24.6, 23.4, 23.0 s), none built at a draw; the second maker's 166 warm was not seen again. What merged against the base: every morph attribute's split (393), the bone count's (35, the skinned now 29, all "pooled"), shadow 133 to 7, world 248 to 95, rooms 233 to 80. **The route on one tab**: two-minute walks among Mos Eisley's crowd (67 bodies) and Bestine's (56), travels to Naboo, to its space, back to Tatooine and to Kashyyyk built nothing at a draw; **the forest view built one (Theed's hangar, warmed under another key) and the hyperspace jump carried from Naboo's space to Tatooine's built 16** (its tunnel's `MeshBasicMaterial`, `MeshLambertMaterial`, `PointsMaterial`, `ShaderMaterial`), as in waves 2 and 3: item 4 is not done for the jump (the maker's 0 was a jump with nothing carried). The census at the views: the street 209 (236 after a travel back), the cantina 210, Bestine 199, the forest 213, Kachirho 202 (wave 1: 599 at Bestine, 614 at Kachirho, 529 at the forest). **The bake's memory** held: the street 1.67 MB (199 bakes; 1.83 after the travel back), the cantina 1.52, Bestine 1.34, the forest 1.37, Kachirho 1.59; above the census's 0.88, 1.37 and 1.06 (the street by 1.9 times), still about a tenth of the 12.7 to 16.5 MB of morph textures it spares; on the card 4.2 to 5.7 MB whole. **The frame report**, three blocks of 240 frames a side, both switches on against both off, interleaved: main p50 Kachirho 4.27 against 4.57 ms, the forest 3.97 against 3.97, the street 5.37 against 5.47, Bestine 3.87 against 4.17; portals 3.03/3.40, 2.54/2.50, 3.76/3.81, 2.30/2.66; the card 0.79/0.83, 0.77/0.82, 0.72/0.75, 0.72/0.75; the street's full refreshes read more with the switches on (252 against 187) and its sun shadows 0.87 against 0.66 ms, unexplained and noisy. **WebGL once**: the base held 391 programs at the street and the cantina and the wave 389, every one of them the base's; the base's other two came in a second load of the wave's; `fxCheck` clean, no Shader Error. **Same-state pairs** (`spairw.js`: off against on, both taken after a switch, the lesson in `testing.md`): over 8 levels the street 0.005% (floor 0.005%), the cantina 0.002% (27 silhouette pixels), Bestine 0.0005%, Kachirho 0.0001%, the forest 0.04% (floor 0.004%: silhouettes and shadow edges), the player running 0.008%, in a speeder's saddle 0.011%, ragdolled 0.013% (none on the body); **not made: climbing** (the course's 6 m wall started no climb from the driven tab). A body hidden 40 and 150 frames reappeared with its first frame equal to the next, but the WebGPU chain draws no motion blur, so no smear could show there. **Previews**: a slider moved in play showed the next frame (7 re-bakes); the doll and the appearance page refreshed with the bake on and off drew the same to the level, and followed a slider; the select screen fresh and after a play matched the base's in look (the after-play figure is brighter on the base too); the creator threw nothing with its place loaded (the base threw 108,969 log lines), and its body sliders moved the figure as on the base. Node: tsc and the wave's seven suites pass, the pool's twelve-process allocation at 0.125 B a call.

**Pass 1's fourth wave, its review and its fixer** (20 November, on `claude/three-187`, uncommitted on 499c5d5; the reviewer's four findings, each reproduced before it was mended; headless Chrome on the NVIDIA adapter through Direct3D 12, one game tab, no other session's debugging Chrome running, the owner's dev server and viewer idle; drivers and every number in `A:\swg3js\port\scratch\p1w4-fixer\`). Mended: **the bone pool's graph key is worked out once a render object and kept** (`patchBones.ts`, its record in `bonePool.ts`): three's `NodeManager.delete` asks the key again as a graph's last user goes, which three makes happen when the scene's key has moved, and the key worked out afresh named another graph, leaving the old one filed with no user and taking a live one out (the review's 18 orphaned graphs after the cantina and 60 after a travel to Corellia, 191 bodies drawing from graphs the cache no longer held); now 0, 0 and 0 over the same route, and none off the cache; `NodeManager.delete` and `RenderObjects.get` are hashed among the patch's reliances and anchored in `threeAnchors.test.ts` 6c, and `bonePool.test.ts` runs three's own `getForRender`, `delete` and key across an environment change and both ways of the switch. **A body rebound to another skeleton of its own bones' count is refreshed for the new slot** (`touch` forgets the old record's epoch; it read the old slot, another body's once that was let go). **The carried hyperspace jump builds nothing at a draw** (item 4): three keys every render object on the scene's fog node, which is the fog object's own, and `World.load` made a new fog, so the tunnel, the hull, its crew and the old sky dome were made again at the swap and their pipelines built again by the next draw (the tunnel's program text the same before and after); on WebGPU the scene now keeps its one fog, its colour and density set. And the destination's own sky, its planets and whatever the swap made are built as they are hung (`World.gpuMadeNow`, `gpuSkyReady`, the warm-up's `WARM_NOW` behind the runtime's `compileNow`: a call's items built in the caller's task, each pipeline still asked for asynchronously, so the next frame skips what has not landed, behind the closed tunnel), and a new sky's reflection capture is listed for the kept cube and held until its pipeline lands (a capture that skipped its dome would leave every reflection without the sky for four seconds). Measured: the carried jump from Naboo's space to Tatooine's (`carried` true) built 0 at a draw in three runs, one each way and one on the route (it built 9 before the mend in this session, 12 in the review, 16 in the proof; with the fog kept the 9 were the destination's own, then 7 with its planets built as hung, then 1, the capture, with its sky, then 0 with the capture held); the longest frame in the closed tunnel 27.8 ms and none over 33 ms, every frame with the tunnel open at most 7.2 ms; the builds at once took 38 ms of the main thread over the jump, in 5 calls of 67 items. STATUS's second-maker paragraph is corrected in its place (its jump carried nothing). **The route on one tab** (an arrival, two-minute walks among Mos Eisley's crowd, 66 bodies, 215 m, and Bestine's, 69 bodies, 115 m, the cantina, travels to Corellia, Naboo, its space, the carried jump, back to Tatooine and to Kashyyyk): nothing built at a draw but **Theed's hangar at the Naboo forest view, once** (one two-sided material of it in the rooms pass, warmed as `?`: listed and still queued when the rooms pass first drew it, a streamed tier the warm-up's clock lost the race to; not mended, named). Once in a session a short-lived `MeshBasicMaterial` (one side, never warmed, gone when looked for) was built at a draw between measures, after an arrival at Kashyyyk and once after a travel into space; not found. **Arrivals at Mos Eisley, cold (fresh profiles) and warm (reloads)**: 204, 213 and 213 pipelines 30 s in cold (screens 46.1, 27.8, 29.1 s; the first unexplained) and 213, 213, 213 warm (23.3, 28.1, 28.1 s), none built at a draw; the base's (the prover's: 645, 631, 645 cold, 503, 631, 645 warm) not run again. The census at the views on the route: the street 209 (229 after the travel back), the cantina 212, Bestine 201, the forest 203, Kachirho 193; the bake 1.44 to 1.64 MB. **WebGL once**: an arrival built 4 programs in play within 30 s (a worn hat's lens, as the prover saw on both builds), `fxCheck` clean, no Shader Error; every line this fixer added under `src/` is WebGPU's alone or a guard whose WebGL branch is 499c5d5's line, held by `gpuOff.test.ts`. Node: tsc; `bonePool` 85, `gpuPatches` 162, `threeAnchors` 199, `gpuOff` 285, `gpuWarmKey` 15 (the build at once), and the suites that read the touched files.

**Pass 1's fifth wave, its maker: one motion buffer** (20 November, on `claude/three-187`, uncommitted on 2b6e3a1; the brief `A:\swg3js\port\PASS1-W5.md`, the design's section 7; the owner answered the wave's five small questions in the tab, each agreeing with its default, so the defaults stand). Built: with Effects on, the WebGPU scene target carries a second half-float output (`src/core/gpu/motion.ts`; 8 bytes a pixel, 11.5 MB at 1600 by 900) that every draw into it writes: this frame's place in normalised device coordinates less last frame's (three's own convention, unjittered) in red and green, the reactive mark in alpha. Still things by one camera-delta matrix a frame worked in doubles (last projection times last view times this inverse view) on three's own view-space place; anything three refreshes because its world matrix moved by its own change in view space, worked in doubles at that refresh and taken by the card only on the frame it was worked for; bodies by the bone pool's last half, the delta turned into view space by the model-view's turn alone; the far rings by last frame's place they hand in (their own vertex over last frame's copies of the eye, the lattices and the frame: `motionPrevious`, `previousUniform`, ready for sway, grass, the wind and the swell when their remakes come); the sky by the camera's turn. A see-through material (three's own rule: its blending, and its transparency under normal blending) keeps the motion behind it and marks itself reactive by its alpha (an additive one by its brightest channel times its alpha), its motion output blended nought and one with the alpha's max: the seventh private patch (`patchMotion.ts`, gated and hashed like the others) makes the renderer's multi-output node follow the target set and tells the pipeline maker which materials blend; three's pipeline key carries the material's blending and transparency and never the node or its blends, so no pipeline is added (the census names the see-through ones: `gpu({ keys: true }).motion`). The step's programs, whose fragment is their own, merge the output as three merges a colour (`withMotionOutput`); the warm-up builds each item under its own context's node. `GPU_MOTION.on` (`__debug.gpu({ motion: false })` makes the chain again with one output behind the Effects notice; `localStorage['swg.gpu.motion'] = '0'` at load) and `__debug.gpu({ view: 'motion' })` draws the buffer. Proved in node (`gpuMotion.test.ts`, 240 checks): what every kind of material writes, the pipeline three makes for each through its own pipeline maker, the key, the node following the target, the arithmetic 0, 8 and 16 km out within 0.003 px of exact (three's own velocity's way 0.87 px off at 16 km), the stamp, and nothing allocated in a frame in twelve processes; `gpuOff`, `gpuPatches` (the sixteen methods of three's the patch wraps or stands on hashed), `anchorScan` and every suite reading a touched file pass, tsc and the build clean. First look in a browser (headless Chrome, NVIDIA Blackwell, nothing else of note running but the owner's dev server and his body viewer): a cold Tatooine arrival held 212 pipelines 30 s in, none built by a draw, no GPU error; two reloads' arrivals 216 and 212 (the 216 with 4 listed and built ahead in play, none by a draw); a two-minute walk at the street built none by a draw; the sky's motion read back while the camera turned agreed with the frame's matrices to 0.01 px; the switch off and on built the other target's pipelines once behind the notice (166) and nothing after. What the prover and the reviewer read off the buffer, and what the fixer mended, are the next two paragraphs; weather tried after TAA's place waits for wave 6's pass.

**Pass 1's fifth wave, its browser proof** (20 November, the wave's prover, on the maker's work, nothing of it changed; headless Chrome 157 on the NVIDIA Blackwell adapter, one game tab, another session's headless Chrome running beside it; every motion read back at the start of the next frame against the camera's own matrices and the depth, worked in doubles apart from `motion.ts`: `A:\swg3js\port\scratch\p1w5-prove\mp.js`, the lesson in `testing.md`). Census at three cold Tatooine arrivals 215, 216 and 216 (wave 4's 204 to 213; the two more are the motion view's pipelines, warmed whichever way the switch stands), none built by a draw in play over the arrival, the street walk, the views, the Bestine walk, travels to Naboo, space and back, the carried jump, Kashyyyk and Kachirho, but the Theed hangar's known leftover twice at the forest (seen with the motion off too). The frame report at the four views, motion on against off in interleaved blocks: the card's world pass 0.04 to 0.06 ms more a frame, the main thread the same at Bestine, Kachirho and the forest and 0.3 to 0.4 ms more at the street (noisy, not split), refreshes, bundles, draws and pipeline switches the same within noise, 25 to 68 more buffer writes a frame. Still things p99 0.003 to 0.04 px turning or walking at the four views and the cantina, the sky p99 0.003 to 0.012 px, the far rings p99 0.0016 to 0.0029 px 8 and 14.9 km out (the farthest Tatooine reaches from its scene's middle), no shimmer; a flying speeder and a ship's parts 0 against 13 to 79 px of motion; people walking, running, climbing, in a saddle and ragdolled within 0.003 to 0.2 px at the median of their own motion, a body shown again after hiding reading still on its first frame and right on the next; a lit blade and the fountain's water marked reactive with the motion behind them kept to 0.03 px and 0.0015 px; particles weakly (two batches). Not proven: glass (none in view), and trees, grass and the swell, which WebGPU does not move yet. One line failed as the brief words it: a blaster bolt's core wrote its own motion (taken up below). WebGL's program set at Mos Eisley's street and cantina held every one of a base build's 390 (the tree's three more are world state from run to run).

**Pass 1's fifth wave, its review and its fixer** (20 November, on `claude/three-187`, uncommitted on 2b6e3a1; the reviewer reproduced the wave with a read-back of its own, `A:\swg3js\port\scratch\p1w5-review\rv.js`: at the street still things p99 0.073 px at 8.7 px a frame, see-through pixels keeping the motion behind to p99 0.012 px, Mustafar's blowing dust marking 74% of the frame reactive and keeping the motion under it, and motion on against off the same draws, refreshes, bundles and render calls; four findings, each reproduced by the fixer before it was answered). **The bolt's core** is opaque and writes the bolt's own motion: not a fault of the buffer, since an opaque draw can never keep the motion behind it (drawn nearest first, what stands behind it fails the depth test there and is never drawn), and its pixels are marked reactive by the glow, which wraps the core whole and is drawn after it, taking the larger reactive and keeping the motion under it; the brief's line had a see-through bolt in mind. Nothing of the game changed for it; `gpuMotion.test.ts` lists the core beside the glow and holds, from `bolts.ts`'s own text, both materials and that each glow (a bolt's and a beam's) wraps its core, the glow's mark over the core being 0.71 and 0.62 for the warm-up's two colours; read in a browser, a flying core's seen points were all marked reactive at 0.7148, reading the bolt's own motion to 0.19 px (0.29 at most, the sample's own offset along a bolt whose motion changes along it) against 20 to 24 px a frame, where a still reading would be 20 px off. **The ropes laid on the CPU** (the grapple's, a rope hanging as a chain, another player's) read as still while they swung: their vertices are written in place under a matrix that never moves. Mended (`motion.ts`'s `laidMotion`, the runtime's `motion.laid`): each strip is registered as it is made, by one WebGPU line in `RopeRibbon`'s and `ChainRibbon`'s constructors (on WebGL nothing, and its geometry as it was), its geometry given an attribute (`swgMotionLaid`) holding each vertex's place last frame less this frame's, worked once a frame from a kept copy (nothing allocated; a strip hidden, out of the scene, or whose draw range changed reads as still that frame, never a jump), which the graph turns into view space by the model-view's turn alone; the attribute's name is in three's key, so the strip's graph is never shared with the dart's on the same material (a mark three's key does not read, `userData` among them, would let two draws filed under one graph disagree). In node, the game's own strips made as on WebGPU and laid over 40 frames: within 0.0003 px of each vertex's exact motion at the middle and 0.011 px 16 km out (the strips' vertices are world places in 32-bit floats as before; the buffer's own half-float step at that 59.5 px is 0.049 px); in a browser, the grapple's rope during a jump on it: 545 pixels within 0.0011 px at the median, 0.011 px at most, against 3 to 6 px of motion where the old still reading was 3.8 px off at the median; firing the hook and showing a rope built no pipeline in play. Still not tracked: a mesh particle's copies (their batch never moves; each copy reads the camera's change, a see-through one keeping the motion behind it as marked): a copy's last place needs each particle's own last matrix carried in `particles.ts`, deferred to the lead and the owner. **The far rings' last-frame copies** put world-measured vectors into new 32-bit uniforms in a file the anchor scan did not read: `anchorScan.test.ts` now scans what pass 1 added to a base file (`PASS1_PARTS`, the rest of the file blanked), and holds every last-frame copy of a uniform (`previousUniform`) to say what it holds; the rings' copy is allowed with its reason (the ring is placed by those vectors from before pass 1, the motion is the difference of two vertices worked alike, read within 0.003 px 8 and 14.9 km out), the ring placed from an anchor being the later precision work's. **This paragraph** is the fourth. A cold arrival on the fixer's fresh Vite (a 54 s screen) held 205 pipelines 30 s on with one built by a draw (a pistol someone in the street held, never listed for the warm-up, nothing of this wave's), a warm one after a reload 203 as the screen went and 205 after, none by a draw, no GPU error.
**Pass 1's sixth wave, its maker A: the edge smoothing** (20 November, on `claude/three-187`, uncommitted on cf79ca5; the brief `A:\swg3js\port\PASS1-W6.md`, the design's section 6; the tiers are maker B's).
