Built, WebGPU's alone with Effects on: `src/core/gpu/taa/`, written over the chain after three's `TRAANode`, `TAAUNode` and `Reprojection` at 0245b6d (MIT, credited in each file) rather than copied, since every input is the game's: the camera nudged by sixteen Halton(2, 3) offsets through its own view offset round the scene's passes and the weather (`taaJitter.ts`; `main.ts`'s frame begins it before `PostFX.begin` and ends it after the chain and in the frame's `finally`, the projection copied back to the bit, picks and the interface never nudged; the motion frame reads the projection unnudged); a pass `taa` last of the scene's passes (sanitize, ssao, taa, bloom, colorGrade, output on WebGPU), on with the chain and drawing by the Graphics page's new Smoothing row (Off, Full, Upscaled; Full the default, the High tier's; the row hidden on WebGL), each pixel's history reprojected by wave 5's motion buffer, held by a linear, relative depth test against the distance kept beside the history (the motion buffer's blue is now the surface's view distance this frame less last frame's, so what moves with the camera keeps its history), the neighbourhood's spread clipping it and the reactive alpha raising the new frame's share; two history targets taken in turn and the colour copied into the chain's buffer, about 29 MB at 1600 by 900 (Full) and 35 (Upscaled). Upscaled draws the scene, its products and every pass before the smoothing at `share` (0.7) of the canvas (`taaSize.ts`, `PostFX`'s WebGPU sizing and its walk in two sizes, `src/core/fx/chainWalkGpu.ts`), and every reader of the scene's pixels reads the scene's size (the portal renderer's visible set, the rain's and the motes' sizes, the cull console's rectangles; the canvas's where no chain stands, and the frame's context at the scene's size too, both made so by the wave's fixer: as the maker left it the context went back to the canvas's every frame and the readers read a pixel with Effects off). The dials are the owner's: `__debug.gpu({ taa: { share, current, currentUp, reactive, depth, edge } })` and the debug menu's sliders under `gpu`. `localStorage['swg.gpu.canvasMsaa'] = '0'` makes a one-sample canvas, to measure the 4x canvas. Proved in node: `gpuTaa.test.ts` (439 checks: the jitter's offsets and sign and its exact put-back, the graphs' parts, the pass over a stand-in renderer, the sizes and the walk, the dials, nothing allocated in twelve processes), `gpuOff`, `fx`, `airOff` and `anchorScan` moved in their own words (the scan now reads every file under `src/core/gpu/`, its folders' too). First look in a browser (headless Chrome 157, NVIDIA Blackwell, one tab, nothing else of ours running): Mos Eisley's street smooth under Full and Upscaled and stepped under Off, no trail seen in a fast turn (the ground softer while it turns, the accepted softness), the cantina's doorway drawn through its stencil, no GPU error, no pipeline built by a draw over an arrival and every mode switch in the first session; card time at the street 0.04 ms for Full and 0.05 for Upscaled (the frame 0.93 and 0.79 against 0.79 Off, one run each); the 4x canvas costs the chain's last draw 0.003 ms there (0.013 against 0.010 ms). Not yet seen: the brief's proof (the pod at speed, the ghosts, the see-through things, the fences and fronds, the budgets), and in a second session (warm arrival, one-sample canvas) the upscaled resolve was built once by its first draw with its warmed key, which the prover should look for in an ordinary session.

**Pass 1's sixth wave, its maker B: the quality tiers** (20 November, on `claude/three-187`, uncommitted on cf79ca5 with maker A's smoothing; the brief `A:\swg3js\port\PASS1-W6.md`, the design's section 9; the tab's wave 6 questions unanswered, so its defaults stand). Built, WebGPU's alone: `src/ui/qualityTiers.ts`, one table of existing settings, a row a setting and Low, Medium, High and Ultra across, with the design's starting values (render scale 1, 1, 1.5, 2, never above the screen's own; Smoothing Upscaled at 0.6 and 0.7, then Full; shadow maps 1024, 2048, 2048, 4096 and distances 120, 200, 320, 400 m; ambient occlusion off for Low; people caps 24, 32, 40, 60; grass reach 0.6, 0.8, 1, 1.25 and tree distance 256 m, 320 m, then as far as the ground, which is 0.6 and 0.8 of the ground's own 416 m and the most there is for Ultra), the budget at 60 frames a second at p95 on both; one setting added, `smoothingShare`, Upscaled's share, which the tiers set and which feeds the smoothing's `share` dial at load. The slider is Picture and effects' first row (a preset drawn as a range, Custom when any of its settings is moved alone, the thumb then at the tier held most of), handed to the game whole: the tier is written and saved at once and applied behind the Effects switch's notice, the scene swept for the target drawn now (`App.applyQualityTier`, `tierCurtain`); left off the page on WebGL, where nothing changed. A new player on WebGPU starts on High (`startOnTier`: only the render scale differs, 1.5 where the screen is denser than that; a new player being a browser with no settings saved and no character, and the start saved, since the wave's fixer: as the maker left it every player at the default render scale was moved). `?gpu=low` asks WebGPU for the low-power adapter. Found and mended on the way, WebGPU's: a shadow map resized in play (the tier, or the Shadow resolution row alone) left every lit thing binding the destroyed maps, a GPU error a draw for good (`sun.ts`, `bundles.ts`'s `relight`; `docs/lessons/webgpu.md`). Proven in node (`qualityTiers.test.ts` 182 checks, the menu driven in a stand-in page; `gpuShadowResize.test.ts` 17, failing with the mend taken out), and in a browser on the 5080 at 1584 by 805: every tier at the four views reads its name, sets what it says (at two device pixels a point too: Ultra 3168 by 1610, High 2376 by 1207, Low 1584 by 805 with the scene at 950 by 483), and builds nothing at a draw but the upscaled resolve at the session's first change to Upscaled (maker A's open line, reproduced); a new player at two device pixels a point arrives at High's 1.5. The budgets: `?gpu=low` reached no other adapter (Chrome answered the 5080, and with its low-power switch no adapter at all), so the card is the 5080's as it is, every tier at every view between 0.53 and 1.42 ms at p95 against 16.7; the main thread holds 16.7 ms at p95 unthrottled at every tier and view (worst 14.9, the street at High) and under the 2x throttle at none but the Naboo forest's Low, Medium and High (13.3 to 15.8; Ultra 16.9; the street 30 to 36, Bestine 21 to 26, Kachirho 24 to 35), the tiers moving the main thread little. Not seen: the tiers on the Mac, the budgets under load-free conditions (another session's Chrome ran beside every measure), and how a tier looks, which is the owner's.

**Pass 1's sixth wave, its prover** (20 November, on `claude/three-187`, uncommitted on cf79ca5 with both makers' work; the brief `A:\swg3js\port\PASS1-W6.md`'s "What proves it"; headless Chrome 157 on the NVIDIA Blackwell adapter through Direct3D 12, one game tab, 1584 by 805 at one device pixel; scripts in `A:\swg3js\port\scratch\p1w6-prove\`, shots `A:\swg3js\port\shots\p1w6-pv-*.png`). **One fault found and mended:** in every session, the first switch to Upscaled built the upscaled resolve's pipeline by a draw. The warm-up had built it for the full history's second output (`r16float`, not `rg16float`), because three gives both histories one render context and the deferred build read the textures a later frame had left in it. The warm-up now puts back the context's textures as they were listed (`patchWarm.ts`, `gpuWarmKey.test.ts` 1b, which fails with the fix taken out; the lesson in `webgpu.md`). After the fix, a cold profile arrived at Tatooine with 220 pipelines and none built in play, then switched Upscaled, Off and Full with none built, and each resolve held one pipeline used by both the warm-up and the draw. **Seen in pictures:** at Mos Eisley's palms and antennas, Off shows stepped edges, Full and Upscaled smooth ones. Over 16 still frames, pixels that moved more than 24 levels were 0.055% of the frond-and-antenna box under Off, 0.173% under Full and 0.100% under Upscaled; under Full the masts' edges flicker 8 to 24 levels while Off holds them steady. There were no ghosts behind a fighter running past a wall, a speeder driven across the view at 25 m/s, a 172-degree-a-second turn at Mos Eisley, Theed's fountain or Kachirho, nor behind a pod at 85 to 98 m/s over the desert, a bolt over Theed's lake (the bolt was visible in every frame of every mode, peak red over green 94 to 100 against 5) or a snowspeeder's canopy. Full drew the saber's swing at night as Off did. Upscaled drew that swing glow speckled, the pod's engine shadows with blocky, speckled edges at speed, and a faint grid at the edge of the fountain's spray during the turn; all three are the dials' territory (`reactive`, `currentUp`, `share`), the owner's to judge. The cantina's doorway drew through its stencil in all three modes. The editor's aim was the same point to the millimetre in all three modes. The appearance page's preview drew. **Budgets** (four views, every tier, p95; main thread unthrottled, then under Chrome's 2x throttle; card): the card held everywhere, at most 1.46 ms (Kachirho, Ultra). The unthrottled main thread held everywhere, at most 14.1 ms (Kachirho, High). Under the 2x throttle the main thread held only at Bestine (13.3 to 16.0 ms); it read 21 to 38 at the street, 26 to 39 at Kachirho and 19 to 30 at the forest. **Smoothing against Off** (two alternating blocks each, High tier): the pass itself costs 0.036 to 0.038 ms of card under Full and 0.043 to 0.048 under Upscaled. The whole frame's card p95 and main-thread p95 stayed within the blocks' own spread at every view. **WebGL:** all 387 programs of a cf79ca5 build at Mos Eisley's street and cantina hash the same in the tree. The tree held five more, content programs (a palace window, a mud-hut rock, two lenses, one plain basic) with no counterpart by name in the base's set, so nothing's source moved. There is no Quality or Smoothing row on WebGL, and `fxCheck()` was clean. **Not the wave's, still to look at:** pipelines built in play by the console's test turret and its bolts (never warmed), and by Theed's hangar on the travel to Naboo (maker B saw it too). Nothing was compared against the base on WebGPU for these.

**Pass 1's sixth wave, its review and its fixer** (20 November, on `claude/three-187`, uncommitted on cf79ca5 with both makers' and the prover's work; the reviewer's seven findings, each confirmed in the code and reproduced in a browser or in node before it was answered; headless Chrome 157 on the NVIDIA Blackwell adapter through Direct3D 12, one game tab at a time, 1584 by 805, no other session's debugging Chrome running, the hud tree's Vite and the space body viewer's server idle beside it; drivers and every number in `A:\swg3js\port\scratch\p1w6-fixer\`, shots `A:\swg3js\port\shots\p1w6-fx-*.png`). Mended, each WebGPU's alone with its WebGL line the one cf79ca5 had (`gpuOff.test.ts`): **under Upscaled a setting that resizes a pass sized it from the canvas** (`PostFX.configure`): the occlusion's region, which shares the scene's depth, went to 1584 by 805 against the scene's 1109 by 564, and every submit of the scene's render context was refused wherever the region draws (indoors); the old line run again in the page in the cantina put 2,272 GPU errors in four seconds, and the occlusion resolution moved through the menu now keeps the region, its targets and its uniforms at the scene's size, Full and Half, in the cantina and out, with none (`configureSizesGpu`, every resize there by the scene-size rule). **The frame's context was put back to the canvas's size every frame** (`updateContext` handed the canvas's): the occlusion's full-size uniforms and its probe's middle read the canvas's while its depth and targets were the scene's, and under Upscaled the occlusion all but went (its darkening over the street's picture 0.052 with the old size stood in, 0.296 under Full); the context is handed the scene's size now, and Upscaled darkens 0.268 at the default half-resolution occlusion and 0.297 at full, the walls, windows and arches as under Full (`p1w6-fx-street-half-m2-aomap.png` against `-m2-old-aomap.png`); the motion view's pixels a frame are the scene's pixels with it. **With Effects off every pixel reader was handed a pixel, or the last chain's size** (`SCENE_SIZE` begins at 1 by 1 and outlives its chain): reloaded with Effects off, the portal renderer counted every room of the house and the cloning centre seen (22 visits, 136 room meshes in the review); the runtime's `taa.sceneSize` answers the drawing buffer's size where no chain stands (`sceneSizeNow`, the newest smoothing pass not let go of, kept as a list since the Effects switch can hold two chains and throw the newer away), and the same view reads 1584 by 805 and 9 visits, 21 room meshes, with Effects on Upscaled, off in play and off from a reload alike. **The start tier moved every player at the default render scale**, not only new ones: saved settings hold only what differs from the defaults, so a player at two device pixels a point who had moved the shadows to 4096 read as new and went from 2 to 1.5 unasked; a new player is now a browser with no settings saved and no character (`isNewPlayer`), and the start is saved so it outlasts the first character made; in a browser at two device pixels a point a new player booted at 2376 by 1207 with `{"renderScale":1.5}` saved, the review's player at 3168 by 1610 with their settings as saved. **`gpuTaa.test.ts` ran in no script**: it is in `test:fx` now (451 checks). The two docs paragraphs that said the context and the readers took the scene's size are true now and say whose it was. **Not mended, the owner's and the lead's:** the main thread under the 2x throttle, measured again with the machine quieter (no other Chrome): at p95 the street 21.1 to 21.5 ms at every tier, Bestine 12.6 to 16.8, Kachirho 16.1 to 21.6, the forest 12.9 to 14.8, so every tier holds at the forest, Low, High and Ultra at Bestine (Medium 16.8), Low alone at Kachirho, and none at the street; unthrottled every tier holds everywhere (p95 4.6 to 8.1 ms), the card every tier everywhere (p95 0.60 to 1.41 ms), and the tiers move the main thread little, so holding the throttled budget is main-thread work or another reading of the stand-in, not a tier's setting. The frame report, Smoothing Full, Off and Upscaled in alternating blocks at High: the pass 0.036 to 0.040 ms of the card under Full and 0.043 to 0.052 under Upscaled, the whole frame's card and main thread within the blocks' own spread at every view. Shown again over what the fixer touched: a cold arrival at Mos Eisley 34.0 s, 220 pipelines 30 s on, none built by a draw, no GPU error; a warm one with Effects off 27.8 s, none; Effects turned on in play and every Smoothing switch, none; every tier at the four views read its name with none built by a tier (the travels built the known carbine scope and Theed's hangar's two); WebGL at the street and cantina held 381 of its 383 programs as a cf79ca5 build's, the other two the prover's same two content programs, `fxCheck` clean, no Quality or Smoothing row, the occlusion's resolution moved and back with nothing wrong. Node: tsc and the build; `gpuTaa` 451, `qualityTiers` 207, `gpuOff` 342, `gpuWarmKey` 17, `gpuShadowResize` 17, `anchorScan` 67, `themes` 39 (its pin of the settings' read moved in its own words), and the whole of `npm run test:par`; `gpuTaa`'s jitter measure, in a script for the first time, read 80 B a call in two of its twelve processes once under the run's load and nothing alone, the known under-load kind.

**Pass 1's seventh wave, its maker A: the meshlet trial, parked** (21 November, on `claude/three-187`, uncommitted on 8980954 beside maker B's `lods`; the brief `A:\swg3js\port\PASS1-W7.md`, the design's section 5, step A; the owner's answer to question 1: park the meshlets below 0.5 ms of main thread saved at Mos Eisley's street, reversed depth off in both arms; drivers and every number in `A:\swg3js\port\scratch\p1w7-makerA\`). **The verdict is no-go, and the trial is parked.** Three's own `MeshletDrawingPipeline` (the addon at the pin, 188dev), drawing the placed objects it accepts, saved no main-thread time at the street: it cost 7.9 to 9.1 ms a frame more at p50 and 13.7 to 15.9 ms more at p95, while the card's frame barely moved (0.817 and 0.858 ms off, 0.875 and 0.889 on). **Built:** `src/core/gpu/meshletTrial.ts`, behind a switch of its own, `?meshlet` on the address, read once at start, WebGPU alone, needing `?norevz` beside it (three's pipeline draws nothing under reversed depth, and the trial says so). Its one import of the addon sits behind `import.meta.env.DEV`, as the world editor's does, so it runs on the dev server alone and a built game carries none of three's addon (no chunk of a build holds its classes); the trial's own module does ship, dormant, in the start chunk (its census, its cluster prepare and its report, which answer "parked" in a build), and removing the trial is what takes that out (the wave's review and fixer). Without the switch nothing of it runs: `__debug.meshlet()` and `__debug.gpu().meshlet` answer one line and the drawing pipeline is three's standard one. With it, the trial offers three's pipeline only placed objects (the streamer's stamped meshes and the buildings' rooms), refuses a material where our node copy would lose a part (the detail map, the leaf cards), puts the parts that carry (0.187's indirect light, the client's highlight) on the draw material three makes, and keeps three's pipeline off inside the warm-up's precompiles, leaving their render lists' batches alone; `__debug.meshlet({ on, occlusion, census, prepare, dress })` moves it live and `gpu.meshlet` is its frame-report A/B switch. `meshletTrial.test.ts` (94 checks, in `test:fx`) drives three's own addon in node: the census against three's own verdict, the warm-up's walk, the switch, the parts, the default path, and hashes of the ten addon methods and functions the trial overrides, calls or names. **The census** at the street: of 6,026 placed object-and-material rows three took 2,696 (44.7% of the rows, 68.7% of the copies, 70.6% of the triangles) and refused, in rows, 1,840 with no copies (level groups standing empty, which draw nothing anyway), 684 for vertex colours, 397 for our detail map, 192 two-sided, 190 see-through, 16 for our leaf cards and 11 for a normal map on the second uv set; no line disagreed with three's verdict, and in three's debug view the trial drew mostly trims, props and arches, the big walls among the refused. Rows taken elsewhere: Bestine 51%, Kachirho 19%, the Naboo forest 39%, the cantina 43%. **The main thread**, trial off against on, p50/p95 in ms, alternating blocks of 96 frames held to 72 a second, High tier unless named, 1584 by 805, headless Chrome 157 on the NVIDIA Blackwell adapter through Direct3D 12: the street 6.5/8.1 against 14.4/21.8 (8 blocks a side, quiet), and again with the trial first and Smoothing off 6.3/7.7 against 15.4/23.6 (8 a side, quiet); the street by tier, 3 blocks a side, Low 7.8/12.3 against 24.7/36.8 (the other maker's tests running), Medium 6.4/8.7 against 13.6/20.5, High 6.6/8.3 against 15.4/23.7, Ultra 6.7/8.7 against 15.0/23.3; Bestine 9.4/12.0 against 23.0/30.9, Kachirho 11.3/13.8 against 21.8/31.2, the forest 8.3/12.9 against 14.6/22.5 (4 a side each, the other maker's game tab running beside: noisy). At the street the world pass's own section went from 2.7 to 10.2 ms, the card's draws from 393 to 355 a frame and its pipeline switches from 134 to 487; profiled, the cost is three's bookkeeping for each of its batches (about 1,100 at the street, each with a node graph and a pipeline of its own), its culling's writes every frame and its `_drawBatches`. **Pictures**, Smoothing off, at one crept state, off against on with both taken after a switch: in the maker's session at the street 0.038% of the pixels over 8 levels (worst 75), the size of the switch's own disturbance (off before against off after, 0.041%), floors 0.0008% and 0.0002%, the mask only on the player's shadow edge and one shadow edge on the ground. That did not hold: in the reviewer's two sessions and the fixer's one the same pair read 3.3 to 3.5% over 8 levels (worst 203 and 204), with three's occlusion culling on or off and after 8, 60 or 120 frames' settle, against floors of 0 to 0.0002%, and with the trial on objects three had taken went undrawn outdoors as well (the building's round windows and door, its ledge trims, a pole, a sign front, a bin), as the cantina's furniture did indoors. A step B would have to explain this before any picture pair of it counts; the verdict stands, the trial costing about 8 ms at p50 while drawing less. **The indoor control**, the cantina with three's occlusion culling off and on: 2.27% over 8 levels (worst 111) either way, floors 0. With the trial on, the room's chairs and table were not drawn and its terminal and shelf were drawn wrong; three's debug material draws them, and they stayed missing with our parts off and with the draw materials' stencil test set to always, so the fault is neither our parts, the stencil nor the occlusion pyramid; it was not chased further. The main thread there read 6.8/11.5 against 15.8/21.0 with occlusion off, 8.1/12.6 against 19.9/30.4 with it on. **Pipelines:** an arrival with the trial on held 1,389 as its screen went (the default path's 207), 1,240 built by a draw behind the screen and 645 in play, none warmed, since each batch's draw material is a fresh node material with vertex-pulling nodes of its own; three made its batches again each time a newly clustered geometry moved its pool (55,878 draw materials over that arrival), and its clusters are made in play (about 12.5 s for 245 to 409 geometries at the street); at a still view nothing more is made. **The default path in a browser** (no `?meshlet`, reversed depth on): the addon was never fetched; an arrival in 27.5 s held 207 pipelines, none built by a draw in the 30 s after; a two-minute walk of 221 m built one by a draw, a crowd body's carried ion rifle "never warmed", not this wave's (the fixer's open item). **What step B would have to solve**, should it ever come: one program for every batch of a kind, warmed behind the screen; the main thread's bookkeeping for each batch's render objects; clusters made at conversion, and the culling not made again for each; reversed depth; vertex colours, the detail map's second uv set, two-sided and see-through materials; the objects it took and left undrawn, outdoors and in the rooms; an occlusion pyramid per pass. **Unproven:** the verdict holds under `?norevz` alone, which also cuts the rings' reach from 16 km to 4.9 km, so it may not carry to the shipping setup; WebGL was not looked at in a browser by this maker (nothing on its path was touched; `gpuOff.test.ts` holds it); no picture pair was taken at Bestine, Kachirho or the forest. The code can stay as the dev server's measure or go; the maker recommends it goes, since its anchors would need pinning again at every three release for a trial that is parked, and this paragraph, the lesson and the scratch folder keep the numbers and the method. The lead decides. **Removed after the verdict** (the lead's call): `meshletTrial.ts`, its test and its suite entry, the four lines in `start.ts` and the README's row are gone, so neither the dev server nor a build carries any of it; the trial stands whole in commit `81968dd` for step B, should a later pass take it up.

**Pass 1's seventh wave, its maker B: `lods`** (21 November, on `claude/three-187`, uncommitted on 8980954 beside maker A's meshlet trial; the brief `A:\swg3js\port\PASS1-W7.md`, the design's section 5's last item). Built: `npm run swg -- lods <out-dir> [--only=] [--dry-run]` (`tools/swg/madelods.mjs`), which gives every placed model and plant the client gave no levels up to three, at about a half, a quarter and an eighth of its triangles, by meshoptimizer 1.1.1's `simplifyWithAttributes` used directly (MIT; pinned in `package.json` and the lock, credited in `LICENSES/meshoptimizer-MIT.txt`, carried by the launcher's release from `node_modules` at the pin, `CONVERTER_PACKAGES`, with every package name the release imports held to it, `bareImports`): the exterior simplified as one mesh, normals and first coordinates weighted, the corners where two pieces meet locked, a level kept while its error stays under 0.02 of the model's size and it has at most 0.8 of the level before's triangles, none under 256 triangles (that error is meshoptimizer's own estimate: measured against the surface both ways, 5 of 28 made levels on two of the owner's packs went past the threshold, by up to about 2.4 times, the wave's review); written as the client's own are (`lod:<n>` in the `lods` scene, the entry's `lods` rows, each made row marked `made` with its error), so `lodLevels` draws them unchanged and only the model's own level collides; switching where the error comes to a pixel of a 1440-row screen at 60 degrees, which joins the detail levels' live knobs as `LOD_LEVEL_TUNE.madeBias` (`src/world/lodLevels.ts`'s `chainNears`, read again by a level group and a flora field at the next sweep once it moves) and the debug menu's new `lod` sliders (`src/world/lodDials.ts`: every level, the made levels, the band, set through `__debug.lod({ tune })` itself). Appended after everything the file had, so a run puts a file whose rows were lost back to the byte and makes it again, and a second run writes nothing; each pack stamped `madeLods`, which `status` asks by once (`lods <out-dir>`, its plan's step after the worlds, flora, gallery, travel and fittings, `pack:*` and the gallery held, 2.0 s and 314 MB measured), and the launcher's words for it. Proved: `madeLods.test.ts` (39 checks, the real command over a pack made with the converter's own writer: counts, every attribute kept, the simplifier's error under the threshold and the fixture's surface measured within twice it, no corner moved, the split's border whole, rooms and a model with its own levels byte for byte, idempotent, a lost file restored, a dry run, `status` asking once, a made chain's bias live in a real `LevelGroup` and a real `FloraField` while the client's stays, the sweep allocating nothing; twelve fresh processes read the harness's floor, 0.76 B a sweep, the client's chain 0.83), the launcher's (197), the plan's (101) and the 259 other suites that read a file it touched, all passing; tsc and the build clean. On copies of the owner's 43 packs in the scratch folder (never `assets-private`): 957 model files with no levels of their own, 82 given 94 levels, 875 given none (532 under 256 triangles, 219 skinned, 123 within the threshold short of 0.8, 1 with nothing outside its rooms); the triangles they come to at their lowest 231,756 to 165,573; the Rryatt Trail's 62,409 to 21,481 (though 16 of its 17 made levels switch in only 5 to 17 km out, its low plant's at 86 m, so that is the far view's gain), the gallery's 29,062 to 23,259, Tatooine's 11,185 to 10,904. Mos Eisley's street, every copy within its tier's reach all round at the level its distance picks: 230,051 triangles to 229,779 (4 copies of one made model), since the client already gave everything heavy there its own. In a browser, the game played the copies through a Vite overlay on 5189 (the repo's config with one plugin in front serving the scratch copies, everything else the real folder read): Tatooine arrived in 33.7 s with no GPU error, the street's level groups put 5 copies at made levels, and a pair across `madeBias` 1 and 1000 at one crept state, the Smoothing row off, read 0.0029% of the pixels over 8 at the street and 0.018% at 130 m from a banner pole (the switch is 114.9 m), the mask on the body's creep alone and nothing on the pole, against a floor of 0.0004% (`A:\swg3js\port\shots\p1w7b-*.png`); no pipeline was built at a draw by the four switches. Not run: the owner's own packs (his command, with the dev server stopped: `npm run swg -- lods assets-private`), WebGL in a browser (`gpuOff.test.ts` holds it; nothing of a program moved), the flora's made levels in a browser (five plants of the Kashyyyk zones have them, Kachirho's low plant and the Rryatt Trail's roots among them; node holds the field).

**Pass 1's seventh wave, its review and its fixer** (21 November, on `claude/three-187`, uncommitted on 8980954 with both makers' work; each of the reviewer's seven findings was reproduced before it was answered; headless Chrome 157 on the NVIDIA Blackwell adapter, one game tab, Vite on 5188; besides them, only the owner's dev server and the space body viewer's server ran, both idle; drivers and every number are in `A:\swg3js\port\scratch\p1w7-fixer\`). **Mended:** `madeLods.test.ts`'s allocation check failed about one run in six alone (776.7 B a sweep). Read with V8's optimisation state, every failing window had opened while the sweep's optimised build was still in flight, in the test's own busy process. It is measured now in twelve fresh processes, each the least of three clean windows of the same 3,000 sweeps against the same 16 B, and it passed 16 runs of 16 (192 processes at 0.76 to 0.83 B); a 57 B allocation planted in a scratch copy failed all twelve (`testing.md`). `gpuTaa`'s jitter measure flaked at 8980954 too (in the tree, 6 runs of 8 failed alone, 1 to 4 processes at 80 to 160 B). The cause was three's `updateProjectionMatrix`, which with a view on boxes the four sides it hands `makePerspective`. The jitter now works the nudge out itself, in three's own order, held to three's own call to the bit under both coordinate systems and reversed depth (`gpuTaa` section 1), with the three methods it follows hashed (`threeAnchors` 6d); its end no longer works out a projection that the kept one then replaces. After the mend 96 processes read 0.12 B and the suite passed 10 runs of 10 alone; in a browser the jitter ran 254 frames in 2 s with none put back late. The made rows are now carried through the game's own pack loader in node (`madeLods.test.ts`: `AssetPack` reads the fixture pack, each made level marked `made` and the client's own not); the check fails with `modelLevels`' line taken out. **Small question 5, the pipelines built in play:** the console's test turret built 4 pipelines by a draw, "never warmed" (its lit material and its eye's, in the world's pass and the rooms'), and its bolts 4 more (core and glow, for a colour first fired in play). Nothing lists a turret behind a screen. A material drawn before the quarter-second scan adopts it keys apart on WebGPU, where the portal renderer's stencil state is in three's key. And the warm-up's bolts, fired 900 m down and gone at the next update, were listed by nothing. On WebGPU alone, a turret as it stands, the warm-up's bolts and a colour's first shot are now adopted and built as they are hung (`World.gpuMadeNow`, through `TurretManager.onPlaced` and `Bolts.onMade`; `gpuOff` holds the guards). On a fresh session the console's shot, a turret and its seven bolts then built nothing by a draw and asked for nothing in play. On WebGL the hooks are unset, and the turret builds its one program in play as at 8980954. Theed's hangar (2 pipelines by a draw, in the rooms pass, listed and still queued) comes of the console's teleport to the forest view, which puts up no loading screen, together with the streamer's own choice to hang a building's rooms at once (`buildInterior`). The same route built 13 programs in play on WebGL, the hangar's rooms among them, so it is not the port's and is left. A player arrives at Theed by shuttle, behind its loading screen; that was not measured, since the console's ride wants a pad whose shuttle stands. **Reproduced and written down, the code unchanged:** the trial's street pair read 3.46% of the pixels over 8 levels in the fixer's session (the review's 3.3 to 3.5%), with objects three took left undrawn outdoors as well as in the cantina; maker A's paragraph and the lesson now say so. `lods`' measured error goes past the threshold on real models (three Rryatt Trail levels at 9.2 to 15.6 m against 8.7 to 13.5 m), since the threshold holds meshoptimizer's own estimate; the README, the threshold's comment, maker B's paragraph and `converter.md` now say so. The Rryatt Trail's figure is a gain of the far view: 16 of its 17 made levels switch in 5 to 17 km out. And the trial's own module ships, dormant, in a build's start chunk, with three's addon in none: input for the keep-or-remove call. Also measured, on WebGPU: a cold arrival in 35.3 s (209 pipelines when the screen went, none by a draw in play); after the mends, a reload's arrival in 47.1 s and a warm one in 20 s, again none by a draw. The trial's session held 527 pipelines when its screen went and 1,034 ten seconds later. On WebGL an arrival took 36.1 s, `fxCheck` was clean and the console's shot built nothing. Node: tsc and the build (`SWG3JS_NO_PRIVATE=1`, into scratch) are clean, and `gpuOff` (347 checks), `gpuTaa`, `threeAnchors` (202), `anchorScan` (67), `airOff` (110), `debugDocs` (61) and `madeLods` (41) pass, and `npm run test:par` passed whole, 824 suites in 927 s with none failing even under load.

**Pass 1's eighth wave, its maker A: the far ground's bake begun early, its colours on four threads** (21 November, on `claude/three-187`, uncommitted on f24dd65 beside maker B's cuts; the brief `A:\swg3js\port\PASS1-W8.md`, cut 2 of its design). Built: `HORIZON_TUNE.early` (on) begins the bake as soon as the pack's list is in (`World.beginHorizon` from `loadPack`, `HorizonField.begin`, the worker's `begin` word: the fetch, the sections, the heights, normals and pyramid), and `loadHorizon` takes it (`takeEarlyHorizon`, held to the terrain's pack, centre and shift) and hands over only the tile tables (`compose`); `HORIZON_TUNE.albedoThreads` (4, held under the machine's cores less one by `albedoThreadsHeld`) shares the fine albedo's rows over the decode worker and helper workers of its own module, each handed a message port (`helper`), the slices stitched back to the one thread's bytes (`composeAlbedoRows`, `stitchAlbedo`: the tile memo's 32-bit float put back wherever the one walk read it, `horizon.md`). Both serve WebGL and WebGPU alike (the same worker; no program or picture changes), and a world made in the browser takes the threads where it composes from tables. `horizon().loadMs` says `early`, `beganWall` and the threads used. Proved: `horizonEarly.test.ts` (132 checks, in `test:terrain`): every share from one to seven slices stitched to `composeAlbedo`'s bytes over two sets of tables, the stitch's rule on a cell standing on an sRGB byte's edge, the decode cut in two against the one word and against f24dd65's own (out of git, else its recorded hash), the worker's every word with and without three helpers on real ports and the same overlay and window answers after each, a helper lost before or while its slice is asked, a made world's make, the field itself both ways with the owner's edits laid at the load and a paint and a dropped building's ground made again after it (every texture as the card holds it), a begin refused or let go; nine mutations of the code each caught. In a browser (headless Chrome 157, WebGPU, a fresh profile an arm): every decoded array (heights fine and coarse, normals, both albedos, the pyramid, the window and its tint, its place) and the same arrays after the owner's edits were laid the same in all four arms (both off, early alone, threads alone, both on) on Tatooine, Naboo and the owner's Utapau world (made in the browser over a lent ground), and on WebGL with both on; the overlays made again after the load the same bytes, in order, in every arm that had them. Timed (ten quiet runs, five each way alternating, a fresh Chrome each, nothing else of ours running): Tatooine's first arrival 19.96 s to 18.39 s (medians), an arrival after a reload 19.28 s to 18.57 s, the wait at the join 2.90 s to 1.41 s, the screen's "flora, the ground and the sky" 3.14 s to 1.61 s, every other phase as it was, the first arrival's renderer peak 4.48 GB to 4.47 GB (a reload's peak follows whether Chrome has let the last page go, which it does or does not in either arm, `performance.md`). Not seen: a landing, the terminal's trip to Naboo and the WebGL arrivals timed with this cut (the wave's measurer's, with every cut together), and a machine of few cores. Found and left as they were at f24dd65: a travel to the owner's Utapau world on WebGPU takes the renderer past 13.7 GB and the GPU process past 21 GB, still rising after its screen lifts, after which WebGPU refused even a 128-byte buffer in that tab; and `__debug.horizon(...)` asked before the first world throws reading `this.terrain.swg` (the tune is applied before it throws).

**Pass 1's eighth wave, its maker B: the dev server's watcher, the hidden world's cadence, the people six at a time, the pictures straight from the file** (21 November, on `claude/three-187`, uncommitted on f24dd65 beside maker A's cut 2; the brief `A:\swg3js\port\PASS1-W8.md`, cuts 1, 3, 4 and 5 of its design; headless Chrome 157 on the NVIDIA Blackwell adapter, one game tab, Vite on 5188; scripts and records in `A:\swg3js\port\scratch\p1w8-makerB\`). Built, each behind its own switch and each on by default because its own proof passed. **Cut 1** (`vite.config.ts`'s `UNWATCHED`): the dev server no longer watches `assets-private/` and `imports-private/`, `SWG3JS_WATCH_ASSETS=1` watches them again (the README's setup says so; the owner's dev server takes it at its next start); the launcher is untouched. Proved by six hundred files of over four hours' rest out of the 5,032-file sound folder, through a Vite with the game's page served: 3.9 MB/s, 33.8 s and 50 CPU-seconds watched, 304 MB/s, 0.4 s and 0.9 CPU-s not (every file's last read moved by the read, so each was at rest), and through a whole journey: a travel to Corellia (707 of its 1,258 pack files at rest) took 29.5 s watched and 17.4 s straight after, the server spending 31 and 1.3 CPU-s, where unwatched Talus (542 of 1,037 at rest) took 14.9 and 14.1 s and a first Mos Eisley arrival over 1,912 files 20.9 against 20.2 after a reload (`devWatch.test.ts`: what Vite's own watcher watches and hears with the config's option, both ways). **Cut 3** (`GPU_DRAW_EVERY_MS`, 1000, set by WebGPU's boot alone; WebGL keeps `drawEveryMs` 100): every frame is still drawn from the moment a screen begins to go. No pipeline was made by a draw in the 30 s after any of twenty-eight arrivals at either cadence, the same 215 or 216 pipelines held as each screen went and 219 or 220 thirty seconds on whatever the cadence; at Mos Eisley's street the first frame drawn after the screen went against the twelfth after it at the same crept state read 0.21 and 0.22% of the pixels over 8 levels at 100 ms and 0.18 and 0.18% at 1000, the floor 0.17 to 0.19%. **Cut 4** (`MOBILE_CACHE.screenConcurrency`, 6, on both renderers, from a screen's coming up to `hide`'s first line, play keeping `concurrency` 2; `__debug.mobileTune({ screenConcurrency })` live): the same 40 bodies stood of a want of 46 (6 refused by the budget) at every reveal; in the two arrivals under load at 2 one or two were still loading at the bound, at 6 none, and the same pipelines and nothing built in play after (`screenLoads.test.ts` drives the cache's own slots). **Cut 5** (`SURFACE_TUNE.directPictures`, the `DirectPicturesPlugin` registered by `surfaces.withPlugin`, on both renderers): every picture of Tatooine's 1,024 model files (5,717) decoded both ways in one page, uploaded by the game's own renderer and read back off the card, the same to the bit on WebGPU and on WebGL, three's way making 5,717 object URLs and the plugin none (`directPictures.test.ts` drives three's own loader both ways, with an imports folder's shared pictures registered after it too, and holds the two methods of three's it leans on by hash). Found on the way: the first cut answered `loadTexture` itself and so hid every texture from the imports folders' one picture per distinct image (`sharedImages.ts`), which then decoded every copy apart (`importImages.test.ts` failed); the plugin now answers no texture and swaps the decoder in at each parser's own `loadImageSource`, the pixel proof made again on both renderers with it (the timings below were taken with the first cut, whose decode call is the same). Timed alone against all three at their old values (two quiet first arrivals and two after a reload each, fresh profiles, nothing else of ours running; four runs earlier under maker A's suites set aside): 22.3 and 22.0 s before; 21.0 and 20.8 with cut 3; 20.9 and 21.1 with cut 4, "the people here" 4.4 and 4.2 s against 5.7 and 5.5; 20.5 and 20.1 with cut 5, "the buildings and the props" 7.3 and 7.4 s against 8.7 and 9.0 and the main thread's busy time over the arrival 17.2 to 18.0 s against 19.5 to 20.0 (that phase 99% busy either way). **WebGL**: in that one session every one of the tree's 382 programs at Mos Eisley's street and cantina hashed as a f24dd65 build's, whose session held one more (a plain basic material no tree program lacks a counterpart for), and `fxCheck()` was clean; over many sessions the list is not one list, on the base as on the tree, and the cuts' timing let a race of the base's own fire more often (the wave's last paragraph). Not seen: the five cuts together (the wave's measurer's), a landing and the trip to Naboo with these cuts, WebGL timed, still pictures against f24dd65 on WebGL (the programs and every model picture are the same), and the people's own parts loader (`character.ts`), the creatures' and the rig's, which register only the skinned plugin and so still decode through three's object URLs. One arrival at 1000 ms had a 557 ms frame in the 30 s after its screen, which none of four more did (Chrome's long animation frames named a 118 ms game frame at 100 as well).

**Pass 1's eighth wave, the five cuts together: its measurer, its review and its fixer** (21 November, on `claude/three-187`, uncommitted on f24dd65 with both makers' work; headless Chrome 157, WebGPU on the NVIDIA Blackwell adapter, WebGL through ANGLE Direct3D 11, a fresh profile a run, one game tab; besides ours only the owner's Chrome, at 0 to 0.44 cores, and his idle server on 5195; the owner's dev server was stopped; records in `A:\swg3js\port\scratch\p1w8-measure\`, `p1w8-review\` and `p1w8-fixer\`). **Measured**, one build with the five switches at today's values against the makers' defaults (cut 1 on in both arms), three quiet runs each way alternating after an uncounted warm-up, the load retake's own scripts, medians, today's values against the defaults: WebGPU's first arrival at Mos Eisley 28.38 s to 22.22 (the design's target was about 23), an arrival after a reload 28.77 to 22.70, the landing at Bestine 19.38 to 15.32, the trip to Naboo 17.99 to 13.83; WebGL's first arrival 27.94 to 23.89 and after a reload 21.34 to 16.65; no run of one arm fell inside the other's range. Where it came from: "the buildings and the props" 2.3 s less on WebGPU's arrivals and 0.9 to 1.2 on WebGL's (cut 5, with cut 3 on WebGPU); "the people here" 1.8 s less on WebGPU's arrivals (the design said 0.9; cuts 3 and 5 free the same main thread, busy 2.4 s less, and the two were not separated), 0.4 and 1.4 on WebGL's; the far ground's wait 1.4 to 1.5 s less on Tatooine's WebGPU arrivals, 2.1 on Naboo and 2.25 on WebGL's first (the design said about 3: with the early start every part begun early is hidden, and what is left is the colours' composition and the window, 0.82 to 0.92 s on WebGL and 1.55 to 1.59 on WebGPU, which four threads would ideally make about 0.7; why WebGPU's is slower is not measured); the shader phases 0.55 and 0.65 s less on WebGPU (cut 3; the design said 0.8) and flat on WebGL, where cut 3 is off; downloads unchanged. No pipeline was made by a draw in play in the 30 s after any screen of any defaults run, and the held counts were the same in both arms at every screen; first arrivals' memory peaks were the same (WebGPU's renderer 4,269 and 4,278 MB, WebGL's 4,350 and 3,994). **Reviewed**: every maker's proof was taken again (the suites and eight planted breaks, cut 1's fetch test both ways, cut 2's hashes on WebGL against maker A's WebGPU ones, cut 3's picture pair, cut 4's bodies and slots, cut 5's 5,717 pictures, every switch live and back, the far ground's helpers all let go); one real problem. **Mended: a lake drawn before it was adopted.** A client lake (`applySwgWater`) was hung in the scene and left to the queued scan to adopt, and behind a busy screen that scan came late (a lake's first adopted draw 0.3 to 5.8 s after its add); a WebGL draw in between built the lake's program without the cascades, two programs (its two sides) no frame shows. The race is f24dd65's own: a water probe on the scene's own `add` (`wprobe.js`) saw a lake drawn before its adoption in one of two base sessions and in the one tree session it watched before the mend, and over sixteen sessions the pair stood in six, one of them the base's; the cuts' timing made it more common. Every water body hung straight in the scene is now adopted as it is hung (`adoptNow`, the scan's own adoption and queue made at once): a client lake, a basin's water, both sea meshes, and behind a screen a lake of ours and a river's bodies (in play `compileReady` adopts them before any frame). In three WebGL sessions with the defaults no lake was drawn before its adoption and the pair was never built, and in the two (and a WebGPU one) that asked, every lake was adopted in the task that hung it; `waterWiring` holds each site and `ourWaterIdentity` the client lake's line, six planted breaks each caught. **Restated: WebGL's program list.** At Mos Eisley's arrival, street and cantina, 385 programs stood in every one of sixteen sessions (three of f24dd65, thirteen of the tree at any arm), and eight came and went on both sides: a points program (4 of 16), a plain line program (12 of 16), a worn lens's four (in every session but two of the base's three) and the water's pair. Every program any tree session built, a base session built too. One session a side proves nothing about such a list (`testing.md`). **WebGPU's pipelines by key**: one session of f24dd65 and one of the fixed tree with every default held the same 222 pipelines, keyed by the hash of each stage's code and three's draw-state key (`gkeys.js`), none made by a draw in play. **Left, and said**: WebGL built 4 colour programs in play in the 30 s after every arrival in both arms alike (not the cuts'; whether they went through the queue is not read); WebGL's link waits were 0.3 to 0.4 s longer with the defaults (unexplained; the race's two links may be part of it, not measured); with the defaults one more person stood at WebGL's first reveal (40 against 39, none still loading); and the makers' own seconds sit 6 to 8 s below the measurer's on the same packs, so their gaps hold within their own runs and their seconds are not set beside these. The packs needed no new `lods` run: 43 manifests carry the made rows, written before either maker measured, and both arms read the same packs. Not seen by anyone: still pictures at Mos Eisley against f24dd65 on either renderer, an allocation measure of a frame (no frame code changed, by the diff), a machine of few cores, and the arrivals timed after the fixer's change (it moves a lake's adoption a moment earlier and asks for nothing more; not timed again). `npm run test:par` passed after the last change: 827 suites in 16 minutes, `storiesServer` failing only under load and passing alone.

**Pass 2's first wave, its maker A: the air itself** (21 November, on `claude/three-187`, uncommitted on 8a7de51 beside maker B; the brief is `A:\swg3js\port\PASS2-W1.md`, the design `A:\swg3js\port\pass2-design\pass2-design.md`). On WebGPU under our sky every world has one record of air numbers (`src/sky/air/airParams.ts`: Hillaire's Earth with the world's ground and haze, the star table's own sun, `src/sky/stars.ts`), and each environment row of its `sky.json` its own through the fit (`airFit.ts`, in a worker that starts as the sky loads and is waited on at the arrival's end, 0.7 s for Tatooine's 86 rows, 1.7 s for Naboo's 95): a row's fog against its family's clear row's becomes its share of the world's clear-day visibility, the gradient picture's noon colour (never the ramp's clear row, which is SWG's haze) turns the Rayleigh's hue with the noon sun's light held (along its power law of the wavelength since the wave's fixer; the first cut turned its channels apart), its dusk colour scales the ozone (never under three quarters of Earth's since the fixer); weather rows differ by their haze alone, and a frame blends the mix's rows by numbers. The card's four tables (`airTables.ts`, reached through `GPU.rt.airTables`): transmittance and multiple scattering drawn again only when the record moves, the sky view and the haze volume (32 cubed to 24 km, on compute) every frame, warmed behind the loading screen. The twin (`airTwin.ts`) works the same sums on kept arrays and writes `SwgSky.lighting`'s main light, hemisphere, bounce, fill (Tatooine's second sun, 1.5 degrees under the caster), fog colour and a fog-equivalent density the rain and wet surfaces read, after keeping SWG's own of the hour in `AIR.swg` (the steer's and the night floor's reference); `World` and the frame call it on WebGPU alone (`AirRun`, `src/sky/air/airRun.ts`). Two dials a world join the sky's dial set (`airDials.ts`): the clear-day visibility, 40 km to start (the design's clear day), and the steer toward SWG, 0 to start (realism first), copied out as `__debug.sky({ air: { <world>: { visibilityKm, steer } } })`. `DayCycle.count` is the sky's one unbroken day count. Proved: in node (`npm run test:sky`'s `airFit`, `airTwin`, `airRun`, `dayCount`, 77 checks) the optical depth against its closed form, the twin's transmittance within 3% of the direct sum from the zenith to half a degree up, its sky radiance within 2.4% and its hemisphere within 6% of the reference sums at the sun 60, 20, 5 and -2 degrees up, a clear Tatooine noon at 2.418 through the whole run (0.75% over 2.4, the first cut's anchor; the fixer moved it to today's key light, 1.903), the sunset only dimming and reddening until the planet hides it, the day count across midnight, 06:00, a held time and a shared clock, and nothing allocated by a frame of the twin or of the run, steady or moving, in 12 of 12 fresh processes; in the browser (headless Chrome, NVIDIA Blackwell on Direct3D 12) the tables read back against the reference sums and the twin at dawn, noon, dusk and night (transmittance 0.08% mean and 1.1% worst, multiple scattering 2% weighted, sky view 1.7 to 2.8% mean of the twin by day, the night's 10 to 22% half-float underflow), Tatooine's clear noon at 2.4176, three's timer at 0.025 ms a frame for the sky view and 0.29 ms more while the weather moves (the multiple scattering), the twin 0.16 µs a still frame and 26 µs a summing one (its sums spread over five frames), a Tatooine arrival of 225 pipelines with none built by a draw in play, and a travel to Naboo likewise. Not proved: the haze volume's own card time (the timer times render passes, not the compute), the sixteen pictures of the horizon (maker B's), WebGL's program list against several base sessions (the fixer's). What it cost to learn is in `rendering.md`, `webgpu.md`, `terrain.md` and `testing.md`.

**Pass 2's first wave, its maker B: what draws from the air** (21 November, on `claude/three-187`, uncommitted on 8a7de51 beside maker A's air; the brief `A:\swg3js\port\PASS2-W1.md`, the design's 2.2 and 2.4; headless Chrome 157 on the NVIDIA Blackwell adapter, one game tab, another session's headless Chrome running beside it, so no timing here counts). **The Sky row** (`src/sky/skyMode.ts`, the Graphics page's Picture section, WebGPU's alone, default ours): read once as the game loads, since the scene's one fog node and the far rings' graph are the session's, so a change is taken at the next start of the game and the row says so (the wave's default asked for the next loading screen; nothing here builds a pipeline for the sky not drawn); SWG's is today's sky to the node. **Our sky** (`src/sky/air/airSky.ts`, the dome's stand-in, named only once the air's tables are made so a frame behind the loading screen cannot build it without them, `SwgSky.airReady`): the air's sky view toward the dome's own direction, and each star of the world's system (`src/sky/stars.ts`: colour by black body in kelvin, size, strength; Naboo and Rori one star, Hoth's blue-white, Tatooine's pair with the second `PAIR_TUNE.belowDeg` 1.5° under the caster on its own azimuth) a limb-darkened disc of its top-of-air light times the air's transmittance toward it, faded by one less the overcast and left out of the reflections' capture; SWG's sun pictures are placed unseen under it; on Tatooine the fill light is the second sun from its own direction while it is up (its colour the twin's). **The night floor** (`src/sky/air/nightFloor.ts`, until wave 2): by night the lights the twin wrote and the dome are held at no less than SWG's own of the hour times `NIGHT_FLOOR.share` (1), nought by day (the fixer added today's night brightness as a floor at every hour and the fog's colour by night: the wave's last paragraph). **The haze** (`src/sky/air/airHaze.ts`, the registry's `haze`, before the edge smoothing): every draw into the scene target writes a third output, the see-through layer (`airSee.ts`: a solid's ±1, the minus in a building's rooms pass; a see-through thing's own light, premultiplied, blended over by its kind, nought into the colour with its own alpha), so the pass takes each solid pixel as `c · T + L · |s.a| + s.rgb` with the air's T and L at the solid's own distance from the haze volume (the sky view's light past 24 km), and every see-through thing hazes itself at its own distance through the scene's one fog node (set once, after the session's first air is made and before that screen's sweep) or, for the client's particles, through `hazeOwnNode`; held exact in node over 2,000 random stacks against every layer drawn and hazed the old way, the one inexact kind a multiply or another custom blend. Rooms take no haze from inside (the rooms' pass draws through a fog node of none); the rings' fog path is not built under our sky (two of their sixteen slots freed); **Haze detail** (Half on Low, Full from Medium, a tier key) works the terms at half size and lays each pixel from the nearest of four by distance (a pair against Full at Mos Eisley at 2 km visibility: 0.422% of pixels over 8 levels against two Full frames' own 0.414%). **`__debug.sky(hour)`** holds the day at dawn, noon, dusk or night (`src/sky/skyHours.ts`, held to `DayCycle.update` itself on both paths) and `sky()` hands it back, reporting the sky, the air, the haze and the discs; the dials are the debug menu's `sky` sliders (`src/sky/skyDials.ts`). **Seen** at Mos Eisley: a cold arrival held 225 pipelines when the screen went and 229 thirty seconds on (the base 8a7de51's 215 and 219), none built by a draw in play in the 30 s after it, over a 20 s walk, a travel to Naboo and a carried jump from Naboo's space to Tatooine's (one or two `MeshBasicMaterial`s never warmed are built by a draw on arriving in Naboo's space, on the base build alike); the haze's own pipelines (the plain composite, the air's, the half mode's terms and lay-on) and the dome's three (world, rooms, capture) all behind a screen; the far ridges hazed to the air's colour at 2 km. This maker's session read the air's sky view back all nought and so saw a black dome; neither the measurer nor the review could make that happen again (0 of 20,736 texels nought at the arrival, noon and dusk, in four sessions), and the pictures the owner should look at are the wave's last paragraph's, not this session's. Tested in node: `skyDraw.test.ts` (114 checks: the star table, the pair over a whole day of `DayCycle`, the hours, the floor, the dials, the row, the haze's arithmetic against three's own camera, the layer's blends, the graphs' text) and `skyDrawAlloc.test.ts` (the pair, the floor and the haze pass, 0.038 B a frame in 12 of 12 processes, the harness's floor).

**Pass 2's first wave, the air and the haze together: its measurer, its review and its fixer** (21 November, on `claude/three-187`, uncommitted on 8a7de51 with both makers' work; headless Chrome 157, WebGPU on Direct3D 12 on the NVIDIA Blackwell adapter, WebGL through ANGLE Direct3D 11, one game tab, a fresh profile; records in `A:\swg3js\port\scratch\p2w1-measure\` (its tables in `after.md`), `p2w1-review\` and `p2w1-fixer\`). **Measured** (no other game tab of ours, no other session's Chrome using CPU during any timing), at 1440p at Mos Eisley's street, clear, the hour held, medians: the sky view 0.023 to 0.026 ms of the card a frame and the haze volume's compute 0.028 (three's timer round the dispatch), so the air still is 0.051 to 0.055 ms (the design's 0.05 to 0.15), and 0.286 ms more on a frame the weather moves (multiple scattering 0.278, transmittance 0.008; the design's 0.3); the haze pass 0.031 on Low (half size) and 0.054 on High (the design's 0.1 to 0.3); our dome 0.026 (Low) and 0.08 (High) against SWG's 0.015 and 0.052; the card's whole frame ours against SWG's 0.907 against 0.821 ms on Low and 1.511 against 1.400 on High at noon. The main thread: `AirRun.frame` 2.6 to 4.7 µs (the design's 50), and `drawTables`, three's own overhead issuing the sky view and the compute, 0.09 to 0.12 ms, which the design's figure (the twin's alone) did not count. Pipelines at a cold Tatooine arrival, five sessions each way: 225 when the screen goes and 229 thirty seconds on against the base's 215 and 219 (the design feared 40 to 60 more: the fog node is set once, so it changed the code of every lit material without adding keys), none built by a draw in play there, over a walk, a travel to Naboo or a carried jump; the one or two `MeshBasicMaterial`s on arriving in Naboo's space are the base's too. The arrival's time is unchanged (18.75 against 18.79 s, three quiet pairs), its memory 50 MB more. WebGL: 390 programs a session against the base's 391, nothing the base lacks (the one the tree did not build is a points program that comes and goes between sessions, `testing.md`). **Reviewed**: the physics sound, every node suite of the sky passing, nothing allocated, the smoke panel unhazed before a hazed ridge, the rooms unhazed, the weather's visibility and the dials live and back, SWG's setting today's to the digit and in pictures, WebGL's programs and pictures the base's; and ten findings, mended or answered here. **Fixed by the wave's fixer**: three suites of `test:all` (`raceDials`, `tierLadder`, `themes`) pinned `main.ts` text the wave had moved, so the sky's dials now join the chain after the volumetrics' and the sky's read stands after the theme; **a clear Tatooine noon lit the world 1.85 times today's**, because the air's units matched the bare 2.4 rather than the key light today's ramps make (2.4 times the row's scale on its colour, 1.903): anchored there (`TODAY_NOON_KEY`), the sun reads 1.887 against today's 1.903 and an up-facing white surface 2.91 against 2.10 (the second sun's fill 0.85 and a sky and bounce physics makes brighter than SWG's ramps are the rest: the owner's to judge); **dawn and dusk were 8 times darker than the night**, the night floor letting go by the sun's height while the air's light there was a twentieth of today's night: the floor now also holds the sun's light and the sky's at no less than today's night brightness at every hour, in their own colours (`AIR.swgNight`, SWG's ramps at the night's middle), which binds only through the twilight: an up-facing surface at Mos Eisley reads 0.148 at dawn and 0.146 at dusk (today's 0.185 and 0.248, the first cut's 0.105 and 0.100) and at Naboo 0.092 and 0.092 (today's 0.132 and 0.086), the night today's exactly, a wall facing the low sun lit red at the night's level; dawn stays darker than the night because the night's moon stands high and the dawn's sun on the horizon, as in today's game; **the dusk sky was green**, the fit having turned the Rayleigh's channels apart and fitted the ozone to 2.4% of Earth's: the tint now moves the Rayleigh along its power law alone (an exponent of 2 to 6) and the ozone stays at three quarters of Earth's or more, so the dusk sky view near the zenith reads (0.005, 0.0033, 0.0133), blue four times green, and every converted world's fitted clear row has its twilight zenith blue over green (`duskprobe.ts`); the fog's colour the rain and wet surfaces read is held by night (Mos Eisley's night fog `1a1d31`, today's, where it was black); `__debug.sky(hour)` reports the sun at the hour it wrote (it was a frame behind) and says the air's numbers follow at the next frame; under SWG's sky the haze report no longer blames the Effects setting; the world's sky log line, a misplaced doc comment and a space in `test:all` are put back. **The haze volume is now held to a reference**: `AIR.tables.readHaze()` reads it back (its one copy draw made when asked, never in play, compiled before it draws) and `hazeProbe.js` holds every texel of a grid of 64 rays by 32 slices against the twin's direct sum along its own ray: at noon the light within 1.0% weighted (2.8% mean, 6.6% worst, at a slice 28 m out), at dusk 2.6% weighted, 5.4% worst, the transmittance within 0.0016. The tables against the reference at noon with the new fit: transmittance 0.14% mean (4.7% worst, at a texel of T 1.2% 70 km up looking under the horizon, where the strong fitted ozone turns it fastest), multiple scattering 1.7% weighted, the sky view 1.9% mean of the twin. The fixer's session (one tab, other sessions' work on the machine, so no timing): a cold arrival held 224 pipelines when the screen went and 228 thirty seconds on, none built by a draw in play there, through every hour, the travels to Kashyyyk and Naboo, or the probes; one at Naboo was Theed's hangar beside the console's teleport (`webgpu.md`, pass 1's seventh wave), not the air's. **The pictures for the owner's eye**: `A:\swg3js\port\scratch\p2w1-fixer\shots\<view>-<hour>-fixed.png` (Mos Eisley, Bestine, Kachirho, the Naboo view and the same spot looking level, at dawn, noon, dusk and night: 20) and the sheet `sheet-fixed.jpg`; SWG's at noon are the measurer's `p2w1-measure\shots\<view>-noon-swg.png`. **The owner's to decide**: Tatooine's noon sky now reads lavender blue (its dust's warm light over the deepest blue the power law allows), where SWG paints it tan and the first cut made it cyan, a hue no clear air makes, which the steer dial or wave 2's grade can carry; the noon's total light against today's (1.39 times at Mos Eisley, 1.42 at Naboo, where SWG's own noon is dimmer than Tatooine's and the air's single anchor makes them alike), Tatoo II's strength (0.45 of the caster) and the size of the sky's own light; the ozone fit stands at its top (four times Earth's) on Naboo, Kashyyyk, Endor, Rori, Lok and Yavin 4, which turns a low sun magenta-red there (Kachirho's dawn); and the Sky row's taking effect at the next start of the game rather than the next loading screen (the brief's default): the scene's one fog node and the rings' graph are the session's, so a live switch would re-key every render object. **Not seen**: Medium and Ultra, the M2 and the mid-range stand-in, the editor's previews, a hyperspace jump after the fixer's change, WebGL after it (nothing it changed runs there: the one line on WebGL's path is put back to the base's, and `SwgSky.lightingAt` is called by the air alone), and a timing after it (the floor is a few multiplications a frame, the night's ramp read only as the mix moves, the fit 1.5 s for Naboo's 95 rows against 1.7). `npm run test:par` passed after the last change: 833 suites in 17 minutes, `horizonEarly`, `skyDrawAlloc`, `itemsRelay` and `storiesServer` failing only under load and passing alone (`airOff` had failed its own one-minute bound at 447 s in a crowd of the fixer's earlier, its checks all passing).

**Pass 2's second wave, its maker A: the eye** (21 November, on `claude/three-187`, uncommitted on 13b7f82 beside maker B's reflections; the brief is `A:\swg3js\port\PASS2-W2.md`, the design's 2.8; headless Chrome 157, WebGPU on Direct3D 12 on the NVIDIA Blackwell adapter, one game tab, maker B's tab running beside it all the while, so no timing here counts; records in `A:\swg3js\port\scratch\p2w2-makerA\`). On WebGPU under our sky the exposure is measured and eased on the card (`src/sky/exposure/`): a pass of the chain after the edge smoothing and before the bloom (the registry's `exposure`) draws a quarter-size copy of the picture as log2 luminance (four bilinear taps a texel, a red half-float target), a compute counts it into 256 atomic bins over -26 to 10 weighted to the middle, and a compute of one invocation takes the 40th to 96th percentiles' mean, the full make-up toward the key, the dark-night rule (up to `knee` 2 stops made up whole, past it 0.7 of each stop and never more than 4 short, never more than `floor` 20 over the day's) and the ease (0.3 s into light, 0.65 s into the dark), into a buffer every reader reads on the card: the chain's output (before three's tone mapping, which keeps the player's Exposure as ±2 stops), the bloom's threshold, the grade's pivot, every emissive thing (the glow factor `2^(-glow·stops)`, `glow` 1: every lit classic material's emissive term and every unlit one's colour through a look part, the lava, marks, sabers, particles and tunnel through `withGlow`, the weather's rain left to the eye) and the leaf cards' 'client' curve; never read back in a frame. Where the card does not adapt (Eye adaptation off, Effects off) the main thread writes the sun's height's answer into the same buffer, and under SWG's sky, in space and on a world with no air an exposure of 1 (today's). The key (0.39) is the eye's own mean of Mos Eisley's street at a clear noon under the air over the 1.39 times today's light wave 1 found there, so the eye closes 0.475 stops at noon. Wave 1's night floor is gone whole; the main light aims at the brightest body up by what each sends through the air (`AIR.frame.lightDir`); the Graphics page has **Exposure** in stops and **Eye adaptation** on WebGPU (WebGL keeps its row; a factor saved before is carried into stops); `__debug.exposure()` reads the buffer back with its dials, and `({ check: true })` holds the card's frame to `eyeMath.ts`; `__debug.sky({ match })` saves and holds a frame by name in the address's storage and restores it in another tab, either renderer; the fit's ozone limits are dials (all worlds, 0.75 and 4 to start, and a world's own), refitting the world in the worker. **Seen**: the card's frame against the JavaScript over its own kept count within 1e-6 stops; a cold Tatooine arrival 228 pipelines at the screen's going and 228 thirty seconds on (227 and 227 in a second session; wave 1's 225/229), none by a draw in play; the street at noon at -0.474 stops; into the building by the cantina sign 3.06 stops darker, within a quarter stop of its target at 1.89 s and a twentieth of the step at 2.0 s (the knee taking 0.25), and out again within a quarter stop at 1.0 s; no lamp at the cap there (0.034% of pixels with a channel at 254 or more, none with all three, against the WebGL tab's 0.003% of a darker room); nights dark but readable, the ground about 20 m out at Mos Eisley a mean of 22 of 255 (5% to 95%: 0 to 34) and in the Naboo forest's riverbed 38 to 44 (0 to 81), stars and lamps unblown; the air's record and tables at Mos Eisley's noon the same as wave 1's to the digit (transmittance 0.137% mean, multiple scattering 1.66% weighted); the ozone's top moved to 2 and back refitting Tatooine in 0.7 s each way, its numbers back exactly, nothing built; the match saved on WebGPU restored on WebGL at the same frame (`cant-inside-gpu.png`, `cant-inside-webgl.png`). Found and mended on the way: the leaf cards under the 'client' curve, drawn as shown, blew white at the Naboo night's 2^17 and their bloom washed the frame; the dark-night rule's conditional write of a dial boxed 16 B a frame. Tested in node: `eye.test.ts` (86 checks) and `eyeAlloc.test.ts` (0.038 B a frame in 12 of 12), seven suites moved in their own words for the moved lines. **Not proved**: the passes' card time (maker B's tab never closed), WebGL's program list against base sessions (one WebGL session drew with no shader error), a jump. **Left**: Kachirho under its canopy at night stays black and the Naboo riverbed at 2^17 reads posterized, the half-float scene target holding little or nothing that dark (a scene stored pre-exposed would mend both); the eye's every dial and the key are the owner's to judge. Pictures: `p2w2-makerA\shots\<view>-<hour>-eye2.png`.

**Pass 2's second wave, its maker B: reflections of the real sky** (21 November, on `claude/three-187`, uncommitted on 13b7f82 beside maker A's eye; the brief `A:\swg3js\port\PASS2-W2.md`, the design's 2.3; headless Chrome 157 on the NVIDIA Blackwell adapter, one game tab of mine, maker A's tab running beside it except during the timings; scripts and pictures in `A:\swg3js\port\scratch\p2w2-makerB\`). On WebGPU the reflections' capture keeps the dome's 128 px cube and kept target (`DomeCapture`) and is now the sky as drawn (`src/sky/capture/skyCapture.ts`, made with the dome in `gpuEnv.ts` and reached as `GPU.rt.environment.sky`): the air's dome, its sun discs spread to at least a degree and capped at four times their own light a steradian in the capture alone (`airSky.ts`, `CAPTURE_TUNE.sunDeg`, `sunCap`), SWG's moons and pictures as `SwgSky.update` places them, a skybox where the sky has one, and in space the planets and the nebula sheets WebGPU draws (none until wave 3 gives the sheets node materials), each a mesh of its own over the live one's geometry and material, placed about the origin, with two lights of the capture scene's own copied from the world's sun and sky light. It is taken a face a frame by its own draws and filtered on a seventh, every `every` (4 s) or as soon as the light turns `sunMoveDeg` (0.5°); an ask (a sky hung, the reflections readied behind a screen, the source switched, an hour moved by hand) takes it all at once as before; `localStorage['swg.reflections'] = 'game'` still hands every surface the area's own cube (seen in Tatooine's space after a reload: held false, no face drawn) and `{ source: 'sky' }` puts the sky back. Dials on the debug menu's `reflections` sliders and `__debug.reflections({ tune })` (`src/sky/capture/captureTune.ts`): `every` 4, `sunMoveDeg` 0.5, `pace` 'faces' ('once' is the capture before), `sunDeg` 1, `sunCap` 4, each with its reason. **Seen**: the cube read back at a held noon at Mos Eisley's starport holds the air's sky as the base 13b7f82's does (the side faces' upper halves red over blue 0.75 on both, the zenith the same to three figures) and the tree's sun spot on top, its brightest texel 11.5 over about three texels where the base has none (0.54, the sky); at dusk the sun's side is orange-red on both (red over blue 2.39 and 2.38) and the rest blue-violet (0.41); with the cap lifted (`sunCap` 1000) the spot reads 2,643, and a real quarter-degree disc 0.54, falling between texels. A Naboo starfighter's nacelles at the starport, the same view on both builds, `shots\hull-{noon,dusk}-{base,tree}.png` (the tree's exposure is maker A's eye, so the pairs differ in brightness by design). In Tatooine's space the capture holds the nebula skybox and the planets (`shots\cube-space-tatooine-tree.png`; the base's six faces are one flat colour). **Pipelines**: a warm Tatooine arrival held 224 when the screen went and 228 thirty seconds on (none by a draw in play), the capture's own two (the dome and the bodies) built behind the screen; through night, dusk, dawn and noon, a 20 s walk, a travel to Naboo, Naboo's space and a carried jump to Tatooine's space, nothing built by a draw in play but the one `MeshBasicMaterial` on arriving in Naboo's space that wave 1 found on the base too. **Cost** (Mos Eisley's street, noon, no other game tab): a face 0.1 ms of the main thread and 0.005 ms of the card, the filter's frame 0.9 ms and 2.3 ms (three's WebGPU generator, 42 draws, the same filter the four-second capture ran before), so a refresh is about 2.3 ms of the card on its seventh frame, over the design's 1 ms; spreading the filter's levels over more frames is the way down, left to the lead. **WebGL**: three sessions of each build at Mos Eisley's arrival, street and cantina: no program the tree built that a base session did not (the base's twelve extra, a worn lens's and an eye film's, came in one session of three); street pictures base against tree 0.50 to 1.19% of pixels over 8 levels beside base against base 0.44 to 0.96%. Tested in node: `skyCaptureSteps.test.ts` (90 checks: the clock over the game's own day, the draws over a recording renderer, the copies' places to the bit, the cap's two readers, the dials, the world's wiring as text) and `skyCaptureAlloc.test.ts` (0.042 B a frame in 12 of 12 fresh processes, five at a time, the harness's floor). Not seen: a smooth surface (water, glass) catching the sun's spot on screen (the starfighter's metal is rough and showed no blown pixel either way), a hull in space, Medium and Ultra, the M2, the night pictures for the owner.

**Pass 2's second wave, the eye and the reflections together: its measurer, its review and its fixer** (22 November, on `claude/three-187`, uncommitted on 13b7f82 with both makers' work; headless Chrome 157, WebGPU on Direct3D 12 on the NVIDIA Blackwell adapter, WebGL through ANGLE Direct3D 11, one game tab, fresh profiles; records in `A:\swg3js\port\scratch\p2w2-measure\` (its tables in `after.md`), `p2w2-review\` and `p2w2-fixer\`). **Measured** (no other game tab of ours; another session's viewer Chrome used a third of a core during the first card window only), at 1440p at Mos Eisley's street, clear noon held, High: the eye's quarter copy 0.014 ms of the card, its count 0.043 and its ease 0.019, so the eye is 0.076 ms (the design's 0.05 to 0.1), and 0.0006 ms of the main thread with 0.061 to issue it; the capture a face 0.0065 ms of the card and its filter frame 2.28 ms (a refresh 2.32 ms, nearly all on the seventh frame: the design said about 1 ms spread; the base's four-second capture was 2.53 ms on one frame, so the spike stands and the faces saved only their own 0.006 ms each), its main thread about 0.11 ms a face and 1.9 to 2.3 ms on the filter's frame; the whole card frame without a refresh 1.537 ms mean against the base's 1.527, inside the noise. Pipelines at a cold Tatooine arrival (1600x900, sessions alternating): the base 225 when the screen went and 225 thirty seconds on (230 in a third), the tree 229 and 229 (233 in one, the queue's four), none built by a draw in play on either: +1 the eye's copy, +2 its computes, +2 the capture scene's materials, -1 the old capture's; in play nothing built by a draw over a walk, a travel to Naboo and a carried jump, the cantina's two and Naboo's space's one or two the base's own too. The cantina twice: into the room +3.03 stops, within a quarter stop at 1.67 and 1.71 s and a twentieth of the step at 1.97 and 1.99; out again within a quarter stop at 0.78 and 0.87 s; inside 0.018% of the pixels with a channel at the cap, none with all three. Nights, the ground 20 m out (of 255): Mos Eisley 14 to 17, Bestine 8 to 28, the Naboo forest 19 to 20 (looking level 46 to 48), Kachirho 0. WebGL: 390, 385 and 391 programs in three base sessions against 387, 391 and 386 in three tree sessions, the unions both 391 and nothing in one the other lacks, `fxCheck` clean; street pictures base against tree 0.19 to 0.68% of pixels over 8 levels beside base against base 0.39 to 2.05%. **Reviewed**: everything the makers proved reproduced, and eight findings. **Fixed by the wave's fixer**: **the scene is stored pre-exposed** (findings 1 and 2: the brief's "store and read what the exposure needs so a dark night is still readable", which both makers had left; `src/sky/exposure/preExpose.ts` and the lesson in `rendering.md`): every draw into a chain's scene target times last frame's eye exposure, rolled on the card once a drawn frame, held to 30,000, every reader of the scene's units (the eye's copy, the edge smoothing's history, the haze, the occlusion, the sanitize, the bloom, the grade, the output) dividing it back out; Kachirho's night from a frame mean of 5 to 23 of 255 (the upper box from one colour, black, to 5,667), the Naboo forest's night ground from 77% of neighbouring pixels equal and 48 colours to 25% and 972 with the halo round the player gone (the smoothing's history had lost every step and its clip's epsilon dwarfed the night: the review was right that it was the smoothing and the store both), the eye's lowest bin there 16% to 0.04%; at noon the store on against off 0.11% of pixels over 8 levels under a floor of 0.26%, the street's -0.474 stops, the cantina's step and times as before (+3.06 stops, a quarter stop in at 1.66 s and out at 0.78 s, a twentieth in at 2.09 s and out at 1.05 s, 0.020% of pixels inside with a channel at the cap and none with all three), Mos Eisley's night ground 24.7 of 255 (2 to 34), no pixel at the cap with all three in any of the twenty pictures; **a material that skips tone mapping** (bolts, holograms, trails, engine glows) takes no glow straight onto the canvas (finding 3: with Effects off at night it was drawn times 2^-14.8 with no exposure to undo it; `eyeGlowPart` reads the canvas for it as for the marks and blades); **a match on the same world** goes behind a loading screen of its own, the world settled round the spot, the room entered and the hold taken before the screen goes (finding 4: restored from Bestine into the cantina the frame matched the saved one to 0.21% of pixels over 8 levels on WebGPU, 0.31% on WebGL, where it had differed over 97.7%); **the eye during an Effects switch** follows the sun's height while the old chain draws on without its eye pass, never frozen (finding 5: `EYE_IN.eye`, seen through a switch at Mos Eisley's night that took 100 s to settle); **the old brightness's migration runs once** (finding 6: the stops are saved beside an old brightness kept for WebGL, so a nought chosen stays nought; `eye.test.ts` runs the game's own load and save). **Answered, not changed**: the dark-night rule's `most` decides every real night (finding 7: at the Naboo forest the full make-up is 20.5 stops, the rule makes up 16.5 where the share alone would make up 15.0, so nights are about 1.5 stops brighter than the share alone gives; both readings are dials, the owner's to choose); the leaf cards under the 'client' curve show SWG's own look at every hour (finding 8: dark against a dusk the eye has opened; lighting them from the air and letting them take the eye, or feeding their curve's light times the eye, is a design call for the lead and the owner);
