the capture's 2.3 ms on its seventh frame (spreading three's filter over frames would need the filter's levels drawn a frame at a time: the lead's call); a switch of the reflections from 'game' back to 'sky' in space raises "Destroyed texture [PMREM] used in a submit" on the base 13b7f82 too (the old target freed the frame it is replaced, `World.useEnvironment`). The fixer's session (one tab at a time, another session's viewer Chrome running, so no timing): a fresh-profile Tatooine arrival held 231 pipelines when the screen went and 235 thirty seconds on (the queue's four), none built by a draw in play (the first fresh profile after the change compiled every pipeline cold, 372.6 s, and held 250, its crowd longer stood: `webgpu.md`); over the cantina, the twenty pictures, the match and a switch three built by a draw in play, the cantina's two stand-in `ShaderMaterial`s and Theed's hangar beside the console's teleport, the base's both; one WebGL session at Mos Eisley's arrival, street and cantina held 386 programs, none outside the base sessions' union of 391, `fxCheck` clean, no shader error, its street picture inside the base's own spread. Tested in node: `eye.test.ts` (112 checks), the eye's allocation child in 12 of 12 processes at 0.038 B a frame (with the roll and the switch's flag in its frame), and 34 suites reading the files the fixer touched; `npm run test:par` (five at a time, below normal) passed after the last change, 837 suites in 17 minutes, none failing even under load. **The pictures for the owner's eye**: `A:\swg3js\port\scratch\p2w2-fixer\shots\<view>-<hour>-fixed.png` (the four views and the Naboo spot looking level at dawn, noon, dusk and night: 20) and the sheet `sheet-fixed.jpg`; the cantina `cant-inside-fixed.png`; the nights before and after `kachirho-night-{before,after}.png`, `naboo-night-{taa,notaa}-{before,after}.png`; the hull at dusk and noon is the measurer's `p2w2-measure\shots\hull-{noon,dusk}-eye.png`. **The owner's to decide**: every dial of the eye and the key; the dark-night rule's reading (finding 7); the leaf cards (finding 8); dawn and dusk, which the eye opening four to seven stops makes strongly magenta and violet everywhere (the physical twilight light, not a fault); Kachirho's noon under the canopy (the eye opens 4.5 stops and 4% of pixels reach the cap) and the Naboo view looking level at noon near white; Tatooine's noon sky against SWG's, by `__debug.sky({ match })`. **Not seen**: Medium, Low and Ultra, the M2, a hyperspace jump after the fixer's change, a bolt or a hologram on screen with Effects off at night (the glow's canvas read is held in node), a smooth surface catching the capped sun, a hull in space, a timing after the fixer's change (the roll is one compute of one invocation a drawn frame; every material's output gains a select, a multiply and a min).

**Pass 2's third wave, its maker A: the night sky and the flare** (22 November, on `claude/three-187`, uncommitted on 4ecee44 beside maker B's space; the brief `A:\swg3js\port\PASS2-W3.md`, the design's 2.7 and 2.9's flare; headless Chrome 157, WebGPU on Direct3D 12 on the NVIDIA Blackwell adapter, WebGL through ANGLE, one game tab, a fresh profile, maker B's tab closed through all of it; records, scripts and pictures in `A:\swg3js\port\scratch\p2w3-makerA\`). On WebGPU under our sky over a planet the night is drawn (`src/sky/night/`, made by the world once the air's tables exist and hung in the sky's group before it is built, `World.loadNight`; SWG's one-pixel star field is not made there, WebGL, SWG's sky and space keep it): **each system's stars** as soft cards 2 to 3 pixels across (`starCatalogue.ts`: 12,000 seeded by the system's name and drawn out of the galaxy's density about its place on the map, ranked by luminosity and distance and given a real sky's counts, 83 brighter than 2 and 659 than 4; the tier draws the brightest 2,000, 4,000, 8,000 or 12,000, its count changed with nothing built) at their physical light, shown only as far as a dark-adapted eye sees them against the air's sky and the towns' glow behind them (Schaefer's limit), twinkling toward the horizon, faded by the air's transmittance and, under storms, by one less the overcast; **the Milky Way** baked behind the loading screen for the system (`nightGpu.ts`: the density summed along each of 2048 by 1024 directions from the system's place, 128 steps, the dust in front dimming each, stored as the density's own sums in 8 MB of two half floats) from **the one density function** the galaxy map's disc is now drawn from (`galaxyDensity.ts`'s `densityOf` over numbers or over three's shading language, `GALAXY_TUNE`'s radius, arms and thickness with `MILKY_SHAPE`'s bulge, arms, clouds and dust; `galaxyView.ts` draws its disc with `sampleDisc`), at its physical brightness, its core toward the map's middle, baked again when the map's numbers move; **each city of `pois.json`** a warm glow low toward it (`cityGlow.ts`, levelling off near and inside a town); **the sky turning with the day's own sun** about the client's pole (`skyTurn.ts`, read off the air's sun, so the sun stands still among the stars) at a slant of each world's own (two seeded dials); by day nothing drawn. **The lens flare** on WebGPU under our sky (`gpuFlare.ts`: the WebGL pass's arithmetic on node graphs, 16 depth taps, through the eye) follows the sun discs and at night the moon (`flareSources.ts`), coloured by the air. `__debug.stars()` and the debug menu's `stars` sliders (`nightTune.ts`: star brightness 1, the eye's reach 0, twinkle 0.3 and 4 a second, Milky Way 1, town glow 1, the tiers' counts, the flare's moon 0.7 and its seen band 1 to 8, each world's tilt), with the line that keeps them; `stars({ off })` an A/B switch. **Seen**: a fresh-profile Tatooine arrival at Mos Eisley 25.1 s, 236 pipelines when the screen went and 236 thirty seconds on (wave 2's 231/235), none built by a draw in play; over a night's walk, a travel to Naboo, Naboo's space, a carried jump to Tatooine's space and the travel down, one built by a draw, the `MeshBasicMaterial` on arriving in Naboo's space that waves 1 and 2 found on the base; the band read back against the numbers within 0.08% at every tried texel, its brightest stretch 3.2° from the map's middle; no star through the cantina's roof at night (night on against off 0.014% of pixels over 8 levels, under the 0.032% of two like frames) or through a hill (the difference mask held to the sky); none by day; the flare at a dusk sun of 12° with both of Tatooine's suns open (its target lit, two starbursts, the ghosts), at night on the moon (open 1), and hidden by a Mos Eisley building at dusk (nought at four street spots, 0.999 at the street's open end). **Cost** (1584 by 805, Tatooine's night, only this tab, the machine at 15%): the night 0.04 to 0.07 ms of the world pass (0.111 and 0.123 on against 0.074 and 0.056 off; the design's 0.02 to 0.05), the flare 0.034 ms (occlusion 0.013, elements 0.007, composite 0.014; the budget 0.12), the bake 2.16 ms of the card once at arrival (the design's 5 to 20) and 0.2 to 0.4 ms of the main thread, a system's catalogue 50 to 190 ms of the main thread at its first arrival in a session (kept after). **WebGL**: two sessions at Mos Eisley's arrival, street and cantina, 402 and 382 programs, the second none outside wave 2's union of seven sessions, the first's eighteen more its crowd's and props' looks of that session alone; `fxCheck` clean, no shader error; the second's street picture differs from wave 2's first base session only in the cloud sheets' drift (hours apart), the player's pose and an animated sign (2.9% of pixels over 8 levels), the first's also over four props drawn with the looks that session alone built (6.5%); a `points` program of wave 2's tree sessions absent from both is absent from two of its three base sessions too. No same-hour pair against a base build was taken. Tested in node: `nightSky.test.ts` (111 checks: the disc against the density in cells, the band's quadrature against a fine sum, the core toward the middle from every system, a retune moving both, the graph built from the function, the catalogue's counts, the turn held to `DayCycle`'s own sun over a day, the towns, the flare's sources, every dial through its setter and the copied line, the tier read as `tierOf` reads it, the world's and the game's lines as text) and `nightAlloc.test.ts` (the night's frame and the flare's sources 0.042 B a frame in 12 of 12 after three finds: `rendering.md`); the 246 suites that read the files touched, three of which (`airWiring`, `gpuOff`, `horizonShaders`) pinned lines this wave moved and were mended and passed again, with every suite reading a line changed after that run passing too; `tsc` and the build clean. **The owner's to look at**: the band is faint at its physical brightness under SWG's moon, which is up all night, and from Tatooine a narrow streak (the map's disc thin, the system under its middle): `stars({ tune: { milkyWay } })` and `galaxy({ discThickness, lift })`; the stars few under the moon (`seeing`, `stars`); the towns' glow; the moon's flare. **Not proved**: Medium, Low and Ultra by eye, the M2, 1440p timings, a same-hour WebGL picture pair against a base build, Hoth's sky (its heroic holds a blizzard), the space zones' stars (they keep their skybox and field).

**Pass 2's third wave, its maker B: space** (22 November, on `claude/three-187`, uncommitted on 4ecee44 beside maker A's night sky; the brief `A:\swg3js\port\PASS2-W3.md`, the design's 2.9 and 2.12; headless Chrome 157 on the NVIDIA Blackwell adapter, one game tab of mine, maker A's suites and another session's Chrome running beside it; scripts and pictures in `A:\swg3js\port\scratch\p2w3-makerB\` and `A:\swg3js\port\shots\p2w3-makerB\`). **The nebulae draw on WebGPU**, which drew none before: the sheets (both looks), the haze inside one and the lightning bolts are node graphs of ours registered as their `ShaderMaterial`s' stand-ins (`src/core/gpu/gpuNebulae.ts`, through `standIns.nebula`), one graph a kind reading each material's own uniforms at each draw, every number the GLSL's own, their light times the eye's glow factor (in space the eye stands at an exposure of 1, so there they are WebGL's numbers) and the scene stored pre-exposed as every node material is; each nebula is placed from its own middle (a sheet's attributes hold its centre less that middle and its row, and a table of one texel a nebula holds each middle less the eye, worked in doubles each frame), the bolts from their ends less the eye, the haze by three's own model-view, so no place measured from the world's middle reaches the card, and the reflections' capture draws them as they are drawn. **Kessel's Maw**: `npm run swg -- space` reads every terrain's `DIST` forms (`parseSpaceDistant`, `distantEntry` in `tools/swg/space.mjs`) and writes them into `space.json` as `distant` in the game's frame, each effect converted into the pack; `status` asks again for a pack without the list. On WebGPU the world prepares their effects behind the loading screen and places them (`src/space/distant.ts`, `World.loadDistant`), moving each that rides with the camera with it every frame; WebGL draws none, as before. Dials (`__debug.distant(...)`, the line to paste back in its answer): `pullTo` 6000 m (a riding system stands 145 to 450 m off in the file; pulled out to the nebulae's own pull at the file's angle it stands behind every ship and station nearer; 0 the file's place), `on`, `starfields` off (the client's particle star fields, six or seven a zone in nine zones, carried but not drawn: the zone's own stars already draw and nobody has seen them together; the next zone), `keepWithin` 12000 m (how far one is kept running; the next zone). **Seen** (Kessel served from this maker's scratch conversion, the packs on disk carrying no `distant` yet): the abnormality nebula's sheets at the player's opacity 0.1, there with the nebulae shown and gone hidden, standing where WebGL draws them; the three black holes' particle streamers lying exactly over the skybox's painted vortices (`kessel-view-gpu-maw-on.png` against `-off.png`), no pixel clipped and 12.6% of the frame over 240 in a channel once they have filled. **Pipelines**: on a route of Play on Tatooine (214 when the screen went), a travel to Kessel (156), a carried jump to Kashyyyk's space, a carried jump back and a travel home, none built by a draw and no GPU error. **Cost** (Kessel, three's timer spans, interleaved twice, under the load above): the Maw at its steady state about 0.08 ms of the card's world pass and 0.17 ms of the main thread's particles; the sheets 0.008 to 0.024 ms of the card. drawn there from the sky pack's picture, a grey disc until converted) and kept in his file of choices only when he has any. **The viewer drawn as the game does** from the ground: SWG's bodies at SWG's places alone (no night sheet, no invented place, no system's bodies) and each held to the game's `seenMost`. **SWG's fallbacks** named in the placeholders (`SWG_FALLBACKS`). **Proved**: with no file, with one naming no world, and after a file named Naboo and the next did not, every world's record, column densities, multiple scattering, sky radiance and fitted rows the base's (0746ccd) to the bit in node, and Rori's four tables read back off the card bit for bit with no file, a file naming no world and the full file; a second session in the same profile read the look from storage (35 ms, nothing baked); the release build (`SWG3JS_NO_PRIVATE=1`) in a private window read the file and its held bodies, wore SWG's picture on the first screen and the twin on the next, and asked for nothing but the file and the player's own converted content; the viewer's held bodies edited through its own controls, saved by Save to game into scratch and read back by the game's reader in node and in that release build; Tatooine at dusk drawn as the game does showing one moon, held (gain 0.74); 103 of 103 looks' prints unchanged. **Found, not mended**: on a cold shader cache a recipe kit's five pipelines take far longer than a screen's budget on this card: the first fresh-profile arrival held its screen some 115 s on the bake (30 s on the kit's ask, 85 on the bake's draws); now the kit is asked at the screen's start and a look whose kit has not landed within the budget waits for the next screen, but the warm-up's pipelines are not the ones the bake's draws use (keyed apart by the game's compile, cause not found), so the next screen's bake still stalls it 37 to 81 s once, after which a bake is 29 ms and a stored look 35 ms; **Pass 2's fourth wave, part b, its maker B: the ground sky** (22 November, on `claude/three-187`, uncommitted on 0746ccd beside makers A's and C's; the brief `A:\swg3js\port\PASS2-W4B.md`, part B, the design's 2.5, 2.6, 2.7 and 2.12; the viewer's calls keep the square, bit for bit), High's 12 steps 1.45% of the ground path over the limb's profile (wave 4a's square: 3.45%), Ultra 0.73%, Medium 3.74%, a grazing ray 6.0% and 4.2% (`editor.md`). **The Milky Way after the scenery**: render order 900 in the opaque list, tested against the depth on the far plane (`bandClip`), the bodies after it (`rendering.md`); `status` asks again for a sky without them). **Held bodies in the game** (`heldBodies.ts`, `SwgSky.hangHeld`, `World.loadHeld`): Endor's Death Star at SWG's stage 1 and placement, or as the Save to game's `held` key says (its picture, yaw, pitch, size, roll and light), any of SWG's sky sprites the same way from the world's pack or the shared one, standing still, drawn Painted from SWG's picture through the air (pale by day); `skyBodies()` names each. **Mustafar's plumes** (`groundDistant.ts`, `World.loadGroundDistant`): wave 3's `DistantSet` way, prepared behind the screen, the file's small frame read at 160 m a unit (`PLUME_TUNE.scale`, a dial: the cross of four stands round the playable middle, 4 to 10 km out), `plumes({ show })` an A/B. **Seen** (the scratch conversion served by request interception in place of the pack's, nothing written under `assets-private/` or `public/`): arrivals at Tatooine 239 and 243 pipelines thirty seconds on, travels to Rori, Endor, Mustafar and home, none built by a draw in play, no GPU error; the night's coverage on Tatooine's fixed moon and on Naboo from Rori (`moon-dark-side-*.png`, `rori-night-naboo-*`); the band stopping at a ridge (`diff-band-ridge.png`); wave 3's moon rings against the store and the dither (`sheet-dark-sky-rings.png`); the plumes on and off (`sheet-mustafar-plume-onoff.png`); WebGL: an arrival with `fxCheck` clean, no shader error, none built in play, the plumes' hook never installed. **Pass 2's fourth wave, part b, its maker C: space and the map** (22 November, on `claude/three-187`, uncommitted on 0746ccd beside makers A's and B's; the brief `A:\swg3js\port\PASS2-W4B.md`, part C, the design's 2.6, 2.9, 2.10 and 3; Chrome 157 on the NVIDIA Blackwell adapter through Direct3D 12, one game tab, the other makers' tabs running beside it all the while, so no timing here counts; scripts, records and pictures in `A:\swg3js\port\scratch\p2w4b-makerC\`). **Space rims** (`src/space/spaceRims.ts`, `spaceAir.ts`, through the runtime as `GPU.rt.spaceRims`, hung by `World.loadSpaceBodies` and framed after the sky group is on the camera): every planet of a zone with air gains a Painted and a Scattered shell beside it, both built behind the screen; Scattered (High 12 steps, Ultra 16, Medium under a quarter of the screen) marches the planet's air by the viewer's march (`airProfile.js`, the bodies' `BODY_LAY`) and draws the planet itself in the same pixel, lit as its Lambert sphere is (the main light through the air, the hemisphere, the fill), the sphere standing down meanwhile; Painted (Low, Medium over a quarter) lays the limb's own light round the planet's sphere (`rimProfile`, 32 heights marched once a record on small tables, 3 to 17 ms), the sphere made 128 by 64 where it has air. **the volumes** (`src/space/spaceVolumes.ts`, `nebulaForm.ts`): a zone's gas looks (his file's, else the viewer's own start, Kessel's Maelstrom round the Maw at its lore place) marched by `volume.js` at its baked Medium (Low below High), never its live High, into a half-size picture by a camera at the origin, laid over by spheres hung 4 km out, read through the eye; the planets end the gas as the viewer's stage stands them; every piece built behind the screen whatever the row says. **The volumes' mends** (`volume.js`): the Maelstrom's near-white core (the shoulder's grey held, the hot edge warmed: the ring's brightest 2% from sRGB 234, 218, 202 to 232, 207, 174 at Kessel), the near side of a veil wider than about 72 degrees thinned (`NEAR_FADE`), and three allocations of the frame gone; the gas's own lightning left out in the game (`noStorms`) until `weather.js`' storm step reads a kept record. **The map's globes** (`src/ui/galaxyLooks.ts`, `galaxyView.ts`, `galaxyMap.ts`): each system's main world's look baked behind every loading screen at 1024 after the screen's own bakes (`bakeMapLooks`), read in the map by `GpuLooks.baked` alone, halved to 512 and laid on the map's WebGL renderer with a turning cloud shell and a painted rim (`globeRimMaterial`, `paintedRimAt`'s rule), SWG's pictures where none is baked; **Pass 2's fourth wave, part b: its checker and its fixer** (22 November, on `claude/three-187`, uncommitted on 0746ccd with the three makers' work; headless Chrome 157, WebGPU on Direct3D 12 on the NVIDIA Blackwell adapter, fresh profiles; the checker's tables in `A:\swg3js\port\scratch\p2w4b-check\after.md`, the fixer's records and pictures in `A:\swg3js\port\scratch\p2w4b-fixer\`). **Measured by the checker** (another session's Chrome at about three cores through every cost run, so every timing is noisy; costs are the world pass over the same blocks with the cards off, ms): a moon at night under High +0.04 on the card (nothing measurable); Naboo from Rori Painted on every tier 0.02 to 0.07 on both builds alike (the look-wearing body was drawn Painted then: see below); Tatooine filling the screen from orbit Painted 0.03 to 0.04 (Low, Medium), Scattered 0.40 (High) and 0.55 to 0.58 (Ultra), inside the design's 0.6 to 1.2; the night with the Milky Way after the scenery 0.078 against the base's 0.102, the dither +0.005 on the output pass; Kessel facing the Maw Sheets +0.002, Volumes +0.24 (its half-size march); the Death Star at stage 1 +0.006. Pipelines when the screen went: a Tatooine arrival 239 with no file and 243 with one (base 240 to 244), Rori 209/218, Endor 214/225, Mustafar 217/228, Naboo's space 175/186 (base 169), Kessel 180/190 (base 169), none built by a draw in play on any player path but space's known `MeshBasicMaterial` (the base builds it too); WebGL's program union 391 against the base's 391, nothing in one and not the other; every allocation suite at the harness's floor. **Fixed by the fixer**: (1) **the cold bake's minute** was three's own quad: `QuadMesh.render` swaps a vertex stage of its own in for each draw (a triangle from the vertex index, no vertex buffer) and puts the material's back, so the warm's `compileAsync`, which takes the material as it stands, built a program reading a position buffer, keyed apart from the draw's; a kit's materials now carry three's draw-time stage as their own (`generate.js`' `quadVertexNode`, read off three's quad by a stand-in renderer), and the warm points the height passes' pictures at the height target first as the bake does. On the select screen the kit landed in 10.8 s and the bake's draws built 4 pipelines in 20 s before; after, 0 built and 71 ms (a second look: landed 9.1 s on its own clock, baked in 17 ms). A cold profile's first Rori arrival with the file then took 19.1 s (the checker's 38.2 and 30.8; base 14.7), Naboo worn from its bake in 30 ms, the next screens 11.7 s (Endor), 9.7 s (Mustafar), 3 s into Naboo's and Kessel's space, the map and back to Tatooine 12.7 s, with no lost tab, no GPU error and none built in play; the second session in the profile baked nothing (every look read back). (2) **A body wearing a look is a sphere**: `skyBody.ts` reads `skyLookOf` as each body is made, sets its card's sphere to 1 and its own reflectance (the card's `a5.w`, the look's albedo 1; SWG's pictures keep `BODY_TUNE.sphereAlbedo`) and lets its tier choose Scattered (Naboo from Rori now reads "scattered, as a sphere, wearing a look"); and a record's write no longer sets `a5` whole, which had halved every air body's light and taken half its cover from its picture's alpha. (3) **The looks' memo** lets a look's pictures go once its reader has made what it draws (`GpuLooks.release`, `BakeCache.dropBytes`, only where the browser's storage holds them), and the map keeps its globes halved and toned behind the screen (`galaxyLooks.ts`' `GLOBES_HAD`, about 1 MB a system), under a budget of its own after the screen's bakes: after the route the memo held 0 MB and the globes 1.5 MB, where the checker read 22 MB for two looks. (4) The space planets and the globes wear the look toned as its layers tone it, as the sky's disc does (`lookPictures.ts`' `layLatLong`, `toneLayer`); `presetLook` is typed. (5) The gates: the volumes' build guarded on WebGPU (`gpuOff`), and the pins of `gpuOff`, `skyCaptureSteps` and `horizonShaders` moved in their own words over the makers' lines; `skyLooks.test.ts` joins `test:sky`. **The rims from Rori and from orbit**: Naboo's card on Rori and its shell in orbit are handed the very same record (a0 to a4 to the seventh figure) and the file's haze moves both alike (grey to 6 km #d09060: the haze's scattering 0.061 to 0.607, its tint 1, 0.44, 0.19 on both); a picture on matched geometry (one sun angle to the limb) was not made. **Not reproduced**: the pipeline the checker saw built after console steps at Rori (sixteen steps one at a time, none built). **Moved to a later wave**: the viewer's "Draw as the game does" with the game's night sky and the haze on solids, the pictured clouds' meridian streaks, the belt rocks without the planet's shadow, his imported pictures in the game in development, a space place's own position and size in the Save to game (a volume stands at its lore start), the gas's lightning in the game (`weather.js`' `Storms.step` takes numbers each call), and a world's rim from its fitted record (the body path reads the world's base record with the file's dials; the ground's own sky reads the fitted one). **Not measured**: Naboo from Rori now Scattered at High (the checker's costs were of its Painted card), and every timing above after the fixes but the screens' lengths. **Waits for the lead**: `npm run swg -- sky @SWG all assets-private --retail-only` with the dev server stopped (Endor's Death Star, Mustafar's plumes and the shared sky pictures).

**The interface rewrite's first wave, the block** (on `claude/hud`, cut from main; the brief is `A:\swg3js\hud\HUD-BRIEF.md`, items 1 to 5, its first maker's half; item 6, the weapon mark, and the wave's proof are the second maker's). Built: **the top-right box is gone** (the clock, the frame rate, the weather note and the strip naming the map's, inventory's and spawner's keys); **three switches on the Interface page's new "The corner"**: the frame rate (off; the number alone at the top right, `62 fps`, a small drop shadow), the weather (on; a word or two under the minimap, in its place with the minimap off, never in space or a dungeon, a muted "· held" while held from the Graphics page or forced or its clock moved from the console, never for a place's own weather) and the clock (off; just above the minimap's coordinates, muted, or above `/loc` with no minimap); **the weather's words** as a pure rule (`src/ui/weatherWords.ts`) over what the weather now tallies of its mix (`FallTally` in `weatherSchedule.ts`: each kind's share and the strength carrying the most of it, so the word changes at a crossfade's half-way mark); **"The bars and the action bar"**: where the block stands (left, middle as before, right; the sides mirrored, the weapon mark's box at the screen's edge with the bars beside it, the action bar lowest; with the block on the left the message line goes to the bottom right), the Force bar laid over health's edge or inside it, and on its lower or upper edge; **the bars without words or numbers**, placed by one pure layout (`barLayout`): health 6 thick (a dial), muted down to 40% and the dark red at and below it, eased, a ripple of red below 30%; Force against it at 75% (sides, flush with the outer end) or 60% (middle, centred); stamina attached to Force as Force is to health, in Force's place with no Force; breath bright blue on top of the top-most bar, flashing white below 30%; health and Force going 1.5 s after full and back the moment they drop, every bar fading over 0.2 s in a place kept for it, so nothing moves. Every number is in `HUD_SIZES` and a slider under `hud` in the debug menu (`src/ui/hudDials.ts`); `__debug.hud()` reports the block, the bars, the readouts and the old note's whole line (`held`). The display's steady frame was made to box nothing (a share and the frame's seconds handed to calls boxed 16 to 48 B in five of twelve processes; now fields). Tested in node: `hudBars.test.ts` and `weatherWords.test.ts` new, ten suites mended for the wordless bars and the gone box. **Item 6, the weapon mark** (the wave's second maker): the two hand cells, picture and name, are gone, and the block's mark box holds our own mark for what the hands hold, slightly see-through, no name (`src/ui/weaponMark.ts`, the `ic-mark-*` marks in `src/ui/hud.svg`, `Hud.readMark`): a lightsaber at its stance's tilt (fast 45° to the right, medium 22.5°, strong upright), the double-bladed saber at 45°, two sabers crossed in an X, a blaster by its kind (pistol, carbine, rifle, heavy weapon), every other class its own mark (sword, knife, two-handed sword, polearm, fist weapon, thrown, instrument, a dancer's prop), the fists for bare hands, and any other pair two marks at a third of the box set diagonally with a muted slanted line between them. The blade on the mark draws in and out with the real one at the blade's own timing (`SaberBlade.openSeconds`, read through `Player.bladeSeconds`), a switch on the Interface page that starts on (**The mark's blade follows the saber**); the stance turns the mark by one property and the blade is one class, so nothing is written in a steady frame. Every number of it is a slider under `hud` in the debug menu (**The weapon mark**), and `hud({ health: 0.35 })` holds health at a share for looking at the bars. `weaponMark.test.ts` new (81 checks, the rule over every pair the hands can hold, the stylesheet, the writes, the switch, the blade's timing as the blade runs it, and 0.114 B a frame in 12 of 12 processes); the 515 suites that read a file either maker touched pass. **Seen in a browser** (WebGL, a headless Chrome at Mos Eisley's street; screenshots in `A:\swg3js\hud\shots\`, not yet seen by the owner): every alignment with both Force looks on both edges, health at full, 35% and 25%, each stance lit and drawn in, the switch off, each kind of weapon the rack can hold, two sabers, a saber and a knife, a sword and a knife, riding a speeder, a ship's controls (the block stands down), under water at Theed with the breath low and flashing, and H twice; `__debug.hud().writes` read 0 in a steady second in every one of those but under water, where the draining breath bar writes about ten times a second, by design. Guessed and the owner's to judge: a plain fog (every fog the game holds) is Misty under level 3 and Foggy from it; Storm needs rain with lightning, which no world holds together; a fall's word wins over level 0's Clear (Corellia's forests say Windy, Mustafar's hot areas Dusty); breath in the inside look stands against health's top edge, wholly outside it; breath's length is a share of the bar it sits on; the god-mode and held-reach notes, which shared the old corner line, are now only the console's; and every size, colour step and time above. The mark's, guessed: an empty right hand shows what the game draws there (the Jedi's stand-in saber in its stance, the Bounty Hunter's stand-in blaster of the last kind held), so the fists are the bare-hands switch alone; a saber in a pair stands at 45° (each half of the X's tilt); a pair keeps the right hand's mark top left and the left hand's bottom right at every alignment, unmirrored; the X leans the right hand's saber to the left; the mark is the palette's ink at three quarters, the split line `muted`; the stance eases over 0.15 s; and a thrown weapon's mark is drawn but cannot be seen in play, since nothing thrown is held. **The wave's fixer**, after its review: each bar in the block is now a layer of its own (`isolation`), since the shared bar's fill and quarter marks painted over every bar, which hid the inside look's Force under health and showed health's fill through the laid-over Force's empty half (seen at health 95%, which no shot of the makers' had; seen mended in WebGL in both looks at either side and in the middle, `fix-*-hp95` in the shots folder); the weather's line says Clear at once with the Graphics page's Weather off (it said Overcast and then Cloudy for about ten seconds as the level walked down; `weatherLineWord`); the block's inset from the side, the ripple's starting strength, the frame rate's type and drop shadow and the weather word's type are sliders now (`blockSide`, `rippleStrength`, a new **The corner** group: `fpsSize`, `fpsShadowY`, `fpsShadowBlur`, `weatherSize`; the weather's line keeps its 13 whatever its type, so the editor's dock sum holds), and every look the display tells the page is held to have one; and a check in `hudBars.test.ts` that could never fail now measures the frames after a move of the block.

**The interface rewrite's second wave, the action bar and the use prompt** (on `claude/hud`, built on ec8350c; the brief's items 7 and 8, its first maker's half; items 9 to 11 and the wave's proof are the second maker's). Built: **the action bar** is one bar of `plate` with slightly rounded corners, a cell an ability: the mark (20 px, from 28) with the key it is on under it, inside the bar (cells 32 by 36, from 40 square), and no numbers (the cost and the saber cell's on and off are gone; the blade's cell lights while the blade is out; the name still shows on hover); a thin line between cells at 80% of the bar's height, or its full height by the Interface page's new **Lines between the cells**. A press darkens the cell (in over 80 ms, back over 200); a cooldown darkens it at once and its own colour fills back up from the bottom over the cooldown's time, then a short brighter flash (a third of a second, a quarter brighter). The fill is one CSS animation whose length the cell reads off the share's own fall, written once at its start and again only when it drifts more than a quarter second from the kit's (`HUD_TUNE.coolSlack`), held while the game stands still (`Hud.setLive`, `paused` on the bar); the turning wedge and its fifty writes a second are gone. **The use prompt** is one rounded see-through bar (the panel at half strength) of every action on offer, the same lines between them, the key in a soft cap, the words in ink at 85% with no outline; it fades in over 150 ms and out over 200, its width eases to its row's (a `ResizeObserver`), and its words cross-fade between two faces; aboard, in a vehicle, at a ship's controls, a passenger or in a jump it is quieter (`promptQuiet`: 60%, nine tenths the size) and raised to 168 px, clear of the ship's condition, which the browser shows 63 tall and reaching 159 (the stylesheet had it at 145, so the old bar sat 4 px into it); it stays centred wherever the block stands. The long **Full prompts** line takes the same look (moved up from 190 to 200 to clear the quieter bar), and **Blur behind the prompt** (off) blurs the world behind both. Twenty-eight new sizes, strengths and times in `HUD_SIZES`, each a slider under `hud` (**The action bar**, **The use prompt**); `hud().onFoot.kit` reads the bar in words and `hud().actions` the prompt's `on`, `quiet` and `width`. Seen in a headless Chrome (WebGL) at Mos Eisley: every state's steady second writes 0 (on foot, by a speeder, riding, at a fighter's controls, the switches on, hidden), a cooldown's fill read its 4.00 s exactly and held under the open map. Not yet seen by the owner; the creator, the crosshair and grapple, and the colour themes are the wave's second maker's.

**The interface rewrite's second wave, its second half: the character list and the creator, the crosshair and the grapple, the colour themes** (on `claude/hud`, the brief's items 9 to 11, and the wave's proof over both halves). Built: **the character list and the creator** show nothing of play (no place block or minimap, no bars, weapon mark or action bar, no crosshair), only their own panels and the frame rate while its switch is on, by the hide key's own rule (`#ui` wears `ui-stage`, `HUD_STAGE_CLASS`; one selector for both classes, so they keep the same windows; the frame rate over the list's dark stage by a z-index of its own; `Hud.stageFrame` writes its line there). **The creator's fault is mended**: a place still standing up when the creator was left (Characters, then Play) finished afterwards and took every frame, so the character stood at Lars Homestead's 1194, 0, -1929 and teleports did nothing; every wait of `showScene` now asks whether it is still wanted (`sceneTurn`), and one left behind puts its place down. **The crosshair's** lines run 7 to 11 px (they ran 9 to 14); **the grapple's diamond** is 6 out and 1.4 thick (12 and 0.8), inside them, its colours as they were; **the rope ring** is drawn like the charge ring (a faint full circle in `muted` with the filled arc on it, at the charge ring's size, amber past half the longest rope) and steps out by its own width while a shot charges. **Colours** (Esc, Interface) picks one of the forty-two themes of the owner's examples page (`src/core/themes.ts`, in its groups, Default the palette as it stands, kept with the settings): a theme is its eighteen written on the root, so every panel, the display, the menus and the loading screen's words take it at once with no reload; the overlay reads the palette again, and what had copied a colour of its own hears of it (`paletteChanged`): the hull flash, the space map's materials and grid, the galaxy's rings and routes, the names over other players' heads. The colours still written into the interface's code are the palette's now: the map window's layers (a station `component`, a hyperspace point `good`, the launch point `ink`, a field `muted`, a nebula `pool`, the group `armour`, ships `accent`), your own mark there and the galaxy's ring round where you are (`chassis`), the map's shades and grid, the names over heads, the message line's plain look and the flight cross's glow, and (the wave's fixer, after the review found them navy under a red theme) the lit wells behind an item's picture and the creator's backdrop, which an earlier pass had kept off the palette on purpose as "a light rather than a surface" and are now the palette's light let into its dark (`--well-pic`, `--well-creation`); the travel curtain's black is the one colour left typed into either stylesheet; the world's own stay (the loading picture, the map pictures, the dyes, a preview's lights, the galaxy's globes). `themes.test.ts` holds every theme to the examples page's own check and Default to the palette; `crosshair.test.ts` reads the strokes off a stand-in overlay. Twelve new numbers in `HUD_SIZES`, each a slider under `hud` (**The crosshair and the grapple**); `hud({ theme })`, `hud().theme`, `hud().stage`. Seen in a headless Chrome (WebGL) at Mos Eisley: the fifty-four states of both waves' proof (every alignment and both Force looks on both edges, health full, 35% and 25%, each saber stance, each kind of weapon and two pairs, the action bar, the crosshair, the diamond, the rope ring, seven themes, by a speeder, riding, at a fighter's controls, H twice), each steady second 0 writes but under water at Naboo, where the draining breath bar writes by design; all forty-two themes' steady seconds 0; the list and the creator with the frame rate up and nothing else of play; the creator's fault reproduced, then gone with Back pressed three seconds into the place's load and again with it fully up. The wave's fixer: on the list and the creator the frame rate stands at its own place, 12 down and 88 in from the right (`stageFpsTop`, `stageFpsRight` under **The corner**), since at play's 12 in it sat on the list's "1 of 5" on a window under about 720 high (measured clear by 12 px or more from 853 by 480 to 2560 by 1440 at every scale, and clear of the creator's words); and the use prompt's cap (its size, side room, corners and type), the words' type and their gap from the cap, and a lit action bar cell's glow are sliders now, nine numbers that had been typed into the stylesheet (`capW` and `capH` were in the table but moved nothing). Not yet seen by the owner, nor a shot charged while hanging on the rope (held to the node suite).

**The interface rewrite, after the owner's look** (20 November, on `claude/hud`). He tried the rewrite and called it great, and asked for a way to keep the bars from fading so he could size them and judge them: **Keep the bars up when full** (Esc → Interface → The bars and the action bar, `hudBarsKept`, `hud({ barsKept })`, off to begin with) keeps health, Force, stamina and breath shown full instead of going away after the linger, one write each way and none while they stay (`hudBars.test.ts`). The linger and the fade's speed stay the debug menu's sliders (`fullLinger`, `barFadeSeconds`). With it the rewrite goes to main and `claude/animations` on his word.

**Animations from Mixamo** (Animations I, on `claude/animations`, wave 1; the design is `A:\swg3js\anim\DESIGN.md`). Built: a developer's importer (`npm run anims -- import "<folder>"`, `tools/anim/README.md`) that turns the owner's Mixamo FBX files into a clip pack of 1.5 MB committed under `public/anims/mixamo/` and shipped with every build (81 clips: his 77 files and a mirrored twin of four one-sided strafes), so players download nothing more and the FBX files are never committed; a fitter that puts the clips on every two-legged species' skeleton as its rig loads, with the pelvis level (the old way of lining the two rest poses up tipped the hips 32 degrees back) and with no clip ever moving the body; and the brief's "Generic" swaps, for every class: treading water when still in the water, the flying loop adrift on foot in space, the long fall and the flat impact when thrown from a wrecked vehicle (moving, jumping, the postures, the fight, the ability slots, the grapple and the emotes held until the impact has played, then lying prone as before; dying, being stood elsewhere or falling into water while thrown ends it), the jump's start and the falling loop in the air (the floating loop after a flip, a Force jump or a wall move), and a landing by how fast the body came down: none under 3.6 m/s, the short one under 9.8 (every hop: a plain one comes down at 5.7 m/s, a Force user's at 7.4 to 9.6 by how long the tap was, through the game's own movement at 20 to 240 frames a second), the longer one from there up (a Force user's harmless drops from about 2.4 m; a body with no Force is hurt from 1.7 m and lands those in the hard one), the hard one whenever the fall hurt, a held move key ending it after 0.35 s into whatever the body is doing by then. Jedi Academy's own jump, in-air pose and landing stay while blocking or fighting, as the owner asked, and a roll and a Force jump's harmless landing are Jedi Academy's too; a flip, a Force jump and a wall move open with a moment of the plain jump's start (about a tenth of a second), since how long the key will be held is not known as it goes down, which is the owner's to accept or not. Walking out of water plays what it always did on the shore (a moment of Jedi Academy's own landing) and no imported one, and a held blaster's aim correction is held while an imported clip has the arms. Every number of feel is a slider under `anim` in the debug menu (`__debug.anim({ tune })`), `__debug.anim()` reports the pack, each skeleton's fit and the player's pose against the bind pose, `anim({ play })` plays any of the 81 on the player by its id or its file's name, `__debug.files()` lists the pack's files among the session's, and `localStorage['swg.mixamo'] = '0'` and a reload are the game before it, but for one mend that holds either way: a body thrown from a wreck that falls into water, dies or is stood elsewhere no longer lies down prone where it next comes to ground. Seen only in headless tabs of ours on Direct3D 11, in numbers and thirteen screenshots (`A:\swg3js\anim\shots\`), never by the owner: a human male and a Wookiee (both Jedi) each trod water with the hips 35.0 to 36.2 degrees off the bind pose, which is the source's own lean and nothing added (the game's own idle stands them 10.8), drifted in the flying loop, jumped into the jump's start and the falling loop, landed with nothing from 0.3 m, the short landing from 1.2 m, the longer one from 2.6 to 9 m and the hard one from 14 m (27 health), each landing ending in the idle with both ankles within 2 mm of where they rest, floated after a Force jump and landed it in Jedi Academy's own landing, kept Jedi Academy's jump behind the block, and thrown from a wrecked speeder fell in the long fall, hit in the impact with the keys held 1.2 s (the body moved nought with W held) and crawled once freed; a human female Bounty Hunter's hop came down at 5.7 m/s into the short landing, and her drop of 4 m, which hurts her where it does not hurt a Jedi (19 health), into the hard one; no program was built in play by any of it, and the ten clips were fitted in 4 to 6 ms inside the rig's load. Its review round's mends (fingertips, a one-off's anchor, the impact's flat landing with no footsteps, a thrown fall ended by a death or by water, the slots held in the impact, no landing on the shore, a landing's footsteps folded, the aim's correction held, the pack's files in `files()`, a landing cut into the run, the impact at a hold of nought, every hop the short landing, `anim({ play })` by a file's name) were driven again there, in numbers and thirteen fresh screenshots (`fix-human-*.png`; the first thirteen are from before it): a Jedi's jump with the key down 33 to 117 ms came down at 7.8 to 9.3 m/s into the short landing every time and from 133 ms was a Force jump into Jedi Academy's own; his drops of 1.2 and 1.6 m landed short, 2.6 and 6 m in the longer one, 14 m in the hard one (27 health), and a Bounty Hunter's 1.2 and 1.6 m short and 2.6 m (7 health), 6 m and 14 m hard; W pressed a tenth of a second into the longer and the hard landing ended each a third of a second after touchdown in the run's own clip; thrown with `impact.lock` at nought the impact played alone with no key held; with every slot, the grapple, an emote, the jump and W held through the hold (1.18 s) the body rose and moved nought; killed in the air and respawned, a Bounty Hunter stood at the spawn and ran at once (as did a Jedi with the switch off, who by 87280ee's code lay prone there: read, not driven), and killed in the impact she came round with the keys free and still prone, which is the game's own rule for a posture; a dive and a walk out played a moment of Jedi Academy's landing on the shore and no imported one; the Bounty Hunter aiming through a hop stood at most 26 degrees off the view (58 before the mend) and through a 4 m drop 26 (42); and with the switch off the pack was asked for nothing and `anim({ play })` said the switch. The programs built in play in those sessions were the world's own (furniture and people streaming in after console teleports, a speeder spawned from the console), none the player's. Not built: the long fall plays only when thrown from a wreck, since nothing in the game yet pushes a player off their feet (the brief's "falling from being pushed" waits for something that does); the pack's walks, runs, strafes, stairs, crouches, climbs and hangs are imported, measured and playable from the console and nothing blends them into the movement (later waves); other players see the tread, the flying loop and the falling loop only through the state already sent, and none of the one-offs; creatures and droids are not fitted. The owner's to judge, all by eye: the pelvis and the stomach on a real body, each clip's look on each species, whether the short landing (which starts with the feet 0.43 m up and brings them down in a quarter of a second) and the longer one (0.13 m up at its trim) read right against a body already on the ground, where the line between the short landing and the longer one belongs (`land.soft`, started at 9.8, just over the fastest a jump that is no Force jump comes down, so that every hop is the short one; at the first cut's 7 and the review round's 8 a Force user's tap of more than a few hundredths of a second landed in the longer one), whether a landing should be heard as a footstep at all, how long the impact should hold the keys, and whether the flying loop is the right float for space. **Wave 2a gave the walk on foot its paces** (on the branch, not yet on main; the design is `A:\swg3js\anim\DESIGN-W2A.md`). Built: standing on foot the legs play the pack's own walks, jogs, runs and strafes by a table that is the brief's Locomotion list (twenty clips: eight ways by walk, jog and run, the sprint forward, the tired jog and the slow strafe), and the body moves at the class's own speed: the jog with nothing held (2.85 m/s forward), the run with Shift held (4.9), the sprint with Shift let go and pressed again within 0.35 s (6.35, the one run the game had; forward only), the walk with Alt held (1.44; sideways it is the slow strafe, 0.59), a class lower every half second after Shift is let go; backing up and strafing keep the body facing the view, the diagonals' jogs have clips of their own and a walked or run diagonal turns the body 45 degrees with the torso taking half back, except with a blaster up, where a diagonal plays its own jog clip at every pace and the body stays square (`gunSquare`, a rule of ours over the brief's, on); stamina is a third bar (100 points: the sprint spends 12 a second, the run holds, the forward jog gives 4 back, walking, standing and backing up 10, sideways from the jog up nothing and at the walk 10 (`stamina.sideWalk`), the air and the water giving none back while a sprint through a jump still spends, and a seat, a ride, a flight and noclip giving it back as standing does; the walk sideways giving back is the owner's answer of 2026-11-15), and emptied it tires the body to a slower jog (2.38) with no run until three tenths are back; sprinting, a fight's key (the attack, the other attack, the other trigger, the saber's throw, the block) ends the sprint on its press and goes through, the body dropping to the run until a fresh double tap of Shift (`sprintFight` 'ends', the owner's word of 2026-11-15; 'muted', the first rule, left them doing nothing while it lasted and a block capping the pace only while held), and getting on anything ends the sprint; a block or a swing holds the pace to the run on Jedi Academy's own legs (its saber walk up to the jog, its saber run from the run up, its back-pedal backing), a blaster aimed to the jog; two striding clips fade in step from the left foot's landing; every fitted clip is anchored where the game's own idle holds the hips; and other players see the same clip, worked out by their game from the state, heading and speed already sent (no word is new and the relay is as it was). Shift is the `speedUp` action now (run and sprint on foot, the boost on a vehicle and a ship, fast in noclip and the editor) and the walk Alt, held, on foot. Every number is `LOCO_TUNE` (`__debug.loco({ tune })`, sliders under `loco` in the debug menu), `__debug.loco()` reports the class, the clip, the rate, the turn, the stamina, the stride and the peers, and `localStorage['swg.loco'] = '0'` or `loco({ on: false })` is the movement before it (one run at 6.35, Shift or Alt walks, the body turns the way it runs, no stamina row), held line by line against 3432677. Seen only in headless tabs of ours on Direct3D 11, in numbers and twenty screenshots (`A:\swg3js\anim\shots\w2a-*.png`), never by the owner: a human male Jedi moved at each class's speed in all eight ways with its clip at 0.93 to 0.96 of its own rate (the run 0.85, the sprint 1.09), faced the view backing and strafing and stood 45 degrees off it on a walked or run diagonal; Shift held ran, let go 0.2 s and pressed sprinted, dropped to the run at 0.5 s and the jog at 1.0 s; the sprint spent 30 points in 2.5 s, emptied it jogged tired at 2.38 and ran again from 30; on flat ground the planted ankle slid 6 to 11 per cent of the ground speed against the travel, 2 to 5 cm a plant, in every way and class but the forward jog (13 per cent the other way, its path 34 cm a plant by the looser measure) and more wherever a slope held the body under its commanded speed (the clip's rate follows the speed commanded, as the old run's did); a human Bounty Hunter kept the body square with a blaster up in every way and class and took the slow strafe aimed; with the switch off the run was 6.35 on the game's own clip, Shift walked at 1.55 and the body turned the way it ran; and on a relay of this build two tabs saw the same legs' clip on both screens in 26 of 26 Jedi cases and 8 of 8 blaster ones, after two faults found there were mended (a body turned by the aim on an aimed diagonal, and a runner on a dune whose travel lay 14 to 20 degrees off its heading). Its review round (33 findings) built the owner's two answers, ended a sprint and its mutes in every branch off the feet, put the stop, the tap window, the legs under a block, the switch flipped on the move, the stamina row's place and the peers' states right, made the frame allocate nothing, eased the rig's steady hold and held the first-person view this side of a wall; in the same tabs afterwards a fighter mounted mid-sprint fired six bolts in a second and a half as its control did, a stop from the sprint took 0.32 s and 0.77 m as with the classes off, an aimed start or stop left the barrel within 7.5 degrees for a few frames where it had swung 15 to 27 and the movement before the wave 8, the first-person view pressed to a wall stood 0.12 m this side of it at every pace where it had stood up to 0.16 m through it, and two tabs on a relay of this build saw the same legs' clip in 32 of 32 Jedi cases and 30 of 30 blaster ones. **Its fix round (2026-11-15, the owner's words "let's reimport the animations to fix them")** mended the planted feet: the importer reads a clip's speed off its stances' settled middles against the ground it is carried on and the game scales a stride by the legs' own lengths (the lesson above), the owner's clips were imported again with only the index's speeds moving (every table clip 2 to 12% faster, not one `.mxa` changed) and no pace's dial moved, so the clips' rates moved instead; `locoSlip.test.ts` plays the clip the game fits between its keys and holds each planted foot within 2% of the ground across its stances' middles on every table clip and three figure sizes (1.85% at worst, the run's right foot; the two feet together 1.0%; the Ithorians' longer thigh 1.5% together and 2.5% on the run's right foot), and in a headless tab of ours, on the fix round's first cut (whose speeds its review then moved by -1 to +1.2%), the planted ankle's slip along the travel went from 8.3, 4.6, 14.6, 10.0, 5.2 and 9.8% back (the walk, the slow strafe, the jog, the run, the sprint, the backward jog, on a human) to 2.4% forward, 0.2 back, 0.1 back, 1.4, 3.6 and 1.0% forward, and on a Bothan woman at 0.72 of the size from 8.2, 4.1, 14.3, 10.5, 1.5 and 10.0% back to 2.5, 1.3, 0.2, 1.9, 2.4 and 1.1% forward (that measure is the ankle alone, where the node test's is the whole foot). A fight's key now ends a sprint (above), seen there with a lit saber, whose swing started on the press's own frame, and a blaster, which fired on it; and walking sideways gives stamina back, the owner's answer. Any of the five keys ends a sprint whatever is in the hands, one that does nothing with it included (the other trigger with a saber, the throw with a gun, a click with an instrument), which is the brief's list read to the letter and the owner's to narrow. Not built (wave 2b and after): the crouched and prone sets, stairs, idle fidgets, turns in place, starts and stops, the creator's gait, a peer's torso and head turned back and a peer's own aimed pose (with a gun their upper body keeps the standing pose of the carry their state names); under a block or a swing at the jog the legs are Jedi Academy's saber walk at 1.43 of its own rate; Force Speed and the lava's pace change a peer's speed and so the class their figure shows; on the grapple's line the pace is the jog, since Shift is the reel there; a friend on a build from before the wave sees its one run and walk whichever way you go. The owner's to judge, all by eye and by hand: every pace's speed and how each clip reads at it (the forward jog's slide above all), the run against the sprint (their clips are nearly one), the Shift timings, how fast stamina spends and returns, the 45-degree turn and the torso's half, the spine's turn under an aimed blaster over these legs (69 degrees against the 34 it had), the 0.2 s fades, whether the stamina bar belongs under the other two, whether a pace's dial should now stand at its clip's own measured speed, and where `gunSquare`, `stamina.air` and `fight.walkUnder` should start. **Its polish (2026-11-15, the owner's first look; `A:\swg3js\anim\DESIGN-W2A-POLISH.md`)** built, each a switch whose 'before' is the game as it was: walking sideways plays the walked diagonal's own clip with the body square to the view (`sideWalkClip` 'diagonal'; 'forward' the forward walk turned 90 degrees, 'strafe' the slow strafe); a plain jump floats in the Force jump's float with no start until it falls past 10 m/s, then the falling loop, the rope's air is the float, and a step off a ledge 1.5 m deep or more plays the hop (`airRule`); treading at the surface the drawn figure sits 0.3 m lower (scaled by the figure) and held still under the water it treads (`swim.rule`); Jedi Academy's clips' lower back rebased onto the game's idle (`jkaSpine`, 486 clips on a rig); a gun out of combat plays the pace's whole clip for a pistol and the game's own carry walk and run for the rest (`gunCarry`); the paces start at the owner's speeds (walk 1.66, jog 4, tired 3.5, run 6, sprint 7; back 3 and 4; sideways 1.44, 4 and 6; diagonals 1.66, 4 and 6 forward, 3 and 4 back), the progression's sprint base with them; and each pace's animation speed is a share of its feet-locked rate (`rate.byClass`, 1). Seen only in headless tabs of ours on Direct3D 11, in numbers and screenshots (`A:\swg3js\anim\shots\polish-*.png`), never by the owner: a human Jedi walked sideways on the strafe walk square to the view at 0.76 of its rate, on the forward walk turned 90 at 0.86, and on the slow strafe held at the band's 1.6 where it would need 2.42; a plain jump floated near its apex (the falling loop under 'before'), and one taken 6 m up floated until it fell at 10.1 m/s, then fell; treading at the surface the figure was drawn 0.333 m lower, the shoulders at the waterline, and held still on a lake's bed it trod where 'before' swam; the paces moved at 1.66, 4, 6 and 7 m/s with their clips at 0.99, 1.15, 0.94 and 1.11 of their rates, the sprint at 0.94 under a share of 0.85; a Bounty Hunter's pistol under 'full' at the sprint came no nearer the spine's axis than 0.17 m over 90 frames (its standing carry held over the legs, 'split', 0.18), and a carbine's 'original' played the rifle's own run at 0.66 of its rate at the jog and 1.16 at the sprint; no program was built in play for the player. Not built: a two-tab check of what a peer shows (the maker's ports exclude a relay), the rope's float and the ledge hop in a browser (node holds both through the game's own movement), and a pelvis taken per frame under a swing over the imported legs (wave 2b's `swingPelvis` takes the swing's own pelvis over whatever legs, below, measured over Jedi Academy's). The owner's to judge: every one of them by eye, where `fall.floatUntil` and `fall.hopDrop` should stand, how far the treading figure should sink, whether the rebased lower back reads right in a swing, and how much the 'original' carries' feet may slide (43% of the speed on a human's rifle run).

**Animations wave 2b, items A to D** (on `claude/animations`, built on 7424bad; the design is `A:\swg3js\anim\DESIGN-W2B.md`; items E and F, the slope limit and the swing's lean-back, are another maker's). Built: **crouched** (Ctrl held) has three paces by the keys standing uses and no sprint -- Alt the sneak walk (0.81 m/s), nothing the crouched walk (1.63, what crouching moved at before), Shift the game's own crouched walk (2.2) -- every way but back the pace's forward clip with the body turned toward the travel (45 degrees on a diagonal, 90 sideways) and the torso and head back on the view, backing up Jedi Academy's crouched walk back with the body to the view (0.61, 1.22, 1.65); **prone** (Z) the game's crawl (0.66), the low crawl (0.95) and the running crawl (2.15), the body turning the way it crawls as before, backing up turned round (`proneBack` 'today') or the crawl played backwards with the body to the view ('reverse'), a gun in hand keeping its own prone crawl at the pace's speed; crouched and prone the stamina comes back at the walk's rate on the slower two and the jog's on the quickest. A **gait** (standard, masculine, feminine, heavy, the game's own) chooses the walk's clips only, kept on the record (`gait`, none for the standard), chosen on the creator's and the in-game appearance page (the Walk row, whose pick plays the walk in place on the doll for four seconds) or `loco({ gait })`, and sent in the hello; **WAVE 2B CHANGED THE RELAY**: `server/wire.mjs` passes a hello's `gait` from a closed list, so the relay must be restarted for others to see a gait, and an older relay or build shows the standard. **Walking hurt** (under a quarter of the health) is the injured walk forward, on the forward diagonals and sideways (0.57 m/s, the body turned toward the travel), the jog and up unchanged; other players show it where their health reaches this side. Every rule is a switch whose 'before' is 7424bad's frame trace to the bit (`crouchRule`, `proneRule`, `proneBack`, `injured.rule`, the standard gait), every number a slider under `loco`. Seen only in a headless tab of ours on Direct3D 11, in numbers and screenshots (`A:\swg3js\anim\shots\w2b-*.png`), never by the owner: on a human male at full height (scale 1.11) the sneak walk held at the band's 0.6 (it would need 0.28), the crouched walk at 1.09, the game's crouched walk at 1.33, the walk back at 0.79, the low crawl at 1.43, the running crawl at 0.90 and the injured walk at 0.91 of their rates; a gait picked on the appearance page walked on the doll with no program built in play. Not built: a two-tab check of a peer's crouch, gait or hurt walk (node holds each), and nothing of the creator driven in a browser (its two lines are pinned). The owner's to judge: every speed, how far the sneak walk's feet slide at the band's bottom, whether the gaits read as their names, and 'reverse'.

**Animations wave 2b, items E and F** (on `claude/animations`, built on 7424bad beside items A to D; the design is `A:\swg3js\anim\DESIGN-W2B.md`). Built: **a true slope limit** for the player -- a face steeper than the climb limit (55 degrees, `slope.climb`) is never ground, whether terrain or a model's, building's or object's: standing on one, landing on one or walking onto one, the body slides off it at the world's gravity along the face (`slope.slide` 1, Jedi Academy's own rule; less is a rougher face), playing the falling loop (`slope.fade`), and the fall it slides into is paid for its speed into the ground; a plain jump off such a face is refused (`slopeJump` 'refused', 'allowed' the other), the rope and the water are as they were, and every face up to the limit, stairs, ramps, jumps, rolls and landings are 7424bad's frame for frame (`slopeLimit.test.ts`). **A swing over moving legs leans as it does standing** (`swingPelvis` 'mended'): a Jedi Academy swing riding the upper body stands on its own pelvis, not the legs', so over the back-pedal or a walk it no longer leans up to 33 degrees back and swings over people, and over a run it no longer leans 11 to 15 forward. Each is a switch whose 'before' is 7424bad's trace to the bit (`slopeRule`, `swingPelvis`), every number a slider (the debug menu's `loco`, "The slope"; `anim`, the swing's choice), and `loco()` reports `slope`, `anim()` `swingPelvis` and the pose's `chestLeanDeg` and `reachDeg`. Seen only in a headless tab of ours on Direct3D 11, in numbers and screenshots (`A:\swg3js\anim\shots\w2b-slope-*.png`, `w2b-swing-*.png`), never by the owner: on Tatooine near the arrival, the steepest terrain cell (75 degrees) held a body 1.25 m up its face for 1.5 s under 'before' and slid it 3.23 m to the foot under the limit; of 961 drops from 30 m over the buildings round the arrival, 9 that came to rest perched 2 to 8 m up a building's face under 'before' slid to the ground under the limit (12 had the controller's word overturned); a walk up a 40-degree slope was the same to the millimetre under both; and a human Jedi's strong swing chained from a stance, standing, ended its chest 32.3 degrees forward with its reach 74.8 down, backing up 6.6 and 56.5 under 'before' and 34.3 and 74.7 mended, strafing 10.8 and 15.9 against 14.9 and 19.3. Its review round (in node, never driven in a browser since): a body dropped on a steep ridge's peak, a model's or the terrain's, 65, 80 or 89 degrees, on its top or up to 0.25 m off it, slid off where it had perched under both rules, a lip's top still stands frame for frame as before, a slide no longer ends hanging 0.2 to 0.29 m over the floor at its foot, and the rule now casts no ray while a body stands and one each 16 m while it walks on flat ground, where it cast one every frame (`loco()`'s `slope` counts `casts`, `kept` and `edges`). Not built: the fighters' slope (their controller copy has the same fault and creeps 0.55 to 1.62 m in 2 s down a 65 to 89 degree face; their movement is the AI handoff's), the people's (dynamic bodies, not measured), a body falling straight (not sliding) into the crease where a steep face meets the floor, which can still come to rest leaning on the face up to 0.32 m over the floor (42 of 192 such drops in node, 165 under 'before'), and a side-on picture of a swing (the shots are the chase view). The owner's to judge: where the climb limit should stand, how fast a slide goes and whether a rough face should hold a little, whether jumping off a steep face should be allowed, and whether a swing over the legs now reads as it should.

**Animations wave 3: the feet, stairs and the crease** (on `claude/animations`, built on 23d7898; the design is `A:\swg3js\anim\DESIGN-W3.md`, the owner's brief's lines 19, 52 and 146). Built: **the feet on what is under them** -- standing on its feet (still, every pace, crouched, kneeling, Jedi Academy's legs, a gun's carry; never the air, the water, prone, riding, the rope, dead, flying, adrift, the boots' surface or a whole-body special move), each planted foot of the player's figure, and of every other player's within `ik.peerReach` (40 m) of the camera, stands on its ground: the leg bent by a two-bone solve, the foot pitched and rolled to its slope (`ik.pitchMost` 35, `rollMost` 20 degrees), on a step's edge the heel, the ball and the toe's tip each on their own ground with the toe bent up to `toeMost` (30) and never down over the edge, a foot over a drop drawn in onto the edge (`edgePull` 0.25 m), the pelvis lowered by what the lower foot needs (`pelvisMost` 0.4 m under the capsule's feet) and back up over `ease` (0.08 s), each foot's planting and any sudden change of its ground eased in over the same, a foot not planted carried down with the pelvis in the clip's own leg, the first-person eye held where the head would be with no drop (`ik.eye` 'steady'). **Stairs** are found by a rule from the ground's heights along the travel, never a mesh's name, and walked forward up and down by the pack's four stair clips (`stairs.ways` 'forward'), at their measured strides (on the human male the up walk's feet go 0.365 m/s, the up run's 0.749, the down walk's 0.485, the down run's 1.119, where the index said 0.387, 0.754, 0.218 and 0.493); the drawn body's height eases onto the capsule's over every step it takes, stairs or a kerb (`stairs.ease` 0.12 s, at most `lagMost` 0.5 m behind), the chase camera following it (`stairs.camera` 'smooth'). **The crease**: a fall into where a face too steep to stand on meets the floor ends on the floor (`slopeCrease` 'floor'). Each is a switch whose 'before' is 23d7898's trace to the bit (`ik.rule`, `stairs.rule`, `slopeCrease`), every number a slider under `loco`, and `__debug.loco()` reports `feet`, `stairs`, `crease` and each peer's feet and stairs (`loco({ side })` swings the chase camera round the figure). In node: on faces of 20 to 50 degrees, terrain and trimesh, up and across, every sole on the face within a millimetre (the 50-degree face faced up, past the pitch's 35, leaves the heel 48 mm over it); beside steps of 0.15 to 0.4 m both soles on their ground; on staircases of 0.15, 0.25 and 0.4 m risers walked and run by the clips and by the game's own movement at 60 and 144 frames a second every planted sole on its tread within a centimetre of where it is put (its ground, less what of a sudden change of it is still being let in, which settles within 0.3 s), but for a few foot-frames of the 0.4 m risers at 144 fps the pelvis's most leaves short; the rule held stairs up and down over three of the game's own buildings' flights (a theatre's, a guard house's, a starport's steps) and never a ramp of 5 to 50 degrees, terraces, a single kerb, a kerb's lip or bumps; 192 of 192 drops into a crease end on the floor (42 hung 0.08 to 0.32 m up under 'before'); and standing in the rest pose the feet cast four rays once and none after, walking a staircase 0.77 a frame at 60 fps, the frame making nothing but the engine's own answers. Seen only in headless tabs of ours on Direct3D 11, in numbers and twenty screenshots (`A:\swg3js\anim\shots\w3-*.png`), never by the owner: on a 29.6-degree slope on Naboo a human Jedi's soles stood from 8 cm over to 9 cm in the ground under 'before' and within 15 mm of it under the rule (the pelvis 0.054 m down), crouched from 24 cm over to 19 cm in against 9 mm; on Theed's bridge stairs (a prop, risers of 0.24 m), walked and run up and down, the stairs held for the whole flight going down and 147 of 159 frames going up, the drawn body easing up to 0.47 m behind the capsule running up and held at its 0.5 m running down, every planted sole on its ground within 6 mm, but one frame where the report's ray from over a toe tip at a riser's foot met the tread above and read it 0.24 m in, where under 'before' soles stood up to 0.38 m in the steps and 1.4 m over them; at the stairs' top platform's edge a foot over the drop was drawn 0.25 m in onto the edge; and on a relay of this build a second tab saw the first's figure on those stairs (put down through the server's props) on the stair clips with both feet planted in every sample and its pelvis lowered up to 0.33 m. Not built: the creatures', the fighters' and the people's feet (the AI pass's), stairs on the diagonals by default, and the stairs' pace: the stair clips' feet go far slower than every pace (at the walk's 1.66 m/s the up walk would need 4.55 of its rate, held at the band's 1.6), so they slide on the treads at `stairs.pace` 1, and 0.35 to 0.47 would keep them still. The owner's to judge, all by eye: every dial, whether the stairs should slow the pace, how a pelvis dropped 0.9 m on a run down a flight (the drawn body held 0.5 m over the capsule) reads, the toe's bend, the edge pull, and the steady first-person eye. Its review round mended, in node: the knee bending backward and swinging 0.23 m out of the clip's leg plane wherever a nearly straight leg's target moved forward (walking up a 20-degree hill or onto a kerb; its pole now the posed limb's own, `twoBone.ts`, and over slopes of 10 to 30 degrees and kerbs of 0.2 and 0.35 m at 30, 60 and 144 fps no knee backward and none off the clip's plane past its ankle); the feet and the drawn body popping by a riser in one frame (each foot's share now ramps in over `ik.ease`, a sudden change of a planted foot's ground is let in over it while what its clip does is followed as it stands, and the pelvis goes down with that eased need: a foot slid off a 0.25 m step moves no more a frame than that ease gives, and walking onto and off kerbs the pelvis's drop moves at most 7.7 m/s past the capsule's own at every frame rate, where at the ease's nought it moves 58 m/s at 144); a swinging foot left at the clip's height over a pelvis dropped 0.5 m, its ankle over its hip (a foot not planted now rides down with the pelvis in the clip's own leg); and an idle swaying the feet casting rays for good (each probe slot keeps four answers: in node an idle swaying them a centimetre casts 14 rays over its first sway and none after; standing with the species' own idle at Naboo's arrival, in a headless tab of ours, 40 rays over the first half minute and none over the 50 s after, which is the standing cost, not none). Two things it says that the wave did not: on flat ground the whole figure now stands 3.9 cm lower than at 23d7898, the character controller's 0.04 m hover taken out by the feet (under `ik.rule` 'before' every sole stands 0.039 m over the floor); and the two descending stair clips are not loops (the index marks them one-offs; played round, both ankles jump 0.30 to 0.50 m at every wrap), so a planted foot stays on its tread through the jump while a swinging one shows it: a cycle window or a mirrored half is a choice for a later wave.

**Animations wave 4, item A: hanging from a ledge** (on `claude/animations`, built on e2b9776; the design is `A:\swg3js\anim\DESIGN-W4.md`, the owner's brief's lines 157 to 159; item B, climbing a wall face and the hands bracing on walls, is maker B's, who reads the hang's state off `Player.climbHolds` and `Player.ledge`, spends stamina through `player.loco.stamina.step`, adds its groups to `CLIMB_TUNE` beside `CLIMB_RANGES`/`CLIMB_CHOICES` and `CLIMB_GROUPS`, and places a hand through `LimbPlace.setHand` and `place`). Built: a lip within the hands' reach is grabbed by a jump with W at it, by falling past it moving toward it (no faster than `grabFall` 12 m/s), or by crouch-walking off a top a hang deep, and hung from **braced** (the wall under the lip within the feet's reach, feet on it) or **free** (an overhang, arms straight), on the twenty-four of the owner's parkour clips the table names (`LEDGE_ROLES`; the twins set aside are measured and listed); A and D shimmy at the clip's own pace along the lip's own edge (the fix round's, the owner's additions while the wave ran: a `Rail` of samples a 0.1 m apart followed round outer and inner corners and along curves, each hand gripping the lip under it at its own height, the body hung from the two hands' middle and turned to face their faces' mean, and on a sloping lip by `lipTilt` 0.5 of its slope), stopping a hand short of where no lip goes on, a part steeper than `lipSlope` (35 degrees), or where the body's capsule would meet something; Space with A or D hops to the nearest other lip that way (`hopSide`, `hopUp` 1.5 m, their sliders to 8: straight on across a gap, a face turned up to 60 degrees, or a block's near side facing back, on another face, the body turning to it as it goes), its way checked for the capsule's room first (straight, else bent out from the faces or up over what stands in its line, else refused in words), Space with W hops braced up the same face, Space or W climbs up onto the top (its capsule carried out clear of the face, up past the lip and in over the top crouched, every step of it clear or the climb refused in words; the figure drawn on the climb's own straight line as before), the crouch key lets go, S with Space jumps off backward; hanging drains stamina (3, 5 shimmying, 12 a hop) and an empty pool lets go with no grab until it is back; a grab ends a fall with no harm; the fight, the postures, the saber's switch, the use key, the emotes and the class switch are muted while hanging (`LEDGE_MUTES`; `playEmote` refuses a climb as well), the grapple refuses in words, and a grapple haul that ends under a lip it cannot go over hangs braced there with the rope let go (`HAUL_STALL` 0.3 s against the face, or the haul's own end); the hands are placed on the lip and the braced feet on the wall by a two-bone solve, the body hung from the hands; other players draw the hang's eight rig states (`hangBraced` to `climbUpFree`, within the relay's 32 characters) lined up by the same table, their hands on a lip their own game finds under them within `ik.peerReach` (round its corners and up its slope, tilted as the hanger's own); a browser built before the wave draws a player hanging or climbing standing idle at the hang's place, in the air beside the wall (its `STATES.has(s) ? s : 'idle'`). `climb.ledges` 'before' is e2b9776's body to the bit (`wave4DayOne.test.ts` against that commit's harness and grapple out of git, and its pack's other 44 clips), every number a slider under `climb` and `__debug.climb` the helper. In node, at 30, 60 and 144 fps: the same grab lands the body at the same place at every rate, a running jump at the same lip too; a lip 3.4 m up is not grabbed, a crouch never grabs, a fall at 20 m/s past a lip goes past; a braced and a free hang, a shimmy at the clip's pace to within 8 mm of a lip's end, an inner corner, hops sideways and up, a climb onto a top at every rate, the climb refused under a low ceiling and on a steep top, a jump off landing 2.3 m back, the pool letting go and refusing a regrab; every hand wholly placed within a centimetre of the lip's line frame by frame through a grab's end, a hang, a shimmy and a hop's landing (worst 0.02 mm), every braced foot's ball 4 cm off the wall within 5 mm, no elbow or knee off the clip's own bend, a peer's figure's hands on the lip within 0.02 mm; and a steady hang's frame making nothing (standing and in the air the sense casts 0 and 1 rays a frame, a still hang 0, a shimmy 4 since the fix round, every cast the engine's own bytes). The fix round's own, in node at 30, 60 and 144 fps (`ledgeCorners.test.ts`, and a corner, a 34-degree lip and a figure 1.22 times the human male's in `ledgeHands.test.ts`): round an outer and an inner corner and along curves of 1.2 and 3 m radius with every hand on the lip under it within a centimetre (worst 0.01 mm) and the capsule never in a wall; a lip at 20 and 34 degrees shimmied up and down whole and one at 40 not, the hands within a centimetre of the lip under each and the body turned by half the slope; a crate at the knees and a flush post rising past the lip stopping the shimmy; a hop arcing over a pillar in its gap, one to a block's near side on another face, one 5 m off at a reach of 6; the climb's capsule never in the wall, nor under a roof 1.25 m over the top; a bigger figure's foot past a wall's end eased off it. Seen only in a headless tab of ours on Direct3D 11, in numbers and nine screenshots (`A:\swg3js\anim\shots\w4-*.png`), never by the owner: on Tatooine beside Bestine, on a slab of the props sunk to stand 2.36 m and a diner counter raised to stand 2.83 m, a human male Jedi caught the slab's lip braced (hands 1 and 1, the wall's feet 1) and the counter's free, shimmied 0.53 m in a second, hopped along a gap in the slab's face, and climbed up onto the top standing; the 19 programs built in play were the town's own pieces the teleports streamed in, none of the hang's. Not built: ledges for the creatures, fighters and people, a hang on anything that moves (a vehicle, a ship's rooms, a peer's ship), a peer's one-offs (their figure plays the hang's loops and the climb's clip, the idle through a hop), a hop round a corner on one lip going on (the shimmy's), a free hang's hop up (no clip), and the hop's leave and land shares (a sixth of the clip each, ours); the fix round's corners, slopes and hops have been driven in node only, never in a browser. The owner's to judge, all by eye: every dial, whether a Jedi's tap jump should grab or its Force jump carry it past, how the crossfade into a hang reads with the body hung from the hands, whether crouch-walking off a top should lower into a hang at all, how a body reads going round a corner, how much of a slope it should lean with (`lipTilt`), and whether a long hop should take longer than its clip (today it covers any distance in the clip's own time).

**Animations wave 4, item B: climbing a wall, and the hands bracing** (on `claude/animations`, built on e2b9776 beside item A; the design is `A:\swg3js\anim\DESIGN-W4.md`, the owner's brief's lines 19 and 157 to 159: "a Breath of the Wild-like wall climbing system that takes stamina", and the hands against walls, in tight spots, in crowds and in space).
